      <h1 class="fgh">BROADBAND DEVELOPMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.39">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.39 OFFICE OF BROADBAND DEVELOPMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.39.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Broadband" or "broadband service" means any service providing advanced telecommunications capability and Internet access with transmission speeds that, at a minimum, meet the Federal Communications Commission definition for broadband.</p>
          <p>(c) "Local unit of government" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116G.03">116G.03</a>, subdivision 3.</p>
          <p>(d) "Office" means the Office of Broadband Development established in subdivision 2, paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.39.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Office established; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An Office of Broadband Development is established within the Department of Employment and Economic Development and shall remain in existence until the commissioner certifies that the state has met the broadband goals established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.012">237.012</a>. The director shall be appointed by the governor and shall serve in the unclassified service. The director must be qualified by experience and training in broadband. The office may employ staff necessary to carry out the office's duties under subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(b) The purpose of the office is to encourage, foster, develop, and improve broadband within the state in order to:</p>
          <p>(1) drive job creation, promote innovation, and expand markets for Minnesota businesses;</p>
          <p>(2) serve the ongoing and growing needs of Minnesota's education systems, health care system, public safety system, industries and businesses, governmental operations, and citizens; and</p>
          <p>(3) improve accessibility for underserved communities and populations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.39.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Organization.</span></h2>
          <p>The office shall consist of a director of the Office of Broadband Development, as well as any staff necessary to carry out the office's duties under subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.39.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The office shall have the power and duty to:</p>
          <p>(1) serve as the central broadband planning body for the state of Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(2) coordinate with state, regional, local, and private entities to develop, to the maximum extent practicable, a uniform statewide broadband access and usage policy;</p>
          <p>(3) develop, recommend, and implement a statewide plan to encourage cost-effective broadband access, and to make recommendations for increased usage, particularly in rural and other underserved areas;</p>
          <p>(4) coordinate efforts, in consultation and cooperation with the commissioner of commerce, local units of government, and private entities, to meet the state's broadband goals in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.012">237.012</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) develop, coordinate, and implement the state's broadband infrastructure development program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.391">116J.391</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) provide consultation services to local units of government or other project sponsors in connection with the planning, acquisition, improvement, construction, or development of any broadband deployment project;</p>
          <p>(7) encourage public-private partnerships to increase deployment and adoption of broadband services and applications, including recommending funding options and possible incentives to encourage investment in broadband expansion;</p>
          <p>(8) monitor the broadband development efforts of other states and nations in areas such as business, education, public safety, and health;</p>
          <p>(9) consult with the commissioner of commerce to monitor broadband-related activities at the federal level, including regulatory and policy changes and the potential impact on broadband deployment and sustainability in the state;</p>
          <p>(10) serve as an information clearinghouse for federal programs providing financial assistance to institutions located in rural areas seeking to obtain access to high-speed broadband service, and use this information as an outreach tool to make institutions located in rural areas that are unserved or underserved with respect to broadband service aware of the existence of federal assistance;</p>
          <p>(11) provide logistical and administrative support for the Governor's Broadband Task Force;</p>
          <p>(12) provide an annual report, as required by subdivision 5;</p>
          <p>(13) coordinate an ongoing collaborative effort of stakeholders to evaluate and address security, vulnerability, and redundancy issues in order to ensure the reliability of broadband networks; and</p>
          <p>(14) perform any other activities consistent with the office's purpose.</p>
          <p>(b) In carrying out its duties under this subdivision, the Office of Broadband Development shall have no authority to regulate or compel action on the part of any provider of broadband service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.39.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning January 15, 2014, and each year thereafter, the Office of Broadband Development shall report to the legislative committees with jurisdiction over broadband policy and finance on the office's activities during the previous year.</p>
          <p>(b) The report shall contain, at a minimum:</p>
          <p>(1) an analysis of the current availability and use of broadband, including average broadband speeds, within the state;</p>
          <p>(2) information gathered from schools, libraries, hospitals, and public safety facilities across the state, determining the actual speed and capacity of broadband currently in use and the need, if any, for increases in speed and capacity to meet current or anticipated needs;</p>
          <p>(3) an analysis of incumbent broadband infrastructure within the state and its ability to spur economic development;</p>
          <p>(4) an analysis of the degree to which new, additional, or improved broadband infrastructure would spur economic development in the state;</p>
          <p>(5) a summary of the office's activities in coordinating broadband infrastructure development under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.391">116J.391</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) suggested policies, incentives, and legislation designed to accelerate the achievement of the goals under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.012">237.012, subdivisions 1</a> and 2;</p>
          <p>(7) any proposed legislative and policy initiatives; and</p>
          <p>(8) any other information requested by the legislative committees with jurisdiction over broadband policy and finance, or that the office deems necessary.</p>
          <p>(c) The report may be submitted electronically and is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.195">3.195</a>, subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.3.13.0">2013 c 85 art 3 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.391">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.391 COORDINATION OF BROADBAND INFRASTRUCTURE DEVELOPMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.391.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Broadband" or "broadband service" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.39">116J.39</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(c) "Broadband conduit" means a conduit, pipe, innerduct, or microduct for fiber optic or other cables that support broadband and wireless facilities for broadband service.</p>
          <p>(d) "Local unit of government" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116G.03">116G.03</a>, subdivision 3.</p>
          <p>(e) "Office" means the Office of Broadband Development established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.39">116J.39</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.391.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Broadband infrastructure development.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The office shall, in collaboration with the Department of Transportation and private entities, encourage and coordinate "dig once" efforts for the planning, relocation, installation, or improvement of broadband conduit within the right-of-way in conjunction with any current or planned construction, including, but not limited to, trunk highways and bridges. To the extent necessary, the office shall, in collaboration with the Department of Transportation, evaluate engineering and design standards, procedures and criteria for contracts or lease agreements with private entities, and pricing requirements, and provide for allocation of risk, costs, and any revenue generated.</p>
          <p>(b) The office shall, in collaboration with other state departments and agencies as the office deems necessary, develop a strategy to facilitate the timely and efficient deployment of broadband conduit or other broadband facilities on state-owned lands and buildings.</p>
          <p>(c) To the extent practicable, the office shall encourage and assist local units of government to adopt and implement policies similar to those under paragraphs (a) and (b) for construction or other improvements to county state-aid highways, municipal state-aid roads, and any other rights-of-way under the local unit of government's jurisdiction, and to other lands or buildings owned by the local unit of government.</p>
          <p>(d) Special consideration must be paid to projects under this subdivision that will likely improve access to broadband by rural or underserved communities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.391.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>As part of its annual report under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.39">116J.39, subdivision </a>5, the office shall report on activities taken under this section, including, but not limited to, the number of current and planned projects using the "dig once" approach, any gains in broadband speed or access associated with the project, and any costs or cost savings to the state, private entity, or end user of broadband services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.391.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">No right of action.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section shall be construed to create any right or benefit, substantive or procedural, enforceable at law or in equity by any party against the state of Minnesota; its departments, agencies, or entities; its officers, employees, or agents; or any other person.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.391.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.3.14.0">2013 c 85 art 3 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.394">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.394 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <p>(a) For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.394">116J.394</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.398">116J.398</a>, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
        <p>(b) "Broadband" or "broadband service" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.39">116J.39</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (b).</p>
        <p>(c) "Broadband infrastructure" means networks of deployed telecommunications equipment and technologies necessary to provide high-speed Internet access and other advanced telecommunications services for end users.</p>
        <p>(d) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
        <p>(e) "Last-mile infrastructure" means broadband infrastructure that serves as the final leg connecting the broadband service provider's network to the end-use customer's on-premises telecommunications equipment.</p>
        <p>(f) "Middle-mile infrastructure" means broadband infrastructure that links a broadband service provider's core network infrastructure to last-mile infrastructure.</p>
        <p>(g) "Political subdivision" means any county, city, town, school district, special district or other political subdivision, or public corporation.</p>
        <p>(h) "Underserved areas" means areas of Minnesota in which households or businesses lack access to wire-line broadband service at speeds of at least 100 megabits per second download and at least 20 megabits per second upload.</p>
        <p>(i) "Unserved areas" means areas of Minnesota in which households or businesses lack access to wire-line broadband service, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.39">116J.39</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.394.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.3.2.0">2014 c 312 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/1/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp2015 c 1 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.5.2.0">2016 c 189 art 5 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.395">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.395 BORDER-TO-BORDER BROADBAND DEVELOPMENT GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>A grant program is established under the Department of Employment and Economic Development to award grants to eligible applicants in order to promote the expansion of access to broadband service in unserved or underserved areas of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>Grants may be awarded under this section to fund the acquisition and installation of middle-mile and last-mile infrastructure that support broadband service scalable to speeds of at least 100 megabits per second download and 100 megabits per second upload.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligible applicants.</span></h2>
          <p>Eligible applicants for grants awarded under this section include:</p>
          <p>(1) an incorporated business or a partnership;</p>
          <p>(2) a political subdivision;</p>
          <p>(3) an Indian tribe;</p>
          <p>(4) a Minnesota nonprofit organization organized under chapter 317A;</p>
          <p>(5) a Minnesota cooperative association organized under chapter 308A, 308B, or 308C; or</p>
          <p>(6) a Minnesota limited liability corporation organized under chapter 322C, to expand broadband access.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application process.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An eligible applicant must submit an application to the commissioner on a form prescribed by the commissioner. The commissioner shall develop administrative procedures governing the application and grant award process. The commissioner shall act as fiscal agent for the grant program and shall be responsible for receiving and reviewing grant applications and awarding grants under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) At least 30 days prior to the first day applications may be submitted each fiscal year, the commissioner must publish on the department's website the specific criteria and any quantitative weighting scheme or scoring system the commissioner will use to evaluate or rank applications and award grants under subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Application contents.</span></h2>
          <p>An applicant for a grant under this section shall provide the following information on the application:</p>
          <p>(1) the location of the project;</p>
          <p>(2) the kind and amount of broadband infrastructure to be purchased for the project;</p>
          <p>(3) evidence regarding the unserved or underserved nature of the community in which the project is to be located;</p>
          <p>(4) the number of households passed that will have access to broadband service as a result of the project, or whose broadband service will be upgraded as a result of the project;</p>
          <p>(5) significant community institutions that will benefit from the proposed project;</p>
          <p>(6) evidence of community support for the project;</p>
          <p>(7) the total cost of the project;</p>
          <p>(8) sources of funding or in-kind contributions for the project that will supplement any grant award;</p>
          <p>(9) evidence that no later than six weeks before submission of the application the applicant contacted, in writing, all entities providing broadband service in the proposed project area to ask for each broadband service provider's plan to upgrade broadband service in the project area to speeds that meet or exceed the state's broadband speed goals in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.012#stat.237.012.1">237.012, subdivision 1</a>, within the time frame specified in the proposed grant activities;</p>
          <p>(10) the broadband service providers' written responses to the inquiry made under clause (9); and</p>
          <p>(11) any additional information requested by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Challenge process.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within three days of the close of the grant application process, the office shall publish on its website the proposed geographic broadband service area and the proposed broadband service speeds for each application submitted.</p>
          <p>(b) An existing broadband service provider in or proximate to the proposed project area may, within 30 days of publication of the information under paragraph (a), submit in writing to the commissioner a challenge to an application. A challenge must contain information demonstrating that:</p>
          <p>(1) the provider currently provides or has begun construction to provide broadband service to the proposed project area at speeds equal to or greater than the state speed goal contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.012#stat.237.012.1">237.012, subdivision 1</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) the provider commits to complete construction of broadband infrastructure and provide broadband service in the proposed project area at speeds equal to or greater than the state speed goal contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.012#stat.237.012.1">237.012, subdivision 1</a>, no later than 18 months after the date grant awards are made under this section for the grant cycle under which the application was submitted.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner must evaluate the information submitted in a provider's challenge under this section, and is prohibited from funding a project if the commissioner determines that the provider's commitment to provide broadband service that meets the requirements of paragraph (b) in the proposed project area is credible.</p>
          <p>(d) If the commissioner denies funding to an applicant as a result of a broadband service provider's challenge made under this section, and the broadband service provider does not fulfill the provider's commitment to provide broadband service in the project area, the commissioner is prohibited from denying funding to an applicant as a result of a challenge by the same broadband service provider for the following two grant cycles, unless the commissioner determines that the broadband service provider's failure to fulfill the provider's commitment was the result of factors beyond the broadband service provider's control.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Awarding grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In evaluating applications and awarding grants, the commissioner shall give priority to applications that are constructed in areas identified by the director of the Office of Broadband Development as unserved.</p>
          <p>(b) In evaluating applications and awarding grants, the commissioner may give priority to applications that:</p>
          <p>(1) are constructed in areas identified by the director of the Office of Broadband Development as underserved;</p>
          <p>(2) offer new or substantially upgraded broadband service to important community institutions including, but not limited to, libraries, educational institutions, public safety facilities, and healthcare facilities;</p>
          <p>(3) facilitate the use of telehealth and electronic health records;</p>
          <p>(4) serve economically distressed areas of the state, as measured by indices of unemployment, poverty, or population loss that are significantly greater than the statewide average;</p>
          <p>(5) provide technical support and train residents, businesses, and institutions in the community served by the project to utilize broadband service;</p>
          <p>(6) include a component to actively promote the adoption of the newly available broadband services in the community;</p>
          <p>(7) provide evidence of strong support for the project from citizens, government, businesses, and institutions in the community;</p>
          <p>(8) provide access to broadband service to a greater number of unserved or underserved households and businesses;</p>
          <p>(9) leverage greater amounts of funding for the project from other private and public sources; or</p>
          <p>(10) commit to implementation of workforce best practices, meaning all laborers and mechanics performing construction, installation, remodeling, or repairs on the project sites for which the grant is provided:</p>
          <p>(i) are paid the prevailing wage rate as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.42#stat.177.42.6">177.42, subdivision 6</a>, and the applicant and all of its construction contractors and subcontractors agree that the payment of prevailing wage to such laborers and mechanics is subject to the requirements and enforcement provisions under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.27">177.27</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.30">177.30</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.32">177.32</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.41">177.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.435">177.435</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.45">177.45</a>, which the commissioner of labor and industry shall have the authority to enforce; or</p>
          <p>(ii) receive from the employer:</p>
          <p>(A) at least 40 hours of hands-on skills training annually;</p>
          <p>(B) employer-paid family health insurance coverage; and</p>
          <p>(C) employer-paid retirement benefit payments equal to no less than 15 percent of the employee's total taxable wages.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall endeavor to award grants under this section to qualified applicants in all regions of the state.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner shall endeavor to award no less than 50 percent of grant awards from general fund appropriations for the border-to-border broadband grant program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.396">116J.396</a> for applicants that agree to implement the workforce best practices in this section. The applicant's agreement to implement the workforce best practices described in paragraph (b) must be an express condition of providing the grant in the grant agreement.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No grant awarded under this section may fund more than 50 percent of the total cost of a project.</p>
          <p>(b) Grants awarded to a single project under this section must not exceed $10,000,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Application evaluation report.</span></h2>
          <p>By June 30 of each year, the Office of Broadband Development shall publish on the Department of Employment and Economic Development's website and provide to the chairs and ranking minority members of the senate and house of representatives committees with primary jurisdiction over broadband a list of all applications for grants under this section received during the previous year and, for each application:</p>
          <p>(1) the results of any quantitative weighting scheme or scoring system the commissioner used to award grants or rank the applications;</p>
          <p>(2) the grant amount requested; and</p>
          <p>(3) the grant amount awarded, if any.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Workforce plan data.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Grantees that serve more than 10,000 broadband customers and are receiving funding for projects under this section are required to provide in annual reports information on the workforce performing installation work funded through the grant, including:</p>
          <p>(1) the number of installation labor hours performed by workforce directly employed by the grantee or the Internet service provider;</p>
          <p>(2) the number of installation labor hours performed by contractors and subcontractors on grant-funded projects with subtotals for hours worked by Minnesota residents, people of color, Indigenous people, women, and people with disabilities;</p>
          <p>(3) the name, business address, and number of labor hours performed by each contractor and subcontractor that participated in construction of a grant-funded project;</p>
          <p>(4) the percentages of workforce performing installation labor whose straight-time hourly pay rate was at least $25 and who received employer-paid medical coverage and retirement benefits; and</p>
          <p>(5) any other workforce plan information as determined by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) Following an award, the workforce plan and the requirement to submit ongoing workforce reports shall be incorporated as material conditions of the contract with the department and become enforceable, certified commitments.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.395.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Failure to meet requirements or falsification of data.</span></h2>
          <p>If successful applicants fail to meet the program requirements under this section, or otherwise falsify information regarding such requirements, the commissioner shall investigate the failure and issue an appropriate action, up to and including a determination that the applicant is ineligible for future participation in broadband grant programs funded by the department.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.395.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/157/#laws.2.29.0">2014 c 157 art 2 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.3.3.0">2014 c 312 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/135/#laws.4.6.0">2016 c 135 art 4 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.5.3.0">2016 c 189 art 5 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.20.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.6.28.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 6 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.3.3.0">2023 c 43 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/96/#laws.2.1.0">2024 c 96 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.13.1.0">2024 c 127 art 13 s 1</a>-3</p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 3 by Laws 2024, chapter 96, article 2, section 1, is effective August 1, 2025. Laws 2024, chapter 96, article 2, section 13.</p>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 6 by Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 13, section 1, is effective January 1, 2026. Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 13, section 1, the effective date.</p>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>Subdivisions 9 and 10, as added by Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 13, sections 2 and 3, are effective January 1, 2026. Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 13, sections 2 and 3, the effective dates.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.3951">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.3951 BROADBAND LINE EXTENSION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.3951.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program established.</span></h2>
          <p>A broadband line extension grant program is established in the Department of Employment and Economic Development. The purpose of the broadband line extension grant program is to award grants to eligible applicants in order to extend existing broadband infrastructure to unserved locations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.3951.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Portal.</span></h2>
          <p>No later than November 1, 2022, the department must develop and implement a portal on the department's website that allows a person to report (1) that broadband service is unavailable at the physical address of the person's residence or business, and (2) any additional information that the department deems necessary to ensure that the broadband line extension grant program functions effectively. The department must develop a form that allows the information identified in this subdivision to be submitted on paper.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.3951.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Data sharing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning no later than six months after the date that the portal is implemented and every six months thereafter, the department must send to each broadband service provider serving Minnesota customers: (1) a list of addresses submitted to the portal under subdivision 2 during the previous six months; and (2) any additional information that the department deems necessary to ensure that the broadband line extension grant program functions effectively. The department must send the information required under this section via email.</p>
          <p>(b) No later than ten days after the date that the list in paragraph (a) is provided, a broadband service provider may notify the department of any posted address at which the broadband service provider's broadband service is available. The department must provide persons residing or doing business at those addresses with contact information for:</p>
          <p>(1) the broadband service provider with broadband service available at that address; and</p>
          <p>(2) programs administered by government agencies, nonprofit organizations, or the applicable broadband service provider that reduce the cost of broadband service and for which the persons may be eligible.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.3951.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reverse auction process.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No later than ten days after the date that the notice requirement in subdivision 3, paragraph (b), expires, the department must notify each broadband service provider that the broadband service provider may participate in the reverse auction process under this subdivision. Within 60 days of the date that the notification is received, a broadband service provider may submit a bid to the department to extend the broadband service provider's existing broadband infrastructure to a location where broadband service is currently unavailable.</p>
          <p>(b) A bid submitted under this subdivision must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a proposal to extend broadband infrastructure to one or more of the addresses on the list sent by the department to the broadband service provider under subdivision 3, paragraph (a), at which broadband service is unavailable;</p>
          <p>(2) the amount of the broadband infrastructure extension's total cost that the broadband service provider proposes to pay;</p>
          <p>(3) the amount of the broadband infrastructure extension's total cost that the broadband service provider proposes that the department is responsible for paying; and</p>
          <p>(4) any additional information required by the department.</p>
          <p>(c) Financial assistance that the department provides under this section must be in the form of a grant issued to the broadband service provider. A grant issued under this section must not exceed $25,000 per line extension.</p>
          <p>(d) Within 60 days of the date that the bidding period closes, the department must review the bids submitted and select the broadband service provider bids that request the least amount of financial support from the state, provided that the department determines that the selected bids represent a cost-effective expenditure of state resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.3951.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Line extension agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>The department must enter into a line extension agreement with each winning bidder identified under subdivision 4, except that the department may not enter into a line extension agreement to serve any customer located within an area that will be served by a grant already awarded by the department under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.395">116J.395</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.3951.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Contents of agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>A line extension agreement under subdivision 5 must contain the following terms:</p>
          <p>(1) the broadband service provider agrees to extend broadband infrastructure to support broadband service scalable to speeds of at least 100 megabits per second download and 100 megabits per second upload to each address included in the broadband service provider's winning bid;</p>
          <p>(2) the department agrees to pay the state's portion of the line extension cost in a grant issued to the broadband service provider upon the completion of the broadband infrastructure extension to each address in the broadband service provider's winning bid; and</p>
          <p>(3) the winning bidder has an exclusive right to apply the grant to the cost of the broadband infrastructure extension for a period of one year after the date that the agreement is executed.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.3951.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/95/#laws.5.1.0">2022 c 95 art 5 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.3952">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.3952 LOWER POPULATION DENSITY GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.3952.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>A lower population density grant program is established in the Department of Employment and Economic Development. The purpose of the lower population density grant program is to provide broadband service to unserved and underserved areas of the state where a 50 percent match formula is not adequate to make a business case for the extension of broadband facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.3952.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>Grants awarded under this section may fund up to 75 percent of the total cost of a project and must otherwise adhere to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.395">116J.395, subdivisions 1</a> to 6 and subdivision 7, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.3952.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.3.4.0">2023 c 43 art 3 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.396">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.396 BORDER-TO-BORDER BROADBAND FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.396.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Account established.</span></h2>
          <p>The border-to-border broadband fund account is established as a separate account in the special revenue fund in the state treasury. The commissioner shall credit to the account appropriations and transfers to the account. Earnings, such as interest, dividends, and any other earnings arising from assets of the account, must be credited to the account. Funds remaining in the account at the end of a fiscal year are not canceled to the general fund, but remain in the account until expended. The commissioner shall manage the account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.396.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>Money in the account may be used only:</p>
          <p>(1) for grant awards made under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.395">116J.395</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.3952">116J.3952</a>, including costs incurred by the Department of Employment and Economic Development to administer that section;</p>
          <p>(2) to supplement revenues raised by bonds sold by local units of government for broadband infrastructure development; or</p>
          <p>(3) to contract for the collection of broadband deployment data from providers and the creation of maps showing the availability of broadband service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.396.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner for the purposes of subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.396.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Transfer.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may transfer up to $5,000,000 of a fiscal year appropriation between the border-to-border broadband program, low density population broadband program, and the broadband line extension program to meet demand. The commissioner must inform the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over broadband finance in writing when this transfer authority is used. The written notice must include how much money was transferred and why the transfer was made. The written notice must also be filed with the Legislative Reference Library in compliance with Minnesota Statutes, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.195">3.195</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.396.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.3.4.0">2014 c 312 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/95/#laws.5.2.0">2022 c 95 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.3.5.0">2023 c 43 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/126/#laws.3.1.0">2024 c 126 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.39.1.0">2024 c 127 art 39 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.397">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.397 UPDATED BROADBAND DEPLOYMENT DATA AND MAPS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Beginning in 2016 and continuing each year thereafter, the Office of Broadband Development shall contract with one or more independent organizations that have extensive experience working with Minnesota broadband providers to:</p>
        <p>(1) collect broadband deployment data from Minnesota providers, verify its accuracy through on-the-ground testing, and create state and county maps available to the public by April 15, 2017, and each April 15 thereafter, showing the availability of broadband service at various upload and download speeds throughout Minnesota;</p>
        <p>(2) analyze the deployment data collected to help inform future investments in broadband infrastructure; and</p>
        <p>(3) conduct business and residential surveys that measure broadband adoption and use in the state.</p>
        <p>(b) Data provided by a broadband provider under this section is nonpublic data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.9">13.02, subdivision 9</a>. Maps produced under this paragraph are public data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.03">13.03</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.397.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.5.7.0">2016 c 189 art 5 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.398">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.398 BROADBAND PREVAILING WAGE EXEMPTION.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.871">116J.871</a> does not apply to a project receiving a grant under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.395">116J.395</a> for the construction, installation, remodeling, and repair of last-mile infrastructure, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.394">116J.394</a>, paragraph (e).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.398.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.5.8.0">2016 c 189 art 5 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.399">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.399 BROADBAND EASEMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.399.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
          <p>(1) "broadband infrastructure" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.394">116J.394</a>, paragraph (c);</p>
          <p>(2) "broadband service" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.394">116J.394</a>, paragraph (b); and</p>
          <p>(3) "provider" means a broadband service provider, but does not include an electric cooperative association organized under chapter 308A that provides broadband service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.399.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use of existing easements for broadband services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A provider, provider's affiliate, or another entity that has entered into an agreement with a provider may use the provider, affiliate, or entity's existing or subsequently acquired easements to install broadband infrastructure and provide broadband service, which may include an agreement to lease fiber capacity.</p>
          <p>(b) Before exercising rights granted under this subdivision, a provider must provide notice to the property owner on which the easement is located, as described in subdivision 3.</p>
          <p>(c) Use of an easement to install broadband infrastructure and provide broadband service vests and runs with the land beginning six months after the first notice is provided under subdivision 3, unless a court action challenging the use of the easement has been filed before that time by the property owner as provided under subdivision 4. The provider must also file copies of the notices with the county recorder.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.399.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice to property owner.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A provider must send two written notices to impacted property owners declaring that the provider intends to use the easements to install broadband infrastructure and provide broadband service. The notices must be sent at least two months apart and must be sent by first class mail to the last known address of the owner of the property on which the easement is located or, if the property owner is an existing customer of the provider, by separate printed insertion in the property owner's monthly invoice or included as a separate page on a property owner's electronic invoice.</p>
          <p>(b) The notice must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the provider's name and mailing address;</p>
          <p>(2) a narrative describing the nature and purpose of the intended easement use;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of any trenching or other underground work expected to result from the intended use, and the anticipated time frame for the work;</p>
          <p>(4) a phone number for an employee of the provider that the property owner may contact regarding the easement; and</p>
          <p>(5) the following statement, in bold red lettering: "It is important to make any challenge by the deadline to preserve any legal rights you may have."</p>
          <p>(c) The provider must file copies of the notices with the county recorder.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.399.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Action for damages.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, this subdivision governs an action under this section and is the exclusive means to bring a claim for compensation with respect to a notice of intent to use a provider's existing easement to install broadband infrastructure and provide broadband service.</p>
          <p>(b) Within six months after the date notice is received under subdivision 3, a property owner may file an action seeking to recover damages for a provider's use of an existing easement to install broadband infrastructure and provide broadband service. Claims for damages under $15,000 may be brought in conciliation court.</p>
          <p>(c) To initiate an action under this subdivision, a property owner must serve a complaint upon the provider in the same manner as in a civil action and must file the complaint with the district court for the county in which the easement is located. The complaint must state whether the property owner:</p>
          <p>(1) challenges the provider's right to use the easement for broadband services or infrastructure as provided under subdivision 5, paragraph (a);</p>
          <p>(2) seeks damages as provided under subdivision 5, paragraph (b); or</p>
          <p>(3) seeks to proceed under both clauses (1) and (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.399.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Deposit and hearing required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a property owner files a complaint challenging a provider's right to use an easement to install broadband infrastructure and provide broadband service, after the provider answers the complaint, the district court must promptly hold a hearing on the complaint. If the district court denies the property owner's complaint, the provider may proceed to use the easement to install broadband infrastructure and provide broadband service, unless the complaint also seeks damages. If the complaint seeks damages, the provider may proceed under paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) If a property owner files a claim for damages, a provider may, after answering the complaint, deposit with the court administrator an amount equal to the provider's estimate of damages. A provider's estimate of damages must be no less than $1. After the estimated damages are deposited, the provider may use the existing easement to install broadband infrastructure and provide broadband service, conditioned on an obligation, filed with the court administrator, to pay the amount of damages determined by the court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.399.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Calculation of damages; burden of proof.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In an action under this section involving a property owner's claim for damages:</p>
          <p>(1) the property owner has the burden to prove the existence and amount of any net reduction in the fair market value of the property, considering the existence, installation, construction, maintenance, modification, operation, repair, replacement, or removal of broadband infrastructure in the easement, adjusted to reflect any increase in the property's fair market value resulting from access to broadband service;</p>
          <p>(2) a court is prohibited from awarding consequential or special damages; and</p>
          <p>(3) evidence of estimated revenue, profits, fees, income, or similar benefits accruing to the provider, the provider's affiliate, or a third party as a result of use of the easement is inadmissible.</p>
          <p>(b) Any fees or costs incurred as a result of an action under this subdivision must be paid by the party that incurred the fees or costs, except that a provider is responsible for a property owner's attorney fees if the final judgment or award of damages by the court exceeds 140 percent of the provider's damage deposit made under subdivision 5, if applicable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.399.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">No limits on existing easement.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section limits in any way a provider's existing easement rights.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.399.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Local governmental right-of-way management preserved.</span></h2>
          <p>The placement of broadband infrastructure to provide broadband service under subdivisions 2 to 7 is subject to local government permitting and right-of-way management authority under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.163">237.163</a>, and must be coordinated with the relevant local government unit in order to minimize potential future relocations. The provider must notify a local government unit prior to placing infrastructure for broadband service in an easement that is in or adjacent to the local government unit's public right-of-way.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.399.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Railroad rights-of-way crossing.</span></h2>
          <p>The placement of broadband infrastructure for use to provide broadband service under subdivisions 1 to 7 or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/308A.201">308A.201</a>, subdivision 12, in any portion of an existing easement located in a railroad right-of-way is subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.04">237.04</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.045">237.045</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.399.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/95/#laws.5.3.0">2022 c 95 art 5 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.40"><b>116J.40</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116K.01">116K.01</a>]
</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">GENERAL</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.401">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.401 DESIGNATION; DUTIES; DATA.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.401.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">State agency.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of employment and economic development is designated the "state agency" as defined by United States Code, title 29, section 49c, the Wagner-Peyser Act, as amended.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.401.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties; authorizations; limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of employment and economic development shall:</p>
          <p>(1) provide regional development commissions, the Metropolitan Council, and units of local government with information, technical assistance, training, and advice on using federal and state programs;</p>
          <p>(2) receive and administer the Small Cities Community Development Block Grant Program authorized by Congress under the Housing and Community Development Act of 1974, as amended;</p>
          <p>(3) receive and administer the section 107 technical assistance program grants authorized by Congress under the Housing and Community Development Act of 1974, as amended;</p>
          <p>(4) receive, administer, and supervise other state and federal grants and grant programs for planning, community affairs, community development purposes, employment and training services, and other state and federal programs assigned to the department by law or by the governor in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/4.07">4.07</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) receive applications for state and federal grants and grant programs for planning, community affairs, and community development purposes, and other state and federal programs assigned to the department by law or by the governor in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/4.07">4.07</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) act as the agent of, and cooperate with, the federal government in matters of mutual concern, including the administration of any federal funds granted to the state to aid in the performance of functions of the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(7) provide consistent, integrated employment and training services across the state;</p>
          <p>(8) administer the Wagner-Peyser Act, the Workforce Investment Act, and other federal employment and training programs;</p>
          <p>(9) establish the standards for all employment and training services administered under this chapter and chapters 116L, 248, 268, and 268A;</p>
          <p>(10) administer the aspects of the Minnesota family investment program, general assistance, and the Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP) that relate to employment and training services, subject to the contract under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116L.86#stat.116L.86.1">116L.86, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p>(11) obtain reports from local service units and service providers for the purpose of evaluating the performance of employment and training services;</p>
          <p>(12) as requested, certify employment and training services, and decertify services that fail to comply with performance criteria according to standards established by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(13) develop standards for the contents and structure of the local service unit plans and plans for Indian tribe employment and training services, review and comment on those plans, and approve or disapprove the plans;</p>
          <p>(14) supervise the county boards of commissioners, local service units, and any other units of government designated in federal or state law as responsible for employment and training programs;</p>
          <p>(15) establish administrative standards and payment conditions for providers of employment and training services;</p>
          <p>(16) enter into agreements with Indian tribes as necessary to provide employment and training services as appropriate funds become available;</p>
          <p>(17) cooperate with the federal government and its employment and training agencies in any reasonable manner as necessary to qualify for federal aid for employment and training services and money;</p>
          <p>(18) administer and supervise all forms of unemployment insurance provided for under federal and state laws;</p>
          <p>(19) provide current state and substate labor market information and forecasts, in cooperation with other agencies;</p>
          <p>(20) require all general employment and training programs that receive state funds to make available information about opportunities for women in nontraditional careers in the trades and technical occupations;</p>
          <p>(21) consult with the Rehabilitation Council for the Blind on matters pertaining to programs and services for the blind and visually impaired;</p>
          <p>(22) enter into agreements with other departments of the state and local units of government as necessary;</p>
          <p>(23) establish and maintain administrative units necessary to perform administrative functions common to all divisions of the department;</p>
          <p>(24) investigate, study, and undertake ways and means of promoting and encouraging the prosperous development and protection of the legitimate interest and welfare of Minnesota business, industry, and commerce, within and outside the state;</p>
          <p>(25) locate markets for manufacturers and processors and aid merchants in locating and contacting markets;</p>
          <p>(26) as necessary or useful for the proper execution of the powers and duties of the commissioner in promoting and developing Minnesota business, industry, and commerce, both within and outside the state, investigate and study conditions affecting Minnesota business, industry, and commerce; collect and disseminate information; and engage in technical studies, scientific investigations, statistical research, and educational activities;</p>
          <p>(27) plan and develop an effective business information service both for the direct assistance of business and industry of the state and for the encouragement of business and industry outside the state to use economic facilities within the state;</p>
          <p>(28) compile, collect, and develop periodically, or otherwise make available, information relating to current business conditions;</p>
          <p>(29) conduct or encourage research designed to further new and more extensive uses of the natural and other resources of the state and designed to develop new products and industrial processes;</p>
          <p>(30) study trends and developments in the industries of the state and analyze the reasons underlying the trends;</p>
          <p>(31) study costs and other factors affecting successful operation of businesses within the state;</p>
          <p>(32) make recommendations regarding circumstances promoting or hampering business and industrial development;</p>
          <p>(33) serve as a clearing house for business and industrial problems of the state;</p>
          <p>(34) advise small business enterprises regarding improved methods of accounting and bookkeeping;</p>
          <p>(35) cooperate with interstate commissions engaged in formulating and promoting the adoption of interstate compacts and agreements helpful to business, industry, and commerce;</p>
          <p>(36) cooperate with other state departments and with boards, commissions, and other state agencies in the preparation and coordination of plans and policies for the development of the state and for the use and conservation of its resources insofar as the use, conservation, and development may be appropriately directed or influenced by a state agency;</p>
          <p>(37) in connection with state, county, and municipal public works projects, assemble and coordinate information relative to the status, scope, cost, and employment possibilities and availability of materials, equipment, and labor, and recommend limitations on the public works;</p>
          <p>(38) gather current progress information with reference to public and private works projects of the state and its political subdivisions with reference to conditions of employment;</p>
          <p>(39) inquire into and report to the governor, when requested by the governor, with respect to any program of public state improvements and its financing; and request and obtain information from other state departments or agencies as may be needed for the report;</p>
          <p>(40) study changes in population and current trends and prepare plans and suggest policies for the development and conservation of the resources of the state;</p>
          <p>(41) confer and cooperate with the executive, legislative, or planning authorities of the United States, neighboring states and provinces, and the counties and municipalities of neighboring states, for the purpose of bringing about a coordination between the development of neighboring provinces, states, counties, and municipalities and the development of this state;</p>
          <p>(42) generally gather, compile, and make available statistical information relating to business, trade, commerce, industry, transportation, communication, natural resources, and other like subjects in this state, with authority to call upon other state departments for statistical data and results obtained by them and to arrange and compile that statistical information in a reasonable manner;</p>
          <p>(43) publish documents and annually convene regional meetings to inform businesses, local government units, assistance providers, and other interested persons of changes in state and federal law related to economic development;</p>
          <p>(44) annually convene conferences of providers of economic development-related financial and technical assistance for the purposes of exchanging information on economic development assistance, coordinating economic development activities, and formulating economic development strategies;</p>
          <p>(45) provide business with information on the economic benefits of energy conservation and on the availability of energy conservation assistance;</p>
          <p>(46) as part of the biennial budget process, prepare performance measures for each business loan or grant program within the jurisdiction of the commissioner. Measures include source of funds for each program, number of jobs proposed or promised at the time of application and the number of jobs created, estimated number of jobs retained, the average salary and benefits for the jobs resulting from the program, and the number of projects approved;</p>
          <p>(47) provide a continuous program of education for business people;</p>
          <p>(48) publish, disseminate, and distribute information and statistics;</p>
          <p>(49) promote and encourage the expansion and development of markets for Minnesota products;</p>
          <p>(50) promote and encourage the location and development of new businesses in the state as well as the maintenance and expansion of existing businesses and for that purpose cooperate with state and local agencies and individuals, both within and outside the state;</p>
          <p>(51) advertise and disseminate information as to natural resources, desirable locations, and other advantages for the purpose of attracting businesses to locate in this state;</p>
          <p>(52) aid the various communities in this state in attracting business to their communities;</p>
          <p>(53) advise and cooperate with municipal, county, regional, and other planning agencies and planning groups within the state for the purpose of promoting coordination between the state and localities as to plans and development in order to maintain a high level of gainful employment in private profitable production and achieve commensurate advancement in social and cultural welfare;</p>
          <p>(54) coordinate the activities of statewide and local planning agencies, correlate information secured from them and from state departments and disseminate information and suggestions to the planning agencies;</p>
          <p>(55) encourage and assist in the organization and functioning of local planning agencies where none exist; and</p>
          <p>(56) adopt measures calculated to promote public interest in and understanding of the problems of planning and, to that end, may publish and distribute copies of any plan or any report and may employ other means of publicity and education that will give full effect to the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.60">116J.60 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.63">116J.63</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) At the request of any governmental subdivision in paragraph (a), clause (53), the commissioner may provide planning assistance, which includes but is not limited to surveys, land use studies, urban renewal plans, technical services and other planning work to any city or other municipality in the state or perform similar planning work in any county or metropolitan or regional area in the state. The commissioner must not perform the planning work with respect to a metropolitan or regional area which is under the jurisdiction for planning purposes of a county, metropolitan, regional, or joint planning body, except at the request or with the consent of the respective county, metropolitan, regional, or joint planning body.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner is authorized to:</p>
          <p>(1) receive and expend money from municipal, county, regional, and other planning agencies;</p>
          <p>(2) accept and disburse grants and other aids for planning purposes from the federal government and from other public or private sources;</p>
          <p>(3) utilize money received under clause (2) for the employment of consultants and other temporary personnel to assist in the supervision or performance of planning work supported by money other than state-appropriated money;</p>
          <p>(4) enter into contracts with agencies of the federal government, units of local government or combinations thereof, and with private persons that are necessary in the performance of the planning assistance function of the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(5) assist any local government unit in filling out application forms for the federal grants-in-aid.</p>
          <p>(d) In furtherance of its planning functions, any city or town, however organized, may expend money and contract with agencies of the federal government, appropriate departments of state government, other local units of government, and with private persons.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.401.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Classification of data on individuals.</span></h2>
          <p>Data collected on individuals pursuant to a program operated by the commissioner are private data on individuals as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.12">13.02, subdivision 12</a>, unless more restrictively classified by law.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.401.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
