      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.4011">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.4011 LABOR MARKET INFORMATION DATA PRODUCTION REQUIREMENT.</h1>
        <p>(a) As part of the commissioner's obligation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.401">116J.401</a>, the commissioner must, in collaboration with the Office of Higher Education and local workforce councils, produce and publish labor market analysis describing the alignment between employer requirements and workforce qualifications.</p>
        <p>(b) The analysis must include a description of job trends that supports career choice and job seeking including:</p>
        <p>(1) measures of current job growth, projected future job growth, and current job vacancies;</p>
        <p>(2) a breakdown of these measures, whenever feasible, by industry, occupation, statewide and substate region, by educational requirement, state employee retirement trends, and by racial trends;</p>
        <p>(3) a description of industry- or occupation-based credentials and minimum educational standards necessary for successful employment in each area; and</p>
        <p>(4) a designation of areas of opportunity based on high growth, high vacancy, and high pay conditions.</p>
        <p>(c) The analysis must include a description of workforce supply and quality, including:</p>
        <p>(1) a description of the current educational attainment of the workforce and its distribution across industries, occupations, and regions;</p>
        <p>(2) the number and distribution of recent graduates of and current enrollees in postsecondary institutions by academic concentration or major and by credential type; and</p>
        <p>(3) the completion rate, employment outcome, and average debt for recent postsecondary graduates by program of study, institution type, and credential.</p>
        <p>(d) The analysis must be reviewed on a regular basis by representatives from the business and postsecondary sectors, and any feedback should be incorporated into data collection and presentation where feasible. This feedback may also include surveys of employers on their skill, credential, and other workforce requirements when necessary.</p>
        <p>(e) Analysis, data, and reports required by this section must be easily accessible, easily readable, and prominently presented on the Department of Employment and Economic Development website and websites of workforce centers. Information on job vacancies and areas of potential employment opportunities should link to educational or credential requirements, appropriate training or educational offerings, prevailing wages, and other indicators of market conditions deemed important to career choosers and job seekers.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.4011.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.3.2.0">2013 c 85 art 3 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.402"><b>116J.402</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.44.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 44</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.403">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.403 RULES.</h1>
        <p>No money made available to the commissioner for the Small Cities Community Development Block Grant Program shall be spent for community development and related planning programs until the commissioner adopts rules prescribing standards and procedures to govern the expenditure. The rules must be adopted under the Administrative Procedure Act in chapter 14 and must conform with all terms and conditions imposed on the commissioner when the money is made available.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.403.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/558/#laws.4.6.0">1984 c 558 art 4 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.2.26.0">1996 c 305 art 2 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.404"><b>116J.404</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.25.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 25</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.405"><b>116J.405</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.25.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 25</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.406"><b>116J.406</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/172/#laws.0.93.0">1993 c 172 s 93</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.407">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.407 DAIRY MODERNIZATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.407.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make funds available to eligible regional or statewide development organizations defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8731">116J.8731</a> to be used for the purposes of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.407.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>Money may be used for loans for the acquisition, construction, or improvement of buildings or facilities, or the acquisition of equipment, for dairy animal housing, confinement, animal feeding, milk production, and waste management, including the following, if related to dairy animals:</p>
          <p>(1) free-stall barns;</p>
          <p>(2) fences;</p>
          <p>(3) watering facilities;</p>
          <p>(4) feed storage and handling equipment;</p>
          <p>(5) milking parlors;</p>
          <p>(6) robotic equipment;</p>
          <p>(7) scales;</p>
          <p>(8) milk storage and cooling facilities;</p>
          <p>(9) bulk tanks;</p>
          <p>(10) manure pumping and storage facilities;</p>
          <p>(11) digesters;</p>
          <p>(12) equipment used to produce energy;</p>
          <p>(13) capital investment in pasture; and</p>
          <p>(14) on-farm processing facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.407.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application process.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of agriculture and the commissioner of employment and economic development shall establish a process by which an eligible dairy producer may make application for assistance under this section to the county in which the producer is located. The application must require the producer and county to provide information regarding the producer's existing business, the intended use of the requested funds, and other information the commissioners find necessary to evaluate the feasibility, likely success, and economic return of the project, and to ensure that money can be provided consistent with other state and federal laws.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.407.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/254/#laws.0.24.0">2004 c 254 s 24</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.41"><b>116J.41</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/289/#laws.0.119.0">1983 c 289 s 119</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.411">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.411 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.411.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Terms.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.411">116J.411</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.415">116J.415</a>, the following terms have the meaning given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.411.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.411.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Job enhancement.</span></h2>
          <p>"Job enhancement" means:</p>
          <p>(1) an increase in wages, and an increase in the responsibility or skill level of job duties; or</p>
          <p>(2) the provision of additional training or education for employees in existing jobs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.411.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.40.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.411.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Low income.</span></h2>
          <p>"Low income" means equal to or below the nonmetropolitan median household income.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.411.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Principally.</span></h2>
          <p>"Principally" means more than half.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.411.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Regional organization.</span></h2>
          <p>"Regional organization" or "organization" means an organization selected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.415#stat.116J.415.3">116J.415, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.411.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Rural.</span></h2>
          <p>"Rural" means the area of Minnesota located outside of the metropolitan area as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.1.3.0">1987 c 386 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/369/#laws.0.12.0">1996 c 369 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.6.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.412">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.412 ACCOUNT ALLOCATION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall allocate $6,000,000 from the rural rehabilitation account to be used for the challenge grant program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.412.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.1.4.0">1987 c 386 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.52.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/375/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 375 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/322/#laws.0.19.0">1991 c 322 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/232/#laws.0.6.0">1995 c 232 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/369/#laws.0.12.0">1996 c 369 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.413"><b>116J.413</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.44.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 44</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.414"><b>116J.414</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.53.0">2004 c 206 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.415">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.415 CHALLENGE GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Organization.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make challenge grants to regional organizations, for the purpose of providing financial assistance to encourage private investment, to provide jobs or job enhancement for low-income persons, and to promote economic development in the rural areas of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Funding regions.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall divide the state outside of the metropolitan area as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>, into six regions. A region's boundaries must be coterminous with the boundaries of one or more of the development regions established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.385">462.385</a>. The commissioner shall allocate all funds remaining in each regional subaccount of the rural rehabilitation account, as established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.955">116J.955</a>, to each respective regional organization. The money designated to each region must be used for assistance authorized in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Selection of organizations to receive challenge grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall select at least one organization for each region to receive the challenge grants and shall enter into grant agreements with the organizations. An organization must be a nonprofit corporation and must demonstrate that:</p>
          <p>(1) its board of directors includes citizens experienced in rural development, representatives of the regional development commissions, and representatives from all geographic areas in the region;</p>
          <p>(2) it has the technical skills to analyze projects;</p>
          <p>(3) it is familiar with other available public and private funding sources and economic development programs;</p>
          <p>(4) it can initiate and implement economic development projects; and</p>
          <p>(5) it can establish and administer a revolving loan account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Revolving fund.</span></h2>
          <p>A regional organization shall establish a commissioner certified revolving fund to promote economic development in rural Minnesota. Funds may be used to provide loans, loan guarantees, interest buy-downs, and other forms of participation with private sources of financing, provided that the financial assistance must be for a principal amount that does not exceed one-half of the cost of the project for which financing is sought.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Assistance criteria.</span></h2>
          <p>Projects supported through the challenge grant program must be used principally to benefit low-income persons by:</p>
          <p>(1) creating new jobs, job enhancement, or retaining existing jobs;</p>
          <p>(2) increasing the local tax base;</p>
          <p>(3) demonstrating that investment of public dollars induces private funds;</p>
          <p>(4) providing higher wage levels to the community or adding value to current workforce skills;</p>
          <p>(5) retaining existing business; or</p>
          <p>(6) attracting out-of-state business.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.40.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Revolving fund administration.</span></h2>
          <p>Repayment amounts must be deposited in the regional revolving fund for further distribution by the regional organization, consistent with the loan criteria specified in subdivisions 4 and 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall adopt rules to implement the duties specified in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.40.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.40.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.415.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Reporting requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>An organization that receives a challenge grant shall:</p>
          <p>(1) submit an annual report to the commissioner by August 30 for the preceding fiscal year that includes an account of loans made, written off, and fully paid during the calendar year, the source and amount of money collected and distributed by the regional revolving fund, and the funds' cash balance and loans receivable; and</p>
          <p>(2) provide for an independent annual audit to be performed in accordance with generally accepted accounting practices and auditing standards and submit a copy of each annual audit report to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.415.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.3.18.0">1987 c 384 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.1.10.0">1987 c 386 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/615/#laws.0.6.0">1988 c 615 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.53.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 53</a>,54,106; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/224/#laws.0.64.0">1995 c 224 s 64</a>-66; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/369/#laws.0.12.0">1996 c 369 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/223/#laws.2.18.0">1999 c 223 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.7.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 7</a>-12</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.417">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.417 GREATER MINNESOTA CHILD CARE FACILITY CAPITAL GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.417.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of the greater Minnesota child care facility capital grant program established in this section is to keep or enhance jobs, increase the tax base, or expand or create new economic development in the area in which the grants are made, by providing facilities for the child care necessary to support workers and their families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.417.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Creation of accounts.</span></h2>
          <p>Two greater Minnesota child care facility capital grant accounts are created. One account is created in the general fund and one in the bond proceeds fund. Money in the accounts is appropriated to the commissioner to make grants under this section. Money in the greater Minnesota child care facility capital grant accounts is available until encumbered or spent subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.642">16A.642</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.417.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligible applicant.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A city, county, or school district, or a joint powers board established by two or more cities, counties, or school districts is eligible to apply for and receive a grant from either greater Minnesota child care facility capital grant account established in this section.</p>
          <p>(b) A private child care provider licensed as a child care center or to provide in-home family child care is eligible to apply for and receive a grant from the greater Minnesota child care facility capital grant account in the general fund.</p>
          <p>(c) An applicant must be located outside of the metropolitan area as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.417.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Local government authority.</span></h2>
          <p>A city, county, or school district may own a child care facility and operate a child care facility program that meets the requirements for state licensing under Minnesota Rules, chapter 9503. A city, county, or school district may enter into a lease or management agreement with one or more licensed child care providers to operate a child care program in a facility owned by the city, county, or school district. A lease or management agreement for state bond-financed property is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.695">16A.695</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.417.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Eligible project.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A grant may be used to acquire land or an interest in land, predesign, design, renovate, construct, furnish, and equip facilities in which to provide child care or for other child care facility improvements that support the purposes for which this grant program is established. Money from the account in the general fund may also be used to upgrade or expand existing nonprofit child care facilities for purposes of meeting state requirements.</p>
          <p>(b) All projects must increase child care capacity in the community that is served by the provider and meet all state requirements for child care facilities or programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.417.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall make grants to eligible applicants to provide up to 50 percent of the capital costs of eligible child care facility capital projects. An eligible applicant receiving a grant must provide for the remainder of the costs of the project, either in cash or in kind. In-kind contributions may include the cost of project elements made before or after the grant award is made.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may also distribute money from the general fund account through a regional organization within the meaning of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.75">15.75</a> to provide grants to eligible applicants based on the manner of application and criteria established by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) If the commissioner awards a grant for less than 50 percent of the project cost, the commissioner must provide the applicant and the chairs and ranking minority members of the senate and house of representatives committees with jurisdiction over economic development finance a written explanation for awarding less than 50 percent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.417.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Application; criteria.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must develop forms and procedures for soliciting and reviewing applications for grants under this section. An applicant shall apply for a grant in the manner and at the times the commissioner shall determine. At a minimum, an application must include:</p>
          <p>(1) evidence of the need for improved, expanded, or new child care facilities in the area;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the new or expanded facility or other improvements to be made;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of the specific state requirements making improvements necessary, if applicable;</p>
          <p>(4) estimated costs of the capital project and the sources of funding to complete it;</p>
          <p>(5) estimated costs of the expanded services and the sources of funding to provide them;</p>
          <p>(6) the applicant's analysis of the expected economic benefits to the area in which the project would be located;</p>
          <p>(7) the feasibility study that shows the financial and operational sustainability of the project funded;</p>
          <p>(8) the average number of children provided care by the applicant during the year prior to the application, if any, and the expected number of children that could be provided child care after the proposed project is completed; and</p>
          <p>(9) other information that the commissioner determines is necessary or useful in evaluating the impact of the proposed project on the local economy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.417.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Maximum grant amount.</span></h2>
          <p>Grants must not be awarded for more than $500,000 per project or more than $2,000,000 in two years to an applicant for one or more projects in the same city or county.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.417.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Cancellation of grant; return of money.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that a grantee is unable to proceed with an approved project or has not expended or obligated the grant money within five years of entering into the grant agreement with the commissioner, the commissioner shall cancel the grant and the money is available for the commissioner to make other grants under this section. Money made available to the commissioner from a canceled grant is subject to cancellation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.642">16A.642</a> as if it had been appropriated to the program in the year in which the grant is canceled.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.417.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.4.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/72/#laws.3.1.0">2023 c 72 art 3 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.418">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.418 OFFICE OF CHILD CARE COMMUNITY PARTNERSHIPS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.418.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Child care" means the care of children while parents or guardians are at work or absent for another reason.</p>
          <p>(c) "Local unit of government" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116G.03">116G.03, subdivision </a>3.</p>
          <p>(d) "Office" means the Office of Child Care Community Partnerships established in subdivision 2, paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.418.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Office established; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An Office of Child Care Community Partnerships is established within the Department of Employment and Economic Development. The department may employ a director and staff necessary to carry out the office's duties under subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(b) The purpose of the office is to support child care businesses within the state in order to:</p>
          <p>(1) increase the quantity of quality child care available; and</p>
          <p>(2) improve accessibility to child care for underserved communities and populations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.418.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Organization.</span></h2>
          <p>The office shall consist of a director of the Office of Child Care Community Partnerships, as well as any staff necessary to carry out the office's duties under subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.418.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The office shall have the power and duty to:</p>
          <p>(1) coordinate with state, regional, local, and private entities to promote investment in increasing the quantity of quality child care in Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(2) coordinate with other agencies including but not limited to Minnesota Management and Budget, the Department of Human Services, and the Department of Education to develop, recommend, and implement solutions to increase the quantity of quality child care openings;</p>
          <p>(3) administer the child care economic development grant program and other appropriations to the department for this purpose;</p>
          <p>(4) monitor the child care business development efforts of other states and countries;</p>
          <p>(5) provide support to the governor's Children's Cabinet;</p>
          <p>(6) provide an annual report, as required by subdivision 5; and</p>
          <p>(7) perform any other activities consistent with the office's purpose.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.418.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning January 15, 2024, and each year thereafter, the Office of Child Care Community Partnerships shall report to the legislative committees with jurisdiction over child care policy and finance on the office's activities during the previous year.</p>
          <p>(b) The report shall contain, at a minimum:</p>
          <p>(1) an analysis of the current access to child care within the state;</p>
          <p>(2) an analysis of the current shortage of child care workers within the state;</p>
          <p>(3) a summary of the office's activities;</p>
          <p>(4) any proposed legislative and policy initiatives; and</p>
          <p>(5) any other information requested by the legislative committees with jurisdiction over child care, or that the office deems necessary.</p>
          <p>(c) The report may be submitted electronically and is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.195">3.195, subdivision </a>1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.418.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.15.1.0">2023 c 53 art 15 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
