      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.421">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.421 RURAL POLICY AND DEVELOPMENT CENTER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.421.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Established.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Rural Policy and Development Center is established at Mankato State University. The center may be established by the board as a nonprofit corporation under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code or the board may organize and operate the center in a manner and form that the board determines best allows the center to carry out its duties.</p>
          <p>(b) If the board organizes the center as a nonprofit corporation under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code, staff of the center may participate in state insurance plans that apply to state employees generally, and are subject to regulation by the state Campaign Finance and Public Disclosure Board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.421.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Governance.</span></h2>
          <p>The center is governed by a board of directors appointed to six-year terms by the governor comprised of:</p>
          <p>(1) a representative from each of the two largest statewide general farm organizations;</p>
          <p>(2) a representative from a regional initiative organization selected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.415#stat.116J.415.3">116J.415, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) the president of Mankato State University;</p>
          <p>(4) a representative from the general public residing in a town of less than 5,000 located outside of the metropolitan area;</p>
          <p>(5) a member of the house of representatives appointed by the speaker of the house and a member of the senate appointed by the subcommittee on committees of the senate committee on rules and administration appointed for two-year terms;</p>
          <p>(6) three representatives from business, including one representing rural manufacturing and one rural retail and service business;</p>
          <p>(7) three representatives from private foundations with a demonstrated commitment to rural issues;</p>
          <p>(8) one representative from a rural county government; and</p>
          <p>(9) one representative from a rural regional government.</p>
          <p>The board shall appoint one additional member to the board of directors who shall represent the general public.</p>
          <p>If the board concludes at any time that the composition of the board does not adequately reflect the ethnic and gender diversity of rural Minnesota, the board may appoint up to four additional members in order to better reflect this diversity. Members appointed by the board under this paragraph shall serve six-year terms. The board may not appoint additional members such that the board would have a total of more than 20 members.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.421.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The center shall:</p>
          <p>(1) research and identify present and emerging social and economic issues for rural Minnesota, including health care, transportation, crime, housing, and job training;</p>
          <p>(2) forge alliances and partnerships with rural communities to find practical solutions to economic and social problems;</p>
          <p>(3) provide a resource center for rural communities on issues of importance to them;</p>
          <p>(4) encourage collaboration across higher education institutions to provide interdisciplinary team approaches to problem solving with rural communities;</p>
          <p>(5) involve students in center projects; and</p>
          <p>(6) submit to the legislature a report on the "State of Rural Minnesota" no later than March 1 in each odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.421.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Statewide focus.</span></h2>
          <p>The center has a statewide mission. It may contract and collaborate with higher education and other institutions located throughout the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.421.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Powers.</span></h2>
          <p>The board has the power to do all things reasonable and necessary to carry out the duties of the center including, without limitation, the power to:</p>
          <p>(1) enter into contracts for goods or services with individuals and private and public entities;</p>
          <p>(2) sue and be sued;</p>
          <p>(3) acquire, hold, lease, and transfer any interest in real and personal property;</p>
          <p>(4) accept appropriations, gifts, grants, and bequests;</p>
          <p>(5) hire employees; and</p>
          <p>(6) delegate any of its powers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.421.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Use of appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>State appropriations to the board, whether from the general fund or the rural policy and development fund, may, at the discretion of the board, be expended for administration of the center and to carry out its duties under this section or under other law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.421.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Board compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>Compensation and expense reimbursement of board members is as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575#stat.15.0575.3">15.0575, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.421.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/200/#laws.1.51.0">1997 c 200 art 1 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/1/1/#laws.3.18.0">1Sp1998 c 1 art 3 s 18</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/223/#laws.2.19.0">1999 c 223 art 2 s 19</a>-22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/488/#laws.2.5.0">2000 c 488 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/86/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 86 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.4.5.0">2006 c 281 art 4 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.422"><b>116J.422</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/271/#laws.1.3.0">2014 c 271 art 1 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.4221">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.4221 RURAL POLICY AND DEVELOPMENT CENTER FUND.</h1>
        <p>(a) A rural policy and development center fund is established as an account in the special revenue fund in the state treasury. The commissioner of management and budget shall credit to the account the amounts authorized under this section and appropriations and transfers to the account. The State Board of Investment shall ensure that account money is invested under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/11A.24">11A.24</a>. All money earned by the account must be credited to the account. The principal of the account and any unexpended earnings must be invested and reinvested by the State Board of Investment.</p>
        <p>(b) Gifts and donations, including land or interests in land, may be made to the account. Noncash gifts and donations must be disposed of for cash as soon as the board prudently can maximize the value of the gift or donation. Gifts and donations of marketable securities may be held or be disposed of for cash at the option of the board. The cash receipts of gifts and donations of cash or capital assets and marketable securities disposed of for cash must be credited immediately to the principal of the account. The value of marketable securities at the time the gift or donation is made must be credited to the principal of the account and any earnings from the marketable securities are earnings of the account. The earnings in the account are annually appropriated to the board of the Center for Rural Policy and Development to carry out the duties of the center.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.4221.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.6.1.0">2017 c 94 art 6 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.423">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.423 MINNESOTA 21ST CENTURY FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.423.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Created.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota 21st century fund is created as a separate account in the treasury. Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner of employment and economic development for the purposes of this section. All money earned by the account, loan repayments of principal and interest, and earnings on investments must be credited to the account. For the purpose of this section, "fund" means the Minnesota 21st century fund. The commissioner shall operate the account as a revolving account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.423.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use of fund.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall use money in the fund to make loans, including forgivable loans, equity investments, or grants for infrastructure in mineral, steel, or any other industry processing, production, manufacturing, or technology project that would enhance the economic diversification and that is located within the taconite assistance area as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1341">273.1341</a>. The commissioner must, prior to making any loans or equity investments and after consultation with industry and public officials, develop a strategy for making loans, equity investments, or grants for infrastructure that assists the taconite assistance area in retaining and enhancing its economic competitiveness. Money in the fund may also be used to pay for the costs of carrying out the commissioner's due diligence duties under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.423.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Grants authorized.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Notwithstanding subdivision 2, the commissioner may use money in the fund to make grants to a municipality or county, or to a county regional rail authority as appropriate, for public infrastructure needed to support an eligible project under this section. Grant money may be used by the municipality, county, or regional rail authority to acquire right-of-way and mitigate loss of wetlands and runoff of stormwater; to predesign, design, construct, and equip roads and rail lines; and, in cooperation with municipal utilities, to predesign, design, construct, and equip natural gas pipelines, electric infrastructure, water supply systems, and wastewater collection and treatment systems. Grants made under this subdivision are available until expended.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.423.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Requirements prior to committing funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, prior to making a commitment for a loan or equity investment must, at a minimum, conduct due diligence research regarding the proposed loan or equity investment, including contracting with professionals as needed to assist in the due diligence.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.423.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Requirements for fund disbursements.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may make conditional commitments for loans or equity investments but disbursements of funds pursuant to a commitment may not be made until commitments for the remainder of a project's funding are made that are satisfactory to the commissioner and disbursements made from the other commitments sufficient to protect the interests of the state in its loan or investment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.423.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Company contribution.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may provide loans or equity investments that match, in a proportion determined by the commissioner, an investment made by the owner of a facility.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.423.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/223/#laws.2.23.0">1999 c 223 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/179/#laws.0.38.0">2008 c 179 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.3.5.0">2014 c 312 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.7.14.0">2016 c 189 art 7 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.7.9.0">2017 c 94 art 7 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.4231">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.4231 OFFICE OF NEW AMERICANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.4231.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Office established; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Office of New Americans is established within the Department of Employment and Economic Development. The governor must appoint an assistant commissioner who serves in the unclassified service. The assistant commissioner must hire a program manager, an office assistant, and any staff necessary to carry out the office's duties under subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(b) The purpose of the office is to foster immigrant and refugee inclusion through an intentional process to improve economic mobility, enhance civic participation, and improve receiving communities' openness to immigrants and refugees by incorporating the needs and aspirations of immigrants and refugees, their families, and their communities for the benefit of all by fulfilling the duties outlined in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.4231.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The Office of New Americans has the following duties:</p>
          <p>(1) create and implement a statewide strategy and programming to foster and promote immigrant and refugee inclusion in Minnesota so as to improve economic mobility, enhance civic participation, and improve receiving communities' openness to immigrants and refugees;</p>
          <p>(2) address the state's workforce needs by connecting employers and job seekers within the immigrant and refugee community;</p>
          <p>(3) identify and support implementation of programs and strategies to reduce employment barriers for immigrants and refugees, including the creation of alternative employment pathways;</p>
          <p>(4) support programs and activities designed to ensure equitable access to the workforce for immigrants and refugees, including those who are disabled;</p>
          <p>(5) support equitable opportunities for immigrants and refugees to access state government services and grants, including collaborating with Minnesota's ethnic councils as created by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0145">15.0145</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) work with state agencies, Minnesota's ethnic councils, and community and foundation partners to undertake studies and research and analyze economic and demographic trends to better understand and serve the state's immigrant and refugee communities;</p>
          <p>(7) coordinate and establish best practices for language access initiatives to all state agencies after soliciting input from Minnesota's ethnic councils;</p>
          <p>(8) convene stakeholders to further the objectives identified in subdivision 1;</p>
          <p>(9) make policy recommendations to the governor on issues impacting immigrants and refugees after soliciting input from Minnesota's ethnic councils;</p>
          <p>(10) engage all stakeholders to further the objectives identified in subdivision 1 within the context of workforce access and workforce readiness, including in the areas of employment, housing, legal services, health care, and education and communicate the importance of immigrant and refugee inclusion in the success of immigrants, refugees, their children, and the communities in which they settle;</p>
          <p>(11) engage with and support existing municipal and county offices that promote and foster immigrant and refugee inclusion and encourage the development of new municipal and county offices dedicated to immigrant and refugee inclusion;</p>
          <p>(12) serve as the point of contact for immigrants and refugees accessing resources both within the department and with boards charged with oversight of a profession;</p>
          <p>(13) promulgate rules necessary to implement and effectuate this section;</p>
          <p>(14) provide an annual report, as required by subdivision 3;</p>
          <p>(15) perform any other activities consistent with the office's purpose; and</p>
          <p>(16) administer any grant program or other appropriation to the office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.4231.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning January 15, 2025, and each year thereafter, the Office of New Americans shall report to the legislative committees with jurisdiction over the office's activities during the previous year.</p>
          <p>(b) The report shall contain, at a minimum:</p>
          <p>(1) a summary of the office's activities;</p>
          <p>(2) suggested policies, incentives, and legislation designed to accelerate the achievement of the duties under subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(3) any proposed legislative and policy initiatives;</p>
          <p>(4) the amount and types of grants awarded under subdivision 6; and</p>
          <p>(5) any other information deemed necessary and requested by the legislative committees with jurisdiction over the office.</p>
          <p>(c) The report may be submitted electronically and is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.195">3.195, subdivision </a>1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.4231.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Interdepartmental Coordinating Council on Immigrant and Refugee Affairs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An Interdepartmental Coordinating Council on Immigrant and Refugee Affairs is established to advise the Office of New Americans.</p>
          <p>(b) The purpose of the council is to identify and establish ways in which state departments, agencies, and Minnesota's ethnic councils can work together to deliver state programs and services effectively and efficiently to Minnesota's immigrant and refugee populations. The council shall implement policies, procedures, and programs requested by the governor through the state departments and offices.</p>
          <p>(c) The council shall be chaired by the assistant commissioner of the Office of New Americans and shall include the commissioners, department directors, or designees from the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the governor's office;</p>
          <p>(2) the Department of Administration;</p>
          <p>(3) the Department of Employment and Economic Development;</p>
          <p>(4) the Department of Human Services;</p>
          <p>(5) the Department of Human Services Refugee Resettlement Programs Office;</p>
          <p>(6) the Department of Labor and Industry;</p>
          <p>(7) the Department of Health;</p>
          <p>(8) the Department of Education;</p>
          <p>(9) the Office of Higher Education;</p>
          <p>(10) the Department of Public Safety;</p>
          <p>(11) the Department of Corrections;</p>
          <p>(12) the Council on Asian Pacific Minnesotans;</p>
          <p>(13) the Council for Minnesotans of African Heritage; and</p>
          <p>(14) the Minnesota Council on Latino Affairs.</p>
          <p>(d) Each department or office specified in paragraph (c) shall designate one staff member as an immigrant and refugee services liaison. The liaison's responsibilities shall include:</p>
          <p>(1) preparation and dissemination of information and services available to immigrants and refugees; and</p>
          <p>(2) interfacing with the Office of New Americans on issues that impact immigrants and refugees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.4231.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">No right of action.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section shall be construed to create any right or benefit, substantive or procedural, enforceable at law or in equity by any party against the state; its departments, agencies, or entities; its officers, employees, or agents; or any other person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.4231.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The Office of New Americans may apply for grants for interested state agencies, community partners, and stakeholders under this section to carry out the duties under subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.4231.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.15.2.0">2023 c 53 art 15 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.424">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.424 IRON RANGE RESOURCES AND REHABILITATION CONTRIBUTION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation, after consultation with the Iron Range Resources and Rehabilitation Board, may provide an equal match for any loan or equity investment made for a project located in the taconite assistance area defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1341">273.1341</a>, by the Minnesota 21st century fund created by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.423">116J.423</a>. The match may be in the form of a loan or equity investment, notwithstanding whether the fund makes a loan or equity investment. The state shall not acquire an equity interest because of an equity investment or loan by the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation and the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation, after consultation with the advisory board, shall have sole discretion to decide what interest the fund acquires in a project. The commissioner of employment and economic development may require a commitment from the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation to make the match prior to disbursing money from the fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.424.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/223/#laws.2.24.0">1999 c 223 art 2 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.6.1.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.7.1.0">2009 c 78 art 7 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/3/#laws.0.1.0">2013 c 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.7.15.0">2016 c 189 art 7 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.7.10.0">2017 c 94 art 7 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.43"><b>116J.43</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116K.05">116K.05</a>]
</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.431">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.431 GREATER MINNESOTA BUSINESS DEVELOPMENT PUBLIC INFRASTRUCTURE GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant program established; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall make grants to counties or cities to provide up to 50 percent of the capital costs of public infrastructure necessary for an eligible economic development project. The county or city receiving a grant must provide for the remainder of the costs of the project, either in cash or in kind. In-kind contributions may include the value of site preparation other than the public infrastructure needed for the project.</p>
          <p>(b) The purpose of the grants made under this section is to keep or enhance jobs in the area, increase the tax base, or to expand or create new economic development.</p>
          <p>(c) In awarding grants under this section, the commissioner must adhere to the criteria under subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(d) If the commissioner awards a grant for less than 50 percent of the project, the commissioner shall provide the applicant and the chairs and ranking minority members of the senate and house of representatives committees with jurisdiction over economic development finance a written explanation of the reason less than 50 percent of the capital costs were awarded in the grant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "City" means a statutory or home rule charter city located outside the metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) "County" means a county located outside the metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Public infrastructure" means publicly owned physical infrastructure necessary to support economic development projects, including, but not limited to, sewers, water supply systems, utility extensions, streets, wastewater treatment systems, stormwater management systems, and facilities for pretreatment of wastewater to remove phosphorus.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An economic development project for which a county or city may be eligible to receive a grant under this section includes:</p>
          <p>(1) manufacturing;</p>
          <p>(2) technology;</p>
          <p>(3) warehousing and distribution;</p>
          <p>(4) research and development;</p>
          <p>(5) agricultural processing, defined as transforming, packaging, sorting, or grading livestock or livestock products into goods that are used for intermediate or final consumption, including goods for nonfood use; or</p>
          <p>(6) industrial park development that would be used by any other business listed in this subdivision even if no business has committed to locate in the industrial park at the time the grant application is made.</p>
          <p>(b) Up to 15 percent of the development of a project may be for a purpose that is not included under this subdivision as an eligible project. A city or county must provide notice to the commissioner for the commissioner's approval of the proposed project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Ineligible projects.</span></h2>
          <p> Projects, including but not limited to the following types, are ineligible for a grant under this section:</p>
          <p>(1) retail development; or</p>
          <p>(2) office space development, except as incidental to an eligible purpose.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Development restrictions expiration.</span></h2>
          <p>After ten years from the date of the grant award under this section, if an eligible project for which the public infrastructure was intended has not been developed, any other lawful project may be developed and supported by the public infrastructure. The city or county must notify the commissioner of the project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must develop forms and procedures for soliciting and reviewing applications for grants under this section. At a minimum, a county or city must include in its application a resolution of the county or city council certifying that the required local match is available. The commissioner must evaluate complete applications for eligible projects using the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the project is an eligible project as defined under subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(2) the project is expected to result in or will attract substantial public and private capital investment and provide substantial economic benefit to the county or city in which the project would be located;</p>
          <p>(3) the project is not relocating substantially the same operation from another location in the state, unless the commissioner determines the project cannot be reasonably accommodated within the county or city in which the business is currently located, or the business would otherwise relocate to another state; and</p>
          <p>(4) the project is expected to or will create or retain full-time jobs.</p>
          <p>(b) The determination of whether to make a grant for a site is within the discretion of the commissioner, subject to this section. The commissioner's decisions and application of the criteria are not subject to judicial review, except for abuse of discretion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.44.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 44</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Maximum grant amount.</span></h2>
          <p>A county or city may receive no more than $2,000,000 in two years for one or more projects.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Cancellation of grant; return of grant money.</span></h2>
          <p>If after five years, the commissioner determines that a project has not proceeded in a timely manner and is unlikely to be completed, the commissioner must cancel the grant and require the grantee to return all grant money awarded for that project. For industrial park development projects, if after five years the industrial park is not developed and available for business use, the commissioner must cancel the grant and require the grantee to return all grant money for that project. If the industrial park is developed and available for use within five years, but no businesses have located in the park, the grantee is not required to return any grant money.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.431.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>Grant money returned to the commissioner is appropriated to the commissioner to make additional grants under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.431.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.46.0">2002 c 393 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.7.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 7</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.7.16.0">2016 c 189 art 7 s 16</a>-19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/10/#laws.2.2.0">1Sp2021 c 10 art 2 s 2</a>-4</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.433"><b>116J.433</b> MS 2014 [Expired, 2012 c 293 s 33]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.435">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.435 INNOVATIVE BUSINESS DEVELOPMENT PUBLIC INFRASTRUCTURE GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.435.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation of account.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An innovative business development public infrastructure account is created in the bond proceeds fund. Money in the account may only be used for capital costs of public infrastructure for eligible innovative business development projects.</p>
          <p>(b) An innovative business development public infrastructure account is created in the general fund. The account consists of funds as provided by law and any other money donated, allotted, transferred, or otherwise provided to the account. Money in the account may only be expended on a project receiving financial assistance as provided under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.435.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section:</p>
          <p>(1) "local governmental unit" means a county, city, town, special district, public higher education institution, or other political subdivision or public corporation;</p>
          <p>(2) "governing body" means the council, board of commissioners, board of trustees, board of regents, or other body charged with governing a local governmental unit;</p>
          <p>(3) "public infrastructure" means publicly owned physical infrastructure in this state, including, but not limited to, wastewater collection and treatment systems, drinking water systems, storm sewers, utility extensions, telecommunications infrastructure, streets, roads, bridges, parking ramps, facilities that support basic science technology and clinical research, and research infrastructure;</p>
          <p>(4) "innovative business" means a business that is engaged in, or is committed to engage in, innovation in Minnesota in one of the following: using proprietary technology to add value to a product, process, or service in a high technology field; researching or developing a proprietary product, process, or service in a high technology field; researching, developing, or producing a new proprietary technology for use in the fields of tourism, forestry, mining, transportation, or green manufacturing;</p>
          <p>(5) "proprietary technology" means the technical innovations that are unique and legally owned or licensed by a business and includes, without limitation, those innovations that are patented, patent pending, a subject of trade secrets, or copyrighted; and</p>
          <p>(6) "eligible project" means an innovative business development capital improvement project in this state, including: manufacturing; technology; warehousing and distribution; research and development; innovative business incubator; agricultural processing; or industrial, office, or research park development that would be used by an innovative business.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.435.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall make grants to local governmental units to acquire and prepare land on which public infrastructure required to support an eligible project will be located and to construct, furnish, and equip public infrastructure required to support an eligible project. The local governmental unit receiving a grant must provide for the remainder of the public infrastructure costs from other sources.</p>
          <p>(b) The amount of a grant may not exceed 50 percent of the sum of the cost of the public infrastructure project.</p>
          <p>(c) The purpose of the program is to keep or enhance jobs in the area, increase the tax base, or to expand or create new economic development through the growth of new innovative businesses and organizations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.435.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must develop forms and procedures for soliciting and reviewing applications for grants under this section. At a minimum, a local governmental unit must include in its application a resolution certifying that the money required to be supplied by the local governmental unit to complete the public infrastructure project is available and committed. The commissioner must evaluate complete applications for eligible projects using the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the project is an eligible project as defined under subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(2) the project is expected to result in or will attract substantial public and private capital investment and provide substantial economic benefit to the county or city in which the project would be located;</p>
          <p>(3) the project is not relocating substantially the same operation from another location in the state, unless the commissioner determines the project cannot be reasonably accommodated within the county or city in which the business is currently located, or the business would otherwise relocate to another state; and</p>
          <p>(4) the project is expected to create or retain full-time jobs.</p>
          <p>(b) The determination of whether to make a grant for a site is within the discretion of the commissioner, subject to this section. The commissioner's decisions and application of the criteria are not subject to judicial review, except for abuse of discretion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.435.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/120/#laws.6.3.0">2024 c 120 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.435.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Cancellation of grant.</span></h2>
          <p>If a grant is awarded to a local governmental unit and funds are not encumbered for the grant within four years after the award date, the grant must be canceled.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.435.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Repayment of grant.</span></h2>
          <p>If an eligible project supported by public infrastructure funded with a grant awarded under this section is not occupied by an innovative business in accordance with the grant application under subdivision 4 within five years after the date of the last grant payment, the grant recipient must repay the amount of the grant received. The commissioner must deposit all money received under this subdivision into the state treasury and credit it to the debt service account in the state bond fund if the grant was funded with state general obligation bond proceeds or to the general fund if the grant was funded with money from the general fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.435.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/258/#laws.0.34.0">2006 c 258 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/35/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 35 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.12.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/189/#laws.0.39.0">2010 c 189 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/71/#laws.2.3.0">2023 c 71 art 2 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/120/#laws.6.1.0">2024 c 120 art 6 s 1</a>,2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.436">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.436 TRANSPORTATION ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT INFRASTRUCTURE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.436.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant program established; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The transportation economic development infrastructure program is created to foster interagency coordination between the Departments of Transportation and Employment and Economic Development to finance infrastructure to create economic development opportunities, jobs, and improve all types of transportation systems statewide.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.436.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible projects.</span></h2>
          <p>Funds appropriated for the program must be used to fund predesign, design, acquisition of land, construction, reconstruction, and infrastructure improvements that will promote economic development, increase employment, and improve transportation systems to accommodate private investment and job creation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.436.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Trunk highway projects.</span></h2>
          <p>Money in the program shall not be used on trunk highway improvements, but can be used for needed infrastructure improvements and nontrunk highway improvements in coordination with trunk highway improvement projects undertaken by the Department of Transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.436.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioners of transportation and employment and economic development shall design an application process and selection process to distribute funding to local units of government for publicly owned infrastructure using criteria that take into account: job creation; increase in local tax base; level of private investment; leverage of nonstate funds; improvement to the transportation system to serve the project area; and appropriate geographic balance between the metropolitan area and greater Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.436.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/293/#laws.0.34.0">2012 c 293 s 34</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.437">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.437 COORDINATING ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT AND ENVIRONMENTAL POLICY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.437.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For the purpose of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Green economy" means products, processes, methods, technologies, or services intended to do one or more of the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) increase the use of energy from renewable sources, including through achieving the renewable energy standard established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.1691">216B.1691</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) achieve the statewide energy-savings goal established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2401">216B.2401</a>, including energy savings achieved by the conservation investment program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241">216B.241</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) achieve the greenhouse gas emission reduction goals of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216H.02">216H.02</a>, subdivision 1, including through reduction of greenhouse gas emissions, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216H.01#stat.216H.01.2">216H.01, subdivision 2</a>, or mitigation of the greenhouse gas emissions through, but not limited to, carbon capture, storage, or sequestration;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) monitor, protect, restore, and preserve the quality of surface waters, including actions to further the purposes of the Clean Water Legacy Act as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/114D.10#stat.114D.10.1">114D.10, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) expand the use of biofuels, including by expanding the feasibility or reducing the cost of producing biofuels or the types of equipment, machinery, and vehicles that can use biofuels, including activities to achieve the petroleum replacement goal in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/239.7911">239.7911</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(6) increase the use of green chemistry, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9401">116.9401</a>.</p>
          <p class="bl">For the purpose of clause (3), "green economy" includes strategies that reduce carbon emissions, such as utilizing existing buildings and other infrastructure, and utilizing mass transit or otherwise reducing commuting for employees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.437.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Coordinating economic development and environmental policy.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner and the Jobs Skills Partnership Board shall cooperate to promote job training that complements green economy business development.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.437.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/356/#laws.0.3.0">2008 c 356 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/347/#laws.1.1.0">2010 c 347 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.5.4.0">2010 c 361 art 5 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.53.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 53</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.438">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.438 MINNESOTA GREEN ENTERPRISE ASSISTANCE.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of employment and economic development, in consultation with the commissioner of commerce, shall lead a multiagency project to advise, promote, market, and coordinate state agency collaboration on green enterprise and green economy projects, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.437">116J.437</a>. The multiagency project must include the commissioners of employment and economic development, natural resources, agriculture, transportation, and commerce and the Pollution Control Agency. The project must involve collaboration with the chairs and ranking minority members of legislative committees overseeing energy policy and energy finance, state agencies, local governments, representatives from business and agriculture, and other interested stakeholders. The objective of the project is to utilize existing state resources to expedite the delivery of grants, licenses, permits, and other state authorizations and approvals for green economy projects. The commissioner shall appoint a lead person to coordinate green enterprise assistance activities.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner of employment and economic development shall seek out and may select persons from the business community to assist the commissioner in project activities.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner may accept gifts, contributions, and in-kind services for the purposes of this section, under the authority provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.035#stat.116J.035.1">116J.035, subdivision 1</a>. Any funds received must be placed in a special revenue account for the purposes of this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.438.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.2.7.0">2009 c 37 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.13.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 13</a></p>
      </div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.541"><b>116J.541</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/200/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 200 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.542"><b>116J.542</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/200/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 200 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.543"><b>116J.543</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.11.32.0">2006 c 282 art 11 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.545">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.545 GETTING TO WORK GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.545.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of employment and economic development shall make grants to nonprofit organizations to establish and operate programs under this section that provide, repair, or maintain motor vehicles to assist eligible individuals to obtain or maintain employment. All grants shall be for two years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.545.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Qualified grantee.</span></h2>
          <p>A grantee must:</p>
          <p>(1) qualify under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code; and</p>
          <p>(2) at the time of application, offer or have the demonstrated capacity to offer a motor vehicle program that provides the services required under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.545.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Program requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A program must offer one or more of the following services:</p>
          <p>(1) provision of new or used motor vehicles by gift, sale, or lease;</p>
          <p>(2) motor vehicle repair and maintenance services; or</p>
          <p>(3) motor vehicle loans.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the requirements of paragraph (a), a program must offer one or more of the following services:</p>
          <p>(1) financial literacy education;</p>
          <p>(2) education on budgeting for vehicle ownership;</p>
          <p>(3) car maintenance and repair instruction;</p>
          <p>(4) credit counseling; or</p>
          <p>(5) job training related to motor vehicle maintenance and repair.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.545.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>Applications for a grant must be on a form provided by the commissioner and on a schedule set by the commissioner. Applications must, in addition to any other information required by the commissioner, include the following:</p>
          <p>(1) a detailed description of all services to be offered;</p>
          <p>(2) the area to be served;</p>
          <p>(3) the estimated number of program participants to be served by the grant; and</p>
          <p>(4) a plan for leveraging resources from partners that may include but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(i) automobile dealers;</p>
          <p>(ii) automobile parts dealers;</p>
          <p>(iii) independent local mechanics and automobile repair facilities;</p>
          <p>(iv) banks and credit unions;</p>
          <p>(v) employers;</p>
          <p>(vi) employment and training agencies;</p>
          <p>(vii) insurance companies and agents;</p>
          <p>(viii) local workforce centers; and</p>
          <p>(ix) educational institutions, including vocational institutions and jobs or skills training programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.545.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Participant eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be eligible to receive program services, a person must:</p>
          <p>(1) have a household income at or below 200 percent of the federal poverty level;</p>
          <p>(2) be at least 18 years of age;</p>
          <p>(3) have a valid driver's license;</p>
          <p>(4) provide the grantee with proof of motor vehicle insurance; and</p>
          <p>(5) demonstrate to the grantee that a motor vehicle is required by the person to obtain or maintain employment.</p>
          <p>(b) This subdivision does not preclude a grantee from imposing additional requirements, not inconsistent with paragraph (a), for the receipt of program services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.545.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Report to legislature.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15, 2026, and each January 15 in an even-numbered year thereafter, the commissioner shall submit a report to the chairs of the house of representatives and senate committees with jurisdiction over workforce and economic development on program outcomes. At a minimum, the report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the total number of program participants;</p>
          <p>(2) the number of program participants who received each of the following:</p>
          <p>(i) provision of a motor vehicle;</p>
          <p>(ii) motor vehicle repair services; and</p>
          <p>(iii) motor vehicle loans;</p>
          <p>(3) the number of program participants who report that they or their children were able to increase their participation in community activities such as after school programs, other youth programs, church or civic groups, or library services as a result of participation in the program; and</p>
          <p>(4) an analysis of the impact of the getting to work grant program on the employment rate and wages of program participants.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.545.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.15.3.0">2023 c 53 art 15 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.548">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.548 HOST COMMUNITY ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.548.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation of account.</span></h2>
          <p>A host community economic development grant program is created in the Department of Employment and Economic Development. Grants awarded under this section may only be spent for capital costs of an eligible project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.548.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section:</p>
          <p>(a) "Capital costs" means expenditures for the public acquisition of land and buildings, betterment of public lands and buildings, and for other publicly owned capital improvements. Capital costs also include expenditures for predesign, design, engineering, and similar activities for specifically identified eligible projects.</p>
          <p>(b) "Eligible project" means a development or redevelopment project that will generate economic development within a time frame of five years or less or facilitate the preparation of long-term economic development within a host community.</p>
          <p>(c) "Economic development" means assistance in preparation of a redevelopment or development area contained in the application that results in at least one of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) job creation, including jobs relating to construction and temporary jobs;</p>
          <p>(2) an increase in the tax base;</p>
          <p>(3) the ability of the eligible project to attract private investment;</p>
          <p>(4) long-term economic development;</p>
          <p>(5) needed public infrastructure or transportation-related improvements to facilitate long-term redevelopment or development; or</p>
          <p>(6) other objective criteria established by the commissioner that demonstrate a public benefit to the host community.</p>
          <p>(d) "Host community" means a city located within the seven-county metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>, that is the site of a waste disposal facility that meets the standards in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.849">473.849</a>, that accepts unprocessed mixed municipal solid waste generated in the metropolitan area.</p>
          <p>(e) "Long-term economic development" means capital costs associated with economic development projects identified by a host community comprehensive plan or redevelopment plan that will generate eligible economic development.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.548.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>Host communities may apply for a grant under this section on a form and in a manner prescribed by the commissioner. In awarding grants under this section, the commissioner must allocate available money between host communities as evenly as practicable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.548.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">No match required.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.86">16A.86</a> or any other law to the contrary, the state share of a project covered by this section shall cover 100 percent of the total cost of the project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.548.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must report to committees of the legislature with jurisdiction over economic development by February 15 of each year on grants awarded under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.548.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.3.3.0">2013 c 85 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/94/#laws.0.1.0">2016 c 94 s 1</a>,2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.549"><b>116J.549</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.6.28.0">2017 c 94 art 6 s 28</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.5491">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.5491 ENERGY TRANSITION OFFICE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5491.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5491">116J.5491</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5493">116J.5493</a>, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Impacted community" means a municipality, Tribal government, or county in which an impacted facility is located.</p>
          <p>(c) "Impacted facility" means an electric generating unit powered by coal, nuclear energy, or natural gas that is or was owned by a public utility, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.02">216B.02</a>, subdivision 4, and that:</p>
          <p>(1) is currently operating and (i) is projected, estimated, or scheduled to cease operations, or (ii) whose cessation of operations has been proposed in an integrated resource plan filed with the Public Utilities Commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2422">216B.2422</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) ceased operations or was removed from the local property tax base no earlier than five years before July 1, 2021.</p>
          <p>(d) "Impacted worker" means a Minnesota resident:</p>
          <p>(1) employed at an impacted facility and who is facing the loss of employment as a result of the impacted facility's retirement; or</p>
          <p>(2) employed by a company that, under contract, regularly performs construction, maintenance, or repair work at an impacted facility and who is facing the loss of employment or of work opportunities as a result of the impacted facility's retirement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5491.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Office established; director.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Energy Transition Office is established in the Department of Employment and Economic Development.</p>
          <p>(b) The director of the Energy Transition Office is appointed by the commissioner of employment and economic development. The director must be qualified by experience in issues related to energy, economic development, and the environment.</p>
          <p>(c) The office may employ staff necessary to carry out the duties required in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5491.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of the office is to:</p>
          <p>(1) address economic dislocations experienced by impacted workers after an impacted facility is retired;</p>
          <p>(2) implement recommendations of the Minnesota energy transition plan developed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5493">116J.5493</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) improve communication among local, state, federal, and private entities regarding impacted facility retirement planning and implementation;</p>
          <p>(4) address local tax and fiscal issues related to the impacted facility's retirement and develop strategies to reduce the resulting economic dislocation experienced by impacted communities and impacted workers; and</p>
          <p>(5) assist the establishment and implementation of economic support programs, including but not limited to property tax revenue replacement, community energy transition programs, and economic development tools, for impacted communities and impacted workers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5491.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The office is authorized to:</p>
          <p>(1) administer programs to support impacted communities and impacted workers;</p>
          <p>(2) coordinate local, state, and federal resources to support impacted communities and impacted workers;</p>
          <p>(3) coordinate the development of statewide policies addressing impacted communities and impacted workers;</p>
          <p>(4) deliver programs and resources to impacted communities and impacted workers;</p>
          <p>(5) support impacted workers by establishing benefits and educating impacted workers on applying for benefits;</p>
          <p>(6) act as a liaison among impacted communities, impacted workers, and state agencies;</p>
          <p>(7) assist state agencies to (i) address local tax, land use, economic development, and fiscal issues related to an impacted facility's retirement, and (ii) develop strategies to support impacted communities and impacted workers;</p>
          <p>(8) review existing programs supporting impacted workers and identify gaps that need to be addressed;</p>
          <p>(9) support activities of the energy transition advisory committee members;</p>
          <p>(10) monitor transition efforts in other states and localities;</p>
          <p>(11) identify impacted facility closures and estimate job losses and the effect on impacted communities and impacted workers;</p>
          <p>(12) maintain communication with all affected parties regarding closure dates; and</p>
          <p>(13) monitor and participate in administrative proceedings that affect the office's activities, including matters before the Public Utilities Commission, the Department of Commerce, the Department of Revenue, and other entities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5491.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning January 15, 2023, and each year thereafter, the Energy Transition Office must submit a written report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over energy, economic development, and tax policy and finance on the office's activities during the previous year.</p>
          <p>(b) The report must contain:</p>
          <p>(1) a list of impacted facility closures, projected associated job losses, and the effect on impacted communities and impacted workers;</p>
          <p>(2) recommendations to support impacted communities and impacted workers;</p>
          <p>(3) information on the administration of assistance programs administered by the office; and</p>
          <p>(4) updates on implementation of the Minnesota energy transition plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5491.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Gifts; grants; donations.</span></h2>
          <p>The office may accept gifts and grants on behalf of the state that constitute donations to the state. Funds received under this subdivision are appropriated to the commissioner of employment and economic development to support the purposes of the office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5491.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Sunset.</span></h2>
          <p>This section expires five years after the date the last impacted facility in Minnesota ceases operations.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.5491.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/4/#laws.8.6.0">1Sp2021 c 4 art 8 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.5492">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.5492 ENERGY TRANSITION ADVISORY COMMITTEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The Energy Transition Advisory Committee is established to develop a statewide energy transition plan and to advise the governor, the commissioner, and the legislature on transition issues, established transition programs, economic initiatives, and transition policy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The advisory committee consists of 19 voting members and eight ex officio nonvoting members.</p>
          <p>(b) The voting members of the advisory committee are appointed by the commissioner of employment and economic development, except as specified below:</p>
          <p>(1) two members of the senate, one appointed by the majority leader of the senate and one appointed by the minority leader of the senate;</p>
          <p>(2) two members of the house of representatives, one appointed by the speaker of the house of representatives and one appointed by the minority leader of the house of representatives;</p>
          <p>(3) one representative of the Prairie Island Indian community;</p>
          <p>(4) four representatives of impacted communities, of which two must represent counties and two must represent municipalities, and, to the extent possible, of the impacted facilities in those communities, at least one must be a coal plant, at least one must be a nuclear plant, and at least one must be a natural gas plant;</p>
          <p>(5) three representatives of impacted workers at impacted facilities;</p>
          <p>(6) one representative of impacted workers employed by companies that, under contract, regularly perform construction, maintenance, or repair work at an impacted facility;</p>
          <p>(7) one representative with professional economic development or workforce retraining experience;</p>
          <p>(8) two representatives of utilities that operate an impacted facility;</p>
          <p>(9) one representative from a nonprofit organization with expertise and experience delivering energy efficiency and conservation programs;</p>
          <p>(10) one representative of a school district facing revenue loss due to energy transition; and</p>
          <p>(11) one representative from the Coalition of Utility Cities.</p>
          <p>(c) The ex officio nonvoting members of the advisory committee consist of:</p>
          <p>(1) the governor or the governor's designee;</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner of employment and economic development or the commissioner's designee;</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner of commerce or the commissioner's designee;</p>
          <p>(4) the commissioner of labor and industry or the commissioner's designee;</p>
          <p>(5) the commissioner of revenue or the commissioner's designee;</p>
          <p>(6) the executive secretary of the Public Utilities Commission or the secretary's designee;</p>
          <p>(7) the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency or the commissioner's designee; and</p>
          <p>(8) the chancellor of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities or the chancellor's designee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Initial appointments and first meeting.</span></h2>
          <p>The appointing authorities must appoint the members of the advisory committee by August 1, 2021. The commissioner of employment and economic development must convene the first meeting by September 1, 2021, and must act as chair until the advisory committee elects a chair at the first meeting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Officers.</span></h2>
          <p>The committee must elect a chair and vice-chair from among the voting members for terms of two years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Open meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>Advisory committee meetings are subject to chapter 13D.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Conflict of interest.</span></h2>
          <p>An advisory committee member is prohibited from discussing or voting on issues relating to an organization in which the member has either a direct or indirect financial interest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Gifts; grants; donations.</span></h2>
          <p>The advisory committee may accept gifts and grants on behalf of the state and that constitute donations to the state. Funds received under this subdivision are appropriated to the commissioner of employment and economic development to support the activities of the advisory committee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>The advisory committee must meet quarterly and submit an updated energy transition plan annually to the governor and the legislature. Once submitted, the committee shall develop a regular meeting schedule as needed. The chair may call additional meetings as necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Staff.</span></h2>
          <p>The Department of Employment and Economic Development shall serve as staff for the advisory committee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5492.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Expiration.</span></h2>
          <p>This section expires on June 30, 2027.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.5492.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/4/#laws.8.7.0">1Sp2021 c 4 art 8 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.15.4.0">2023 c 53 art 15 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/120/#laws.7.1.0">2024 c 120 art 7 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.5493">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.5493 MINNESOTA ENERGY TRANSITION PLAN.</h1>
        <p>(a) By July 1, 2022, the Energy Transition Advisory Committee established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5492">116J.5492</a> must submit a statewide energy transition plan to the governor and the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees having jurisdiction over economic development and energy.</p>
        <p>(b) The energy transition plan must, at a minimum, for each impacted facility:</p>
        <p>(1) identify the timing and location of impacted facility retirements and projected job losses in communities;</p>
        <p>(2) analyze the estimated fiscal impact of impacted facility retirements on local governments;</p>
        <p>(3) describe the statutes and administrative processes that govern how retired utility property impacts a local government tax base;</p>
        <p>(4) review existing state programs that might support impacted communities and impacted workers, and project the effectiveness of each program's response to the effects of impacted facility retirements; and</p>
        <p>(5) recommend how to effectively respond to the economic effects of impacted facility retirements.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.5493.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/4/#laws.8.8.0">1Sp2021 c 4 art 8 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.55">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.55 COMMUNITY ENERGY TRANSITION GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.55.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "eligible community" means a county, municipality, or tribal government located in Minnesota in which an electric generating plant owned by a public utility, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.02">216B.02</a>, that is powered by coal, nuclear energy, or natural gas:</p>
          <p>(1) is currently operating and (i) is scheduled to cease operations, (ii) whose cessation of operations has been proposed in an integrated resource plan filed with the commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2422">216B.2422</a>, or (iii) whose current operating license expires within 15 years of July 1, 2023; or</p>
          <p>(2) ceased operations or was removed from the local property tax base no earlier than five years before the date an application is made for a grant under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.55.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Program establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>A community energy transition grant program is established in the department to award grants to assist eligible communities to address the economic dislocation associated with the closing of a local electric generating plant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.55.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Account established.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A community energy transition account is created in the special revenue fund in the state treasury. The commissioner shall credit to the account appropriations and transfers to the account. Earnings, including interest, dividends, and any other earnings arising from assets of the account, must be credited to the account. The commissioner shall manage the account.</p>
          <p>(b) Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner for grants under this section and must be expended only as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.55.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application process.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Applications for a grant under this section must be made to the commissioner on a form developed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) A grant application made by a county must include a resolution of support from the legislative body in the city in which the electric generating plant is or was located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.55.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Grant awards; limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A grant awarded to an eligible community under this section must not exceed $1,000,000 in any calendar year. The commissioner may accept grant applications on an ongoing or rolling basis.</p>
          <p>(b) Grants funded with revenues from the renewable development account established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116C.779">116C.779</a> must be awarded to an eligible community located within the retail electric service territory of the public utility that is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116C.779">116C.779</a> or to an eligible community in which an electric generating plant owned by that public utility is located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.55.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Eligible expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Money in the account established in subdivision 3 must be used only to:</p>
          <p>(1) award grants to eligible communities under this section; and</p>
          <p>(2) reimburse the department's reasonable costs to administer this section, up to a maximum of five percent of the appropriation made to the commissioner under this section.
