The commissioner may transfer part of the allowable administrative portion of this appropriation to the Environmental Quality Board to assist communities with regulatory coordination and dedicated technical assistance on conversion for these communities.</p>
          <p>(b) An eligible community awarded a grant under this section may use the grant to plan for or address the economic and social impacts on the eligible community of the electric generating plant's cessation of operations, including but not limited to land use studies, economic planning, researching, planning, and implementing activities, capital costs of public infrastructure necessary for economic development, and impact studies and other planning activities enabling communities to become shovel-ready and support the transition from power plants to other economic activities to minimize the negative impacts of power plant closures on tax revenues and jobs designed to:</p>
          <p>(1) assist workers at the plant find new employment, including worker retraining and developing small business start-up skills;</p>
          <p>(2) increase the eligible community's property tax base; and</p>
          <p>(3) develop alternative economic development strategies to attract new employers to the eligible community.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.55.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15, 2022, the commissioner must submit a report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the committees of the senate and house of representatives with jurisdiction over economic development and energy. The report must describe the number and amount of grants awarded under this section and the uses made of grant funds by eligible communities awarded grants.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.55.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/118/#laws.0.2.0">2020 c 118 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.15.6.0">2023 c 53 art 15 s 6</a>-8</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CONTAMINATION CLEANUP</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.551">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.551 CREATION OF ACCOUNTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.551.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant account.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A contaminated site cleanup and development grant account is created in the special revenue fund, general fund, petroleum tank fund, and remediation fund. Money in any account may be used, as appropriated by law, to make grants as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.554">116J.554</a> and to pay for the commissioner's costs in reviewing applications and making grants. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.28">16A.28</a>, money appropriated to the accounts for this program from any source is available until spent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.551.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Revolving loan account.</span></h2>
          <p>A revolving loan account is created in a special revenue fund for the Minnesota cleanup revolving loan fund, funded by the United States Environmental Protection Agency. Money in the account may be invested by the State Board of Investment. Money in the account must be used to provide loans and grants in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.559">116J.559</a> and the Brownfields Revitalization and Environmental Restoration Act of 2001, Public Law 107-118, title II, under the authority of the United States Environmental Protection Agency, and to pay for the commissioner's cost in reviewing applications and making loans and/or grants.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.551.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.13.1.0">1993 c 375 art 13 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.13.0">2004 c 206 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.15.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/135/#laws.2.6.0">2007 c 135 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/1/1/#laws.14.7.0">1Sp2010 c 1 art 14 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/4/#laws.2.2.0">1Sp2011 c 4 art 2 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.552">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.552 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.552.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope of application.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.551">116J.551</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.557">116J.557</a>, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.552.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cleanup costs.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cleanup costs" or "costs" means the costs of developing and implementing a response action plan, but does not include implementation costs incurred before the award of a grant unless the application for the grant was submitted within 180 days after the response action plan was approved by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.552.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contaminant.</span></h2>
          <p>"Contaminant" means a hazardous substance or a pollutant or contaminant as those terms are defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.02">115B.02</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.552.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Development authority.</span></h2>
          <p>"Development authority" includes a statutory or home rule charter city, county, federally recognized Tribe, housing and redevelopment authority, economic development authority, and a port authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.552.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Metropolitan area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Metropolitan area" means the seven-county metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.552.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Municipality.</span></h2>
          <p>"Municipality" means the statutory or home rule charter city, town, federally recognized Tribe, or, in the case of unorganized territory, the county in which the site is located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.552.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Project costs.</span></h2>
          <p>"Project costs" includes cleanup costs for the site and the cost of related site acquisition, demolition of existing improvements, and installation of public improvements necessary for the development authority to implement the response action plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.552.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Response action plan.</span></h2>
          <p>"Response action plan" means a response action plan approved by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, including a "development action response plan" that meets the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.174#stat.469.174.17">469.174, subdivision 17</a>; and a "voluntary response action plan" under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.175#stat.115B.175.3">115B.175, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.552.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.13.2.0">1993 c 375 art 13 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/224/#laws.0.53.0">1995 c 224 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/255/#laws.2.1.0">1995 c 255 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/200/#laws.2.8.0">1997 c 200 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/33/#laws.0.2.0">2023 c 33 s 2</a>,3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.553">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.553 GRANT APPLICATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.553.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application required.</span></h2>
          <p>To obtain a contamination cleanup development grant, the development authority shall apply to the commissioner. The governing body of the municipality must approve, by resolution, the application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.553.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Required content.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall prescribe and provide the application form. The application must include at least the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) identification of the site;</p>
          <p>(2) an approved response action plan for the site, including the results of engineering and other tests showing the nature and extent of the release or threatened release of contaminants at the site;</p>
          <p>(3) a detailed estimate, along with necessary supporting evidence, of the total cleanup costs for the site;</p>
          <p>(4) an appraisal of the current market value of the property, separately taking into account the effect of the contaminants on the market value, prepared by a qualified independent appraiser licensed under chapter 82B using accepted appraisal methodology or, the estimated market value of the property for the latest year shown on the most recent valuation notice used under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.121">273.121</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) an assessment of the development potential or likely use of the site after completion of the response action plan, including any specific commitments from third parties to construct improvements on the site;</p>
          <p>(6) the manner in which the municipality will meet the local match requirement; and</p>
          <p>(7) any additional information or material that the commissioner prescribes.</p>
          <p>(b) A response action plan is not required as a condition to receive a grant under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.554#stat.116J.554.1">116J.554, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.553.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.13.3.0">1993 c 375 art 13 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/203/#laws.0.4.0">1999 c 203 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.13.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.554">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.554 GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.554.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may make a grant to an applicant development authority to pay for up to 75 percent of the project costs for a qualifying site.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may also make a grant to an applicant development authority to pay up to 75 percent or $50,000, whichever is less, toward the cost of performing contaminant investigations and the development of a response action plan for a qualifying site.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may also make a grant to an applicant to fill a site that would represent more than 50 percent of the remaining land in a city suitable for industrial development if it were properly filled.</p>
          <p>(d) The determination of whether to make a grant for a qualifying site is within the sole discretion of the commissioner, subject to the process provided by this section, and available unencumbered money in the appropriation. The commissioner's decisions and application of the priorities under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.555">116J.555</a> are not subject to judicial review, except for abuse of discretion.</p>
          <p>(e) The total amount of money provided in grants under paragraph (b) may not exceed $500,000 per fiscal year.</p>
          <p>(f) In making grants under paragraph (b), the commissioner shall give priority to applicants that have not received a grant under paragraph (a) or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.252">473.252</a> during the year ending on the date of application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.554.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Metropolitan livable communities.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may not make a grant to a municipality in the metropolitan area unless it is participating in the local housing incentives program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.254">473.254</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.554.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Qualifying sites.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A site qualifies for a grant under this section, if the following criteria are met:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the site is not scheduled for funding during the current or next fiscal year under the Comprehensive Environmental Response, Compensation, and Liability Act, United States Code, title 42, section 9601, et seq. or under the Environmental Response, and Liability Act under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.01">115B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.20">115B.20</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) if the proposed cleanup is completed, it is expected that the site will be improved with buildings or other improvements and these improvements will provide a substantial increase in the property tax base within a reasonable period of time or the site will be used for an important publicly owned or tax-exempt facility.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.554.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.13.4.0">1993 c 375 art 13 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/255/#laws.2.3.0">1995 c 255 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/246/#laws.0.14.0">1997 c 246 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.14.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.73.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 73</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/135/#laws.2.7.0">2007 c 135 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.14.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.555">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.555 PRIORITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.555.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Priorities.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The legislature expects that applications for grants will exceed the available appropriations and the agency will be able to provide grants to only some of the applicant development authorities.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If applications for grants for qualified sites exceed the available appropriations, the agency shall make grants for sites that, in the commissioner's judgment, provide the highest return in public benefits for the public costs incurred and that meet all the requirements provided by law. In making this judgment, the commissioner shall consider the following factors:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the recommendations or ranking of projects by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency regarding the potential threat to public health and the environment that would be reduced or eliminated by completion of each of the response action plans;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the potential increase in the property tax base of the local taxing jurisdictions, considered relative to the fiscal needs of the jurisdictions, that will result from developments that will occur because of completion of each of the response action plans;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the social value to the community of the cleanup and redevelopment of the site, including the importance of development of the proposed public facilities on each of the sites;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the probability that each site will be cleaned up without use of government money in the reasonably foreseeable future by considering but not limited to the current market value of the site versus the cleanup cost;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the amount of cleanup costs for each site; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) the amount of the commitment of municipal or other local resources to pay for the cleanup costs.</p>
          <p class="in">The factors are not listed in a rank order of priority; rather the commissioner may weigh each factor, depending upon the facts and circumstances, as the commissioner considers appropriate. The commissioner may consider other factors that affect the net return of public benefits for completion of the response action plan. The commissioner, notwithstanding the listing of priorities and the goal of maximizing the return of public benefits, shall make grants that distribute available money to sites both within and outside of the metropolitan area. The commissioner shall provide a written statement of the supporting reasons for each grant. Unless sufficient applications are not received for qualifying sites outside of the metropolitan area, at least 35 percent of the money provided as grants must be made for sites located outside of the metropolitan area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.555.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application cycles; reporting to legislature.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) In making grants, the commissioner shall establish semiannual application deadlines in which grants will be authorized from all or part of the available appropriations of money in the account.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner shall annually report to the committees of the senate and house of representatives with jurisdiction over environment and natural resources finance on the status of the cleanup projects undertaken under grants made under the programs. The commissioner shall include in the annual report information on the cleanup and development activities undertaken for the grants made in that and previous fiscal years. The commissioner shall make this report no later than 120 days after the end of the fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.555.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.13.5.0">1993 c 375 art 13 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/224/#laws.0.54.0">1995 c 224 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/255/#laws.2.2.0">1995 c 255 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.27.0">1996 c 470 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/135/#laws.2.8.0">2007 c 135 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.15.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 288 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.556">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.556 LOCAL MATCH REQUIREMENT.</h1>
        <p>In order to qualify for a grant under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.551">116J.551</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.557">116J.557</a>, the municipality must pay for at least one-quarter of the project costs as a local match. The municipality shall pay an amount of the project costs equal to at least 12 percent of the cleanup costs from the municipality's general fund, a property tax levy for that purpose, or other unrestricted money available to the municipality (excluding tax increments). These unrestricted moneys may be spent for project costs, other than cleanup costs, and qualify for the local match payment equal to 12 percent of cleanup costs. The rest of the local match may be paid with tax increments, regional, state, or federal money available for the redevelopment of brownfields or any other money available to the municipality.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.556.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.13.6.0">1993 c 375 art 13 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/255/#laws.2.4.0">1995 c 255 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/246/#laws.0.16.0">1997 c 246 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/2/#laws.1.15.0">2003 c 2 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/152/#laws.2.1.0">2005 c 152 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.557">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.557 COST RECOVERY ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.557.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Cause of action.</span></h2>
          <p>The attorney general or a development authority or municipality that incurs cleanup costs to implement an approved response action plan pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.551">116J.551</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.557">116J.557</a>, may bring an action under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.04">115B.04</a> or other law to recover the reasonable and necessary cleanup costs incurred by the development authority or municipality. The attorney general, development authority, or municipality may recover all cleanup costs incurred whether paid from the proceeds of a grant under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.551">116J.551</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.557">116J.557</a> or funds of the development authority or municipality. Recoverable costs include administrative and legal costs related to the development and implementation of the response action plan but do not include any cost associated with development or redevelopment of property. A development authority or municipality must have the consent of the attorney general to bring or settle an action under this subdivision to recover cleanup costs paid from the proceeds of a grant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.557.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall notify the attorney general when a grant is awarded under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.551">116J.551</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.557">116J.557</a>. Upon request of the attorney general the development authority shall prepare and submit a certification of the cleanup costs and shall cooperate in any cost recovery action brought by the attorney general under subdivision 1. Certification by the development authority of the cleanup costs incurred to develop and implement the approved response action plan is prima facie evidence that the costs are reasonable and necessary in any action brought under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.557.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Attorney general assistance and costs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The attorney general may assist a development authority or municipality, if requested to do so, in bringing an action under subdivision 1 by providing legal and technical advice or other appropriate assistance. The attorney general shall not assess any fee to the development authority or municipality for the assistance but may recover the cost of the assistance as provided in paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) If the attorney general brings or assists in an action brought under subdivision 1, the reasonable litigation expenses or other costs of legal or technical assistance incurred by the attorney general must be deducted from any recovery and paid to the attorney general before proceeds of the recovery are otherwise distributed. The attorney general shall deposit any money so deducted in the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.557.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Disposition of recovered amounts.</span></h2>
          <p>Amounts recovered from responsible persons, after any deduction under subdivision 3, and all other amounts otherwise received by the municipality, the agency, or the attorney general for the site shall be used to reimburse the municipality and the account in proportion to their respective payments for response costs. The amount of recovered costs apportioned to tax increments must be treated by the municipality and development authority as an excess increment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.176#stat.469.176.2">469.176, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.557.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.13.7.0">1993 c 375 art 13 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.2.1.0">1994 c 465 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.558">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.558 EFFECT OF ISSUANCE OF GRANTS.</h1>
        <p>The issuance of a contamination cleanup grant under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.551">116J.551</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.557">116J.557</a> has no effect on the responsibility or the liability of the state, under chapter 115B or any other law, in relation to the contamination at a site or sites for which the grant is issued. The issuance of a grant neither implies any state responsibility for the contamination nor imposes any obligation on the state to participate in the cleanup of the contamination or in the cleanup costs beyond the amount of the grant.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.558.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/643/#laws.0.54.0">1994 c 643 s 54</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.559">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.559 LOANS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may provide loans and grants that meet the criteria of the Brownfields Revitalization and Environmental Restoration Act of 2001, Public Law 107-118, title II, under the authority of the United States Environmental Protection Agency, from the account established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.551#stat.116J.551.2">116J.551, subdivision 2</a>. The commissioner shall prioritize the projects pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.555">116J.555</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.559.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.14.0">2004 c 206 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.561"><b>116J.561</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.91.0">2002 c 393 s 91</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.562"><b>116J.562</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.91.0">2002 c 393 s 91</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.563"><b>116J.563</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.91.0">2002 c 393 s 91</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.564"><b>116J.564</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.91.0">2002 c 393 s 91</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.565"><b>116J.565</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.91.0">2002 c 393 s 91</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.566"><b>116J.566</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.91.0">2002 c 393 s 91</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.567"><b>116J.567</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.91.0">2002 c 393 s 91</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.57"><b>116J.57</b> [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/246/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 246 s 17</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.571">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.571 CREATION OF ACCOUNTS.</h1>
        <p>Two redevelopment accounts are created, one in the general fund and one in the bond proceeds fund. Money for the program: (1) may be used to make grants as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.575">116J.575 </a>and loans as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5761">116J.5761</a>; (2) may be used to pay for the commissioner's costs in reviewing applications and making grants and loans; and (3) is available until spent. The repayment of principal and interest on loans and investment income earned on money in the account is deposited in the special revenue fund and may be used for making grants and loans and for administrative costs and are appropriated for such purposes.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.571.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.47.0">2002 c 393 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.16.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.2.0">2012 c 288 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.572">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.572 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.572.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope of application.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.571">116J.571</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5765">116J.5765</a>, the terms in this section have the meanings given.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.572.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Demolition costs.</span></h2>
          <p>"Demolition costs" means the costs of demolition, destruction, removal, and clearance of all structures and other improvements on the project site, including interior remedial activities, and proper disposal thereof. As used in this subdivision, "structure" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116G.03#stat.116G.03.11">116G.03, subdivision 11</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.572.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Development authority.</span></h2>
          <p>"Development authority" includes a statutory or home rule charter city, county, housing and redevelopment authority, economic development authority, or port authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.572.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Metropolitan area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Metropolitan area" means the seven-county metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.572.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Municipality.</span></h2>
          <p>"Municipality" means the statutory or home rule charter city, town, or, in the case of unorganized territory, the county in which the redevelopment or project is located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.572.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Redevelopment costs or costs.</span></h2>
          <p>"Redevelopment costs" or "costs" means the costs of land acquisition, stabilizing unstable soils when infill is required, infrastructure improvements, and ponding or other environmental infrastructure, demolition costs and costs necessary for adaptive reuse of buildings, including remedial activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.572.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.17.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.572.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.48.0">2002 c 393 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.17.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.3.0">2012 c 288 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.573"><b>116J.573</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.124.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 124</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.574">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.574 GRANT APPLICATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.574.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application required.</span></h2>
          <p>To obtain a redevelopment grant, a development authority shall apply to the commissioner. The governing body of the municipality must approve the application by resolution.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.574.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Required content.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prescribe and provide the application form. The application must include at least the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) identification of the site;</p>
          <p>(2) a redevelopment plan for the site;</p>
          <p>(3) a detailed estimate, along with necessary supporting evidence, of the total redevelopment costs for the site;</p>
          <p>(4) an assessment of the development potential or likely use of the site after completion of the redevelopment plan, including any specific commitments from third parties to construct improvements on the site;</p>
          <p>(5) the manner in which the municipality will meet the local match requirement; and</p>
          <p>(6) any additional information or material the commissioner prescribes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.574.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.50.0">2002 c 393 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.18.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.575">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.575 GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.575.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner discretion.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may make a grant for up to 50 percent of the eligible costs of a project. The determination of whether to make a grant for a site is within the discretion of the commissioner, subject to this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.571">116J.571</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.574">116J.574</a> and available unencumbered money in the redevelopment account. The commissioner's decisions and application of the priorities under this section are not subject to judicial review, except for abuse of discretion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.575.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Priorities.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) If applications for grants exceed the available appropriations, grants shall be made for sites that, in the commissioner's judgment, provide the highest return in public benefits for the public costs incurred. "Public benefits" include job creation, bioscience development, environmental benefits to the state and region, efficient use of public transportation, efficient use of existing infrastructure, provision of affordable housing, multiuse development that constitutes community rebuilding rather than single-use development, crime reduction, blight reduction, community stabilization, and property tax base maintenance or improvement. In making this judgment, the commissioner shall give priority to redevelopment projects with one or more of the following characteristics:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the need for redevelopment in conjunction with contamination remediation needs;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the redevelopment project meets current tax increment financing requirements for a redevelopment district and tax increments will contribute to the project;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the redevelopment potential within the municipality;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) proximity to public transit if located in the metropolitan area;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) redevelopment costs related to expansion of a bioscience business in Minnesota;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) multijurisdictional projects that take into account the need for affordable housing, transportation, and environmental impact; or</p>
          <p class="in">(7) the project advances or promotes the green economy as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.437">116J.437</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The factors in paragraph (a) are not listed in a rank order of priority; rather, the commissioner may weigh each factor, depending upon the facts and circumstances, as the commissioner considers appropriate. The commissioner may consider other factors that affect the net return of public benefits for completion of the redevelopment plan. The commissioner, notwithstanding the listing of priorities and the goal of maximizing the return of public benefits, shall make grants that distribute available money to sites both within and outside of the metropolitan area. Unless sufficient applications are not received for qualifying sites outside of the metropolitan area, at least 50 percent of the money provided as grants must be made for sites located outside of the metropolitan area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.575.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application cycles.</span></h2>
          <p>In making grants, the commissioner shall establish semiannual application deadlines in which grants will be authorized from all or part of the available money in the account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.575.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Match required.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to qualify for a grant under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.571">116J.571</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.575">116J.575</a>, the municipality must pay for at least one-half of the redevelopment costs as a local match from any money available to the municipality.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.575.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Grant repayment.</span></h2>
          <p>If a project fails to substantially provide the public benefits listed in the grant application within five years from the date of the grant award, the commissioner may require that 100 percent of the grant amount be repaid by the development authority over a term not to exceed ten years. The commissioner may exercise discretion to require repayment of only a portion of the grant amount taking into account the public benefits generated by the completed development.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.575.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.51.0">2002 c 393 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/20/#laws.1.33.0">2005 c 20 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.19.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/212/#laws.3.9.0">2006 c 212 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/135/#laws.2.9.0">2007 c 135 art 2 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/356/#laws.0.4.0">2008 c 356 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.4.0">2012 c 288 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.5761">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.5761 LOANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5761.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may make loans to development authorities for projects that meet the criteria under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5761">116J.5761</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5764">116J.5764</a>. The commissioner may make a loan for up to 100 percent of the estimated land acquisition and demolition costs of the project. The determination whether to make a loan for a project is within the discretion of the commissioner, subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5761">116J.5761 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5764">116J.5764</a>, and available unencumbered money in the redevelopment accounts. The commissioner's decisions and application of the priorities under this section are not subject to judicial review, except for abuse of discretion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5761.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Qualifying projects.</span></h2>
          <p>A project qualifies for a loan under this section, if the following criteria are met:</p>
          <p>(1) the property and structures are owned by the development authority;</p>
          <p>(2) the structures on the property have been vacant for at least one year;</p>
          <p>(3) the structures constitute a threat to public safety because of inadequate maintenance, dilapidation, obsolescence, or abandonment;</p>
          <p>(4) the structures are not listed on the National Register of Historic Places; and</p>
          <p>(5) upon completion of the demolition, the development authority reasonably expects that the property will be improved and these improvements will result in economic development benefits to the municipality.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.5761.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.5.0">2012 c 288 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.5762">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.5762 LOAN APPLICATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5762.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application required.</span></h2>
          <p>To obtain a demolition loan, a development authority shall apply to the commissioner. The governing body of the municipality must approve the application by resolution.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5762.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Required content.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prescribe and provide the application form. The application must include at least the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) identification of the property;</p>
          <p>(2) proof of ownership by the development authority;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of how the structures on the property constitute a threat to public safety, are functionally obsolete, or are economically unfeasible to repair;</p>
          <p>(4) length of vacancy;</p>
          <p>(5) a detailed estimate, along with supporting evidence, of the total demolition costs for the project;</p>
          <p>(6) evidence that the structures on the property are not listed on the National Register of Historic Places;</p>
          <p>(7) an assessment of the development potential or likely use of the property after completion of the demolition plan;</p>
          <p>(8) the current appraised or assessed value of the property;</p>
          <p>(9) financial documentation necessary for loan underwriting;</p>
          <p>(10) other sources of funding if the total estimated demolition costs exceed the loan amount;</p>
          <p>(11) the proposed source of funds to be used for repayment of the loan;</p>
          <p>(12) information showing the applicant's financial condition and ability to repay the loan;</p>
          <p>(13) the proposed term and principal repayment schedule for the loan;</p>
          <p>(14) the statutory authorization for the applicant to issue bonds, together with a statement that the statutory provision authorizes the use of proceeds of such bonds to pay demolition costs and secure the loan; and</p>
          <p>(15) any additional information the commissioner prescribes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.5762.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.6.0">2012 c 288 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.5763">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.5763 PRIORITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5763.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Priorities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If applications for loans exceed the available appropriations, loans shall be made for projects that, in the commissioner's judgment, provide the highest return in public benefits for the public costs incurred. "Public benefits" include health, safety and other environmental benefits, blight reduction including the property's potential for improved economic vitality, functionality and aesthetics, community stabilization, crime reduction, reduced maintenance costs, and the potential for future development. In making this judgment, the commissioner shall consider the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the extent to which the existing property conditions threaten public safety;</p>
          <p>(2) the length of vacancy of the property;</p>
          <p>(3) the development potential of the property;</p>
          <p>(4) the proximity of the property to existing sufficient public infrastructure; and</p>
          <p>(5) the applicant's financial condition and ability to repay the loan.</p>
          <p>(b) The factors in paragraph (a) are not listed in a rank order or priority; rather, the commissioner may weigh each factor, depending upon the facts and circumstances, as the commissioner considers appropriate. The commissioner may consider other factors that affect the net return of public benefits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5763.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application cycle.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish semiannual application deadlines in which loans will be authorized from available money in the accounts.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.5763.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.7.0">2012 c 288 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.5764">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.5764 LOAN TERMS AND CONDITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5764.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Terms.</span></h2>
          <p>Loans to development authorities for demolition costs may be made by the commissioner subject to the following terms and conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) the agreement to repay the loan may be a general obligation of the development authority, payable primarily from a dedicated source of revenue, or other security subject to review and approval by the commissioner, and the development authority must deliver its bond or note to the commissioner to secure the loan;</p>
          <p>(2) the term of the loan may not exceed 15 years;</p>
          <p>(3) the loan shall bear interest at a rate equal to two percent, but interest will not accrue during the first two years of the loan term;</p>
          <p>(4) the development authority shall make semiannual interest payments and annual principal payments beginning in the third year of the loan until the end of the term;</p>
          <p>(5) the principal amount of a loan may not exceed $1,000,000;</p>
          <p>(6) loan proceeds shall be disbursed for eligible demolition costs as incurred or paid by the borrower and upon submission of invoices and other supporting documentation satisfactory to the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(7) an eligible borrower shall establish a dedicated source of revenue for repayment of the loan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5764.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Modification of loan terms.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner has the discretion to consent to the modification of the rate of interest, time of payment, installment of principal or interest, or other term of a loan made under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5761">116J.5761</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5764">116J.5764</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.5764.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Forgiveness.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may forgive principal of the loan and interest accrued but unpaid thereon, if any, up to 50 percent of the original loan amount, not to exceed the costs of demolition, upon completion of the redevelopment plan, if the project would otherwise have received grant funding in the most recent semiannual grant round, based on the priorities in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.575">116J.575</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.5764.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.8.0">2012 c 288 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/64/#laws.0.1.0">2013 c 64 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.5765">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.5765 NONLIABILITY.</h1>
        <p>The state shall have no responsibility or liability relating to or arising out of activities at the site of a project solely by reason of the making of a grant or loan by the commissioner under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5761">116J.5761</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.5764">116J.5764</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.5765.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.9.0">2012 c 288 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.578">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.578 BIOSCIENCE SUBSIDY.</h1>
        <p class="in">Any bioscience or biotechnology project financed in whole or in part by state appropriations or other public subsidies must document how and to what it extent the project will provide a benefit to consumers in the form of more affordable pricing of the products or services being publicly subsidized. The documentation must be reported to the committees of the legislature with responsibility for economic development and to committees with responsibility for finance.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.578.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/300/#laws.0.47.0">2008 c 300 s 47</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.116J.58">
        <h1>116J.58 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.58.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.44.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 44</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.58.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2008 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.035">116J.035, subd 1a</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.58.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.124.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 124</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.58.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/465/#laws.1.31.0">1986 c 465 art 1 s 31</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.581"><b>116J.581</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/200/#laws.1.74.0">1997 c 200 art 1 s 74</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.59"><b>116J.59</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.44.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 44</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.60">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.60 PROMOTIONAL EXPENSES.</h1>
        <p>In the promotion of trade and economic development of the state, the commissioner of employment and economic development may expend money appropriated by the legislature for these purposes in the same manner as private persons, firms, corporations, and associations make expenditures for these purposes. An expenditure for food, lodging, or travel is not governed by the travel rules of the commissioner of management and budget. No money shall be expended for the appearance in radio or television broadcasts by an elected public official.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.60.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/48/#laws.0.84.0">Ex1967 c 48 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/455/#laws.0.84.0">1977 c 455 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/289/#laws.0.55.0">1983 c 289 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.17.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/171/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 171 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/204/#laws.0.42.0">2008 c 204 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.61"><b>116J.61</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.44.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 44</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.613"><b>116J.613</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116K.14">116K.14</a>]
</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.116J.615">
        <h1>116J.615 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.615.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/171/#laws.0.20.0">2004 c 171 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.615.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.35.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 35</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.615.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/238/#laws.1.26.0">1991 c 238 art 1 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.616"><b>116J.616</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/171/#laws.0.20.0">2004 c 171 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.617">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.617 TOURISM LOAN PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.617.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may establish a tourism revolving loan program and a tourism guarantee loan program to provide loans, participate in loans, or guarantee loans to resorts, campgrounds, lodging facilities, and other tourism-related businesses. The commissioner shall work with financial institutions in making or participating in loans or guaranteeing loans under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.617.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible borrower.</span></h2>
          <p>To receive a loan under this section, the borrower must be a sole proprietorship, partnership, or corporation engaged in a tourism-related business or other entity that is defined by the standard industrial classification codes of 7011 and 7033 as set out in the Code of Federal Regulations, title 13, section 121.2. An eligible borrower under this section must maintain the business or other entity as a tourism-related entity as defined by this subdivision during the term of the loan. An eligible borrower may not receive a loan or loan guarantee under this section if the borrower has received a tourism-related loan, loan participation, or guarantee made by the state in the past 36 months.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.617.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligible loan.</span></h2>
          <p>The maximum loan made or participated in under this section may not be for more than 50 percent of the total cost of the project. Loan proceeds may be used for the following purposes: acquisition of an existing building, building construction and improvement, land site improvement, equipment, other construction costs, and engineering costs. Project-related expenditures made more than 30 days before an application may not be financed by a loan made, guaranteed, or participated in under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.617.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Loan terms.</span></h2>
          <p>The maximum term of a loan made, guaranteed, or participated in under this section may not exceed the useful life of the real property or 80 percent of the useful life of the equipment or machinery, or the following limits, whichever is less:</p>
          <p>(1) ten years for land, building, or other real property;</p>
          <p>(2) five years for equipment or machinery; or</p>
          <p>(3) a weighted average of the limits under clauses (1) and (2) for loans made, guaranteed, or participated in for a combination of real property and equipment or machinery.</p>
          <p>The commissioner may establish interest rates for loans made under this section. All loans made must be secured by collateral.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.617.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.40.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.617.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.40.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.617.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.139.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 139</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/369/#laws.0.46.0">1993 c 369 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.62"><b>116J.62</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/289/#laws.0.119.0">1983 c 289 s 119</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.63">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.63 SALE OF PAMPHLETS AND PUBLICATIONS; FEES; ADVERTISING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.63.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Sale.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may sell reports, publications, or related publicity or promotional material of the department that the commissioner determines should not be supplied gratis to those who wish to employ them in the conduct of their business.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.63.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Fees for reports, publications, or related publicity or promotional material are not subject to the rulemaking requirements of chapter 14 and are not subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1285">16A.1285</a>. The fees prescribed by the commissioner must be commensurate with the distribution objective of the department for the material produced or with the cost of furnishing the services. Except as described in paragraph (b), all fees for materials and services must be deposited in the general fund.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may sell marketing materials at cost to economic development organizations and others in quantities that would not otherwise be available through general fund appropriations. Funds received must be placed in a special revolving account and are appropriated to the commissioner to pay for the production of the materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.63.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Advertising.</span></h2>
          <p>Department publications may contain advertising and may receive advertising revenue from profit and nonprofit organizations, associations, individuals and corporations, and other state, federal or local government agencies. Advertising revenues shall be deposited in the general fund. The commissioner shall set advertising rates and fees commensurate with services rendered and distribution objectives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.63.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/171/#laws.0.20.0">2004 c 171 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.63.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/587/#laws.0.14.0">1947 c 587 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/333/#laws.0.99.0">1979 c 333 s 99</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.19.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.141.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 141</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.3.20.0">1996 c 305 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/223/#laws.2.25.0">1999 c 223 art 2 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.20.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.64">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.64 LOANS TO INDIANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section the following terms shall have the meanings ascribed to them herein.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Indian.</span></h2>
          <p>"Indian" means a person who is an enrolled member of a federally recognized Minnesota-based band or tribe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" means an individual Indian, or a partnership comprising Indians only, or a corporation whose stock is owned wholly by Indians.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Tribal government.</span></h2>
          <p>"Tribal government" means the reservation business committee, board of trustees, tribal council, federally recognized tribal entity, or equivalent duly constituted tribal authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means the Department of Employment and Economic Development.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Eligible organization.</span></h2>
          <p>"Eligible organization" means any organization approved by a tribal government to administer its portion of the Indian business loan fund allotted to the tribal government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Administration.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The remaining 20 percent of the tax revenue received by the county auditor under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.165#stat.273.165.1">273.165, subdivision 1</a>, shall be remitted by the county auditor to the commissioner of management and budget and shall be deposited in an account in the special revenue fund. The account is established under the jurisdiction and control of the agency, which may engage in a business loan program for Indians as that term is defined in subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(b) The tribal governments or eligible organization may administer the account, provided that, before making any eligible loans, each tribal government must submit to the agency, for its review and approval, a plan for that government's loan program which specifically describes, as to that program, its content, the application and reporting forms, utilization of money, administration, operation, implementation, and other matters required by the agency. The plan may provide for the tribal government to contract with an eligible organization to administer its loan program.</p>
          <p>(c) All such plans must provide for a reasonable balance in the distribution of money appropriated pursuant to this section to make business loans between Indians residing on and off the reservations within the state. Each tribal government may allocate all, or a portion of, the funds in its account to one or more other tribal governments for purposes of making eligible loans. As a condition to the making of such eligible loans, the tribal governments shall enter into a loan agreement and other contractual arrangements with the agency to carry out this chapter, and shall agree that all official books and records relating to the business loan program shall be subject to audit by the legislative auditor in the same manner prescribed for agencies of state government.</p>
          <p>(d) Whenever money is appropriated by the commissioner of management and budget to the agency solely for the purposes in this subdivision, the agency shall record in the Indian business loan account the receipt and disbursement of the money and of the income, gain and loss from the investment and reinvestment of the money.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Processing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An Indian desiring a loan for the purpose of starting a business enterprise or expanding an existing business shall make application to the appropriate tribal government. The application shall be forwarded to the appropriate eligible organization, if it is participating in the program, for consideration in conformity with the plans submitted by said tribal governments. The tribal government may approve the application if it determines that the loan would advance the goals of the Indian business loan program. If the tribal government is not participating in the program, the agency may directly approve or deny the loan application.</p>
          <p>(b) If the application is approved, the tribal government shall forward the application, together with all relevant documents pertinent thereto, to the commissioner of the agency, who shall request a payment to be issued to the applicant or the applicable tribal government, if it is administering the loan, with appropriate notations identifying the borrower.</p>
          <p>(c) The tribal government, eligible organization, or the agency, if it is administering the loan, shall maintain records of transactions for each borrower in a manner consistent with good accounting practice. The interest rate on a loan shall be established by the tribal government or the agency, but may be no less than two percent per annum nor more than ten percent per annum. When any portion of a debt is repaid, the tribal government, eligible organization, or the agency, if it is administering the loan, shall remit the amount so received plus interest paid thereon to the commissioner of management and budget through the agency. The amount so received shall be credited to the Indian business loan account.</p>
          <p>(d) On the placing of a loan, additional money equal to ten percent of the total amount made available to any tribal government, eligible organization, or the agency, if it is administering the loan, for loans during the fiscal year shall be paid to the tribal government, eligible organization, or the agency, prior to December 31 for the purpose of financing administrative costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Loan period.</span></h2>
          <p>Loans made under subdivision 7 shall be limited to a period of ten years, if made for the purpose of financing nonreal estate purchases. Loans made for the purpose of financing real estate purchases, where such real property is to be used for nonresidential purposes only, shall be limited to a period of 20 years, and shall be a lien on the real property so acquired. Under no circumstances shall the state take a position junior to third lien. In instances where it is impossible or undesirable to secure a lien against real property, the state may secure a lien against personal property for an amount equal to, or greater than, the face value of the loan to ensure that adequate collateral is provided.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person misrepresenting facts regarding the Indian ancestry of a prospective borrower for the purpose of securing a loan under subdivision 7, whether such borrower is an individual, partnership or corporation, shall be guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Tax revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>The county auditor shall remit the tax revenue received yearly to the commissioner of management and budget as required by subdivision 6 no later than December 15.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.64.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>There is appropriated annually an amount equal to the tax revenue allotted under subdivision 7.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.64.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/254/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 254 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/650/#laws.20.4.0">1973 c 650 art 20 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/430/#laws.0.25.0">1977 c 430 s 25</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/333/#laws.0.100.0">1979 c 333 s 100</a>-102; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/391/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 391 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/308/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 308 s 1</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.210.0">1981 c 356 s 210</a>,211,248; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.128.0">1982 c 424 s 128</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/558/#laws.4.1.0">1984 c 558 art 4 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.4.16.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 4 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.47.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/356/#laws.0.27.0">1989 c 356 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.15.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.15.0">2004 c 206 s 15</a>-21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.3.21.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 3 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.645"><b>116J.645</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.35.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/337/#laws.0.20.0">1993 c 337 s 20</a>]</div>
