      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.655"><b>116J.655</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121.72">121.72</a>]
</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.656"><b>116J.656</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.44.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 44</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.657"><b>116J.657</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/347/#laws.1.36.0">2010 c 347 art 1 s 36</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.658">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.658 MINNESOTA SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT PROJECT.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of employment and economic development shall lead a public-private project with science and technology experts from public, academic, and private sectors to advise state agency collaboration to design, coordinate, and administer a strategic science and technology program for the state designed to promote the welfare of the people of the state, maximize the economic growth of the state, and create and retain jobs in the state's industrial base through enhancement of Minnesota's:</p>
        <p>(1) high technology research and development capabilities;</p>
        <p>(2) product and process innovation and commercialization;</p>
        <p>(3) high technology manufacturing capabilities;</p>
        <p>(4) science and technology business environment; and</p>
        <p>(5) science and technology workforce preparation.</p>
        <p>(b) Project membership shall consist of science and technology experts from public, academic, and private sectors. A member must have a background in science or technology in order to serve on the project. The project members shall consist of at least 13 members as follows:</p>
        <p>(1) a representative of the University of Minnesota;</p>
        <p>(2) a representative of Minnesota State Colleges and Universities;</p>
        <p>(3) the chief executive officer of Mayo Clinic or a designee; and</p>
        <p>(4) six chief executive officers or designees from science- or technology-oriented companies and four representatives from science- and technology-oriented trade organizations.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner of employment and economic development must report by January 15, 2010, to the legislative committees having jurisdiction over science and technology and economic development policy and finance on the activities of the project and must recommend changes or additions to its organization, including specific recommendations for necessary legislation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.658.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.16.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.6581"><b>116J.6581</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/271/#laws.1.3.0">2014 c 271 art 1 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.659">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.659 CANNABIS INDUSTRY STARTUP FINANCING GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.659.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of employment and economic development shall establish CanStartup, a program to award grants to nonprofit corporations to fund loans to new cannabis microbusinesses and to support job creation in communities where long-term residents are eligible to be social equity applicants.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.659.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Cannabis microbusiness" means a cannabis business that meets the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/342.28">342.28</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
          <p>(d) "Industry" means the legal cannabis industry in the state of Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(e) "New business" means a legal cannabis business that has been in existence for three years or less.</p>
          <p>(f) "Program" means the CanStartup grant program.</p>
          <p>(g) "Social equity applicant" means a person who meets the qualification requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/342.17">342.17</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.659.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The CanStartup revolving loan account is established in the special revenue fund. Money in the account, including interest, is appropriated to the commissioner to make grants under the CanStartup program.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must award grants to nonprofit corporations through a competitive grant process.</p>
          <p>(c) To receive grant money, a nonprofit corporation must submit a written application to the commissioner using a form developed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(d) In awarding grants under this subdivision, the commissioner shall give weight to whether the nonprofit corporation:</p>
          <p>(1) has a board of directors that includes citizens experienced in business and community development, new business enterprises, and creating jobs for people facing barriers to education or employment;</p>
          <p>(2) has the technical skills to analyze projects;</p>
          <p>(3) is familiar with other available public and private funding sources and economic development programs;</p>
          <p>(4) can initiate and implement economic development projects;</p>
          <p>(5) can establish and administer a revolving loan account;</p>
          <p>(6) can work with job referral networks that assist people facing barriers to education or employment; and</p>
          <p>(7) has established relationships with communities where long-term residents are eligible to be social equity applicants.</p>
          <p class="bl">The commissioner shall make grants that will assist new cannabis microbusinesses.</p>
          <p>(e) A nonprofit corporation that receives a grant under the program must:</p>
          <p>(1) establish a commissioner-certified revolving loan account for the purpose of making eligible loans; and</p>
          <p>(2) enter into an agreement with the commissioner that the commissioner shall fund loans that the nonprofit corporation makes to new cannabis microbusinesses. The commissioner shall review existing agreements with nonprofit corporations every five years and may renew or terminate an agreement based on that review. In making this review, the commissioner shall consider, among other criteria, the criteria in paragraph (d).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.659.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Loans to businesses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The criteria in this subdivision apply to loans made by nonprofit corporations under the program.</p>
          <p>(b) Loans must be used to support a new cannabis microbusiness in the legal cannabis industry. Priority must be given to loans to businesses owned by individuals who are eligible to be social equity applicants and businesses located in communities where long-term residents are eligible to be social equity applicants.</p>
          <p>(c) Loans must be made to cannabis microbusinesses that are not likely to undertake the project for which loans are sought without assistance from the program.</p>
          <p>(d) The minimum state contribution to a loan is $2,500 and the maximum is either:</p>
          <p>(1) $50,000; or</p>
          <p>(2) $150,000, if state contributions are matched by an equal or greater amount of new private investment.</p>
          <p>(e) Loan applications given preliminary approval by the nonprofit corporation must be forwarded to the commissioner for approval. The commissioner must give final approval for each loan made by the nonprofit corporation under the program.</p>
          <p>(f) A cannabis microbusiness that receives a loan may apply to renew the loan. Renewal applications must be made on an annual basis and a cannabis microbusiness may receive loans for up to six consecutive years. A nonprofit corporation may renew a loan to a cannabis microbusiness that is no longer a new business provided the business would otherwise qualify for an initial loan and is in good standing with the nonprofit corporation and the commissioner. A nonprofit corporation may adjust the amount of a renewed loan, or not renew a loan, if the nonprofit corporation determines that the cannabis microbusiness is financially stable and is substantially likely to continue the project for which the loan renewal is sought.</p>
          <p>(g) If a borrower has met lender criteria, including being current with all payments for a minimum of three years, the commissioner may approve either full or partial forgiveness of interest or principal amounts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.659.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Revolving loan account administration.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish a minimum interest rate for loans or guarantees to ensure that necessary loan administration costs are covered. The interest rate charged by a nonprofit corporation for a loan under this section must not exceed the Wall Street Journal prime rate. For a loan under this section, the nonprofit corporation may charge a loan origination fee equal to or less than one percent of the loan value. The nonprofit corporation may retain the amount of the origination fee.</p>
          <p>(b) Loan repayment of principal must be paid to the commissioner for deposit in the CanStartup revolving loan account. Loan interest payments must be deposited in a revolving loan account created by the nonprofit corporation originating the loan being repaid for further distribution or use, consistent with the criteria of this section.</p>
          <p>(c) Administrative expenses of the nonprofit corporations with whom the commissioner enters into agreements, including expenses incurred by a nonprofit corporation in providing financial, technical, managerial, and marketing assistance to a business receiving a loan under this section, are eligible program expenses the commissioner may agree to pay under the grant agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.659.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Program outreach.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make extensive efforts to publicize this program, including through partnerships with community organizations, particularly those organizations located in areas where long-term residents are eligible to be social equity applicants.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.659.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reporting requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A nonprofit corporation that receives a grant shall:</p>
          <p>(1) submit an annual report to the commissioner by February 1 of each year that the nonprofit corporation participates in the program that includes a description of businesses supported by the grant program, an account of loans made during the calendar year, the program's impact on business creation and job creation, particularly in communities where long-term residents are eligible to be social equity applicants, the source and amount of money collected and distributed by the program, the program's assets and liabilities, and an explanation of administrative expenses; and</p>
          <p>(2) provide for an independent annual audit to be performed in accordance with generally accepted accounting practices and auditing standards and submit a copy of each annual audit report to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) By March 1, 2024, and each March 1 thereafter, the commissioner must submit a report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the committees of the house of representatives and the senate having jurisdiction over economic development that details awards given through the CanStartup program and the use of grant money, including any measures of success toward financing new cannabis microbusinesses and creating jobs in communities where long-term residents are eligible to be social equity applicants.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.659.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/63/#laws.3.1.0">2023 c 63 art 3 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.6595">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.6595 CANNABIS INDUSTRY NAVIGATION GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.6595.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of employment and economic development shall establish CanNavigate, a program to award grants to eligible organizations to help individuals navigate the regulatory structure of the legal cannabis industry.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.6595.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
          <p>(c) "Eligible organization" means any organization capable of helping individuals navigate the regulatory structure of the legal cannabis industry, particularly individuals facing barriers to education or employment, and may include educational institutions, nonprofit organizations, private businesses, community groups, units of local government, or partnerships between different types of organizations.</p>
          <p>(d) "Industry" means the legal cannabis industry in the state of Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(e) "Program" means the CanNavigate grant program.</p>
          <p>(f) "Social equity applicant" means a person who meets the qualification requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/342.17">342.17</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.6595.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grants to organizations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Grant money awarded to eligible organizations may be used for both developing technical assistance resources relevant to the regulatory structure of the legal cannabis industry and for providing technical assistance or navigation services to individuals.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must award grants to eligible organizations through a competitive grant process.</p>
          <p>(c) To receive grant money, an eligible organization must submit a written application to the commissioner, using a form developed by the commissioner, explaining the organization's ability to assist individuals in navigating the regulatory structure of the legal cannabis industry, particularly individuals facing barriers to education or employment.</p>
          <p>(d) An eligible organization's grant application must also include:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of the proposed technical assistance or navigation services, including the types of individuals targeted for assistance;</p>
          <p>(2) any evidence of the organization's past success in providing technical assistance or navigation services to individuals, particularly individuals who live in areas where long-term residents are eligible to be social equity applicants;</p>
          <p>(3) an estimate of the cost of providing the technical assistance;</p>
          <p>(4) the sources and amounts of any nonstate money or in-kind contributions that will supplement grant money, including any amounts that individuals will be charged to receive assistance; and</p>
          <p>(5) any additional information requested by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(e) In awarding grants under this subdivision, the commissioner shall give weight to applications from organizations that demonstrate a history of successful technical assistance or navigation services, particularly for individuals facing barriers to education or employment. The commissioner shall also give weight to applications where the proposed technical assistance will serve areas where long-term residents are eligible to be social equity applicants. To the extent practicable, the commissioner shall fund technical assistance for a variety of sectors in the legal cannabis industry, including both processing and retail sectors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.6595.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Program outreach.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make extensive efforts to publicize these grants, including through partnerships with community organizations, particularly those organizations located in areas where long-term residents are eligible to be social equity applicants.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.6595.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reports to the legislature.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15, 2024, and each January 15 thereafter, the commissioner must submit a report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the committees of the house of representatives and the senate having jurisdiction over economic development that details awards given through the CanNavigate program and the use of grant money, including any measures of success toward helping individuals navigate the regulatory structure of the legal cannabis industry.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.6595.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/63/#laws.3.2.0">2023 c 63 art 3 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">BUSINESS ASSISTANCE AND LICENSING</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.66">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.66 BUSINESS ASSISTANCE.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall establish within the department a business assistance center. The center shall consist of (1) a Bureau of Small Business which shall have as its sole function the provision of assistance to small businesses in the state and (2) a bureau of licenses to assist all businesses in obtaining state licenses and permits. This center shall be accorded at least equal status with the other major operating units within the department. A small business advocate office is established in the Business Assistance Center to provide one-stop access for small businesses in need of information or assistance in obtaining or renewing licenses, meeting state regulatory requirements, or resolving disputes with state agencies.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.66.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/709/#laws.0.2.0">1978 c 709 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/246/#laws.0.2.0">1979 c 246 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.214.0">1981 c 356 s 214</a>,248; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/288/#laws.0.10.0">2012 c 288 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.661"><b>116J.661</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.35.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 35</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.67"><b>116J.67</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.05">116M.05</a>]
</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.68">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.68 BUREAU OF SMALL BUSINESS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.68.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p>The Bureau of Small Business within the business assistance center shall serve as a clearinghouse, technical assistance center, and referral service for information and other assistance needed by small businesses including small targeted group businesses and small businesses located in an economically disadvantaged area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.68.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau shall:</p>
          <p>(1) provide information and assistance with respect to all aspects of business planning, business finance, and business management related to the start-up, operation, or expansion of a small business in Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(2) refer persons interested in the start-up, operation, or expansion of a small business in Minnesota to assistance programs sponsored by federal agencies, state agencies, educational institutions, chambers of commerce, civic organizations, community development groups, private industry associations, and other organizations;</p>
          <p>(3) plan, develop, and implement a master file of information on small business assistance programs of federal, state, and local governments, and other public and private organizations so as to provide comprehensive, timely information to the bureau's clients;</p>
          <p>(4) employ staff with adequate and appropriate skills and education and training for the delivery of information and assistance;</p>
          <p>(5) seek out and utilize, to the extent practicable, contributed expertise and services of federal, state, and local governments, educational institutions, and other public and private organizations;</p>
          <p>(6) maintain a close and continued relationship with the director of the procurement program within the Department of Administration so as to facilitate the department's duties and responsibilities under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.16">16C.16</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.19">16C.19</a> relating to the small targeted group business and economically disadvantaged business program of the state;</p>
          <p>(7) develop an information system which will enable the commissioner and other state agencies to efficiently store, retrieve, analyze, and exchange data regarding small business development and growth in the state. All executive branch agencies of state government and the secretary of state shall to the extent practicable, assist the bureau in the development and implementation of the information system;</p>
          <p>(8) establish and maintain a toll-free telephone number, email account, and other electronic contact mediums determined by the commissioner so that all small business persons anywhere in the state may contact the bureau office for assistance. An outreach program shall be established to make the existence of the bureau and the assistance and services the bureau may provide to small businesses well known to its potential clientele throughout the state. If the small business person requires a referral to another provider the bureau may use the business assistance referral system established by the Minnesota Project Outreach Corporation;</p>
          <p>(9) conduct research and provide data as required by the state legislature;</p>
          <p>(10) develop and publish material on all aspects of the start-up, operation, or expansion of a small business in Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(11) collect and disseminate information on state procurement opportunities, including information on the procurement process;</p>
          <p>(12) develop a public awareness program regarding state assistance programs for small businesses, including those programs specifically for socially disadvantaged small business persons. The commissioner may utilize print and electronic newsletters, personal contacts, advertising devices as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/173.02#stat.173.02.16">173.02, subdivision 16</a>, social media, other electronic and print news media advertising, and any other means determined by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(13) enter into agreements with the federal government and other public and private entities to serve as the statewide coordinator or host agency for the federal small business development center program under United States Code, title 15, section 648; and</p>
          <p>(14) assist providers in the evaluation of their programs and the assessment of their service area needs. The bureau may establish model evaluation techniques and performance standards for providers to use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.68.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/604/#laws.0.5.0">1984 c 604 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.68.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/604/#laws.0.5.0">1984 c 604 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.68.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/271/#laws.1.3.0">2014 c 271 art 1 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.68.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/246/#laws.0.3.0">1979 c 246 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/604/#laws.0.5.0">1984 c 604 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.1.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.142.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 142</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/352/#laws.0.8.0">1989 c 352 s 8</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/541/#laws.0.11.0">1990 c 541 s 11</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.31.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.17.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.29.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.2.36.0">1998 c 386 art 2 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/163/#laws.0.53.0">2005 c 163 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.17.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.7.20.0">2016 c 189 art 7 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.682">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.682 SMALL BUSINESS ASSISTANCE PARTNERSHIPS PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.682.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms in this subdivision have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
          <p>(c) "Partner organizations" or "partners" means:</p>
          <p>(1) nonprofit organizations or public entities, including higher education institutions, engaged in business development or economic development;</p>
          <p>(2) community development financial institutions;</p>
          <p>(3) community development corporations; and</p>
          <p>(4) Tribal economic development entities.</p>
          <p>(d) "Small business" has the meaning given in section 3 of the Small Business Act, United States Code, title 15, section 632.</p>
          <p>(e) "Underserved populations and geographies" means individuals who are Black, Indigenous, people of color, veterans, people with disabilities, people who are LGBTQ+, and low-income individuals and includes people from rural Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.682.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish the small business assistance partnerships program to make grants to local and regional community-based organizations to provide small business development and technical assistance services to entrepreneurs and small business owners.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.682.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Small business assistance partnerships grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall make small business assistance partnerships grants to local and regional community-based organizations to provide small business development and technical assistance services to entrepreneurs and small business owners. The commissioner must prioritize applications that provide services to underserved populations and geographies.</p>
          <p>(b) Grantees shall use the grant funds to provide high-quality, free professional business development and technical assistance services that support the start-up, growth, and success of Minnesota's entrepreneurs and small business owners.</p>
          <p>(c) Grantees may use up to 15 percent of grant funds for expenses incurred while administering the grant, including but not limited to expenses related to technology, utilities, legal services, training, accounting, insurance, financial management, benefits, reporting, servicing of loans, and audits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.682.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 31 of each year, partner organizations participating in the program must provide a report to the commissioner on the outcomes of the program, including but not limited to the number of entrepreneurs and small businesses served, number of hours of business assistance services provided, number of new businesses started, number of full-time equivalent jobs created and retained, and demographic and geographic details of the individuals being served.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.682.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.15.9.0">2023 c 53 art 15 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/120/#laws.9.1.0">2024 c 120 art 9 s 1</a>,2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.69">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.69 UNIFORM BUSINESS LICENSING POLICY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.69.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Finding.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that a uniform policy on business licenses is necessary to maintain an adequate level of protection of the public welfare while preventing business licensing from becoming overly burdensome for the citizens and businesses of Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.69.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Policy.</span></h2>
          <p>It is the policy of the state of Minnesota that to the extent practicable, when required, a business license:</p>
          <p>(1) should be necessary to protect the safety, health or welfare of the citizens of the state or to ensure fair competition, competency in business, responsible financial practices, or other ethical business conduct;</p>
          <p>(2) should not duplicate or significantly overlap any other business license;</p>
          <p>(3) should be issued and renewed for the longest period possible consistent with the need to review eligibility and compliance with the terms and conditions of the license;</p>
          <p>(4) should contain a termination or renewal date determined by the agency to be as convenient as possible for the license holder consistent with clause (3). When an agency issues more than one license to the same business these licenses should have the same calendar renewal date; and</p>
          <p>(5) should involve payment of a fee in an amount no greater than specified by statute. If a fee is authorized by statute and set by rule, the fee shall be no greater than necessary to recover the administrative cost of issuing or renewing the license or enforcing its terms and conditions. The fees and conditions may be different for different classes of businesses and for initial issuance and subsequent renewals.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.69.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.2.2.0">1981 c 342 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.691"><b>116J.691</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116O.091">116O.091</a>]
</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.692"><b>116J.692</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116O.092">116O.092</a>]
</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.693"><b>116J.693</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.40.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 40</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.70">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.70 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.70.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.69">116J.69</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.71">116J.71</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.70.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Business license.</span></h2>
          <p>"Business license" or "license" means any permit, registration, certification, or other form of approval authorized by statute or rule to be issued by any agency or instrumentality of the state of Minnesota as a condition of doing business in Minnesota. The term also includes, when applicable, the substantive and procedural criteria governing the qualifications for, and issuance and maintenance of, a business license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.70.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/271/#laws.1.3.0">2014 c 271 art 1 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.70.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.2.3.0">1981 c 342 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/1/4/#laws.4.14.0">1Sp1981 c 4 art 4 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/413/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 413 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/70/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 70 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/216/#laws.1.23.0">1983 c 216 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.7.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 7</a>,58; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.12.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.8.28.0">1990 c 391 art 8 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/464/#laws.1.18.0">1992 c 464 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/507/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 507 s 1</a>;       <div class="section" id="stat.116J.71">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.71 NEW LICENSES.</h1>
        <p>Any new business license authorized by the legislature or established by rule after June 2, 1981, shall conform to the policy in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.69">116J.69</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.71.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.2.5.0">1981 c 342 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.72">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.72 EXISTING LICENSES.</h1>
        <p>Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.69">116J.69</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.71">116J.71</a> shall affect the validity of duration of an existing issued license.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.72.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.2.6.0">1981 c 342 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.23.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">BUREAU OF BUSINESS LICENSES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.73">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.73 BUREAU OF BUSINESS LICENSES; DECLARATION OF PURPOSE.</h1>
        <p>It is the intent of the legislature that a program of business license assistance be established to provide a centralized state government office to which business license applicants may obtain comprehensive license information and assistance. The program of business assistance will be directed to commercial business undertakings, projects, and activities rather than to the issuance of licenses for individual privileges, including the occupational licenses for practicing a trade or profession, licenses for operating a motor vehicle, and amateur sporting licenses, including, but not limited to, hunting and fishing.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.73.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.1.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.74">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.74 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.74.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Terms.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.73">116J.73</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.86">116J.86</a>, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.74.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.02#stat.14.02.2">14.02, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.74.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Applicant.</span></h2>
          <p>"Applicant" means a person acting personally or authorized to act on behalf of any other person for the purpose of securing a license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.74.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Bureau.</span></h2>
          <p>"Bureau" means the Bureau of Business Licenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.74.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Department of Employment and Economic Development.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.74.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Director.</span></h2>
          <p>"Director" means the director of the Bureau of Business Licenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.74.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Business license.</span></h2>
          <p>"Business license" or "license" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.70#stat.116J.70.2">116J.70, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.74.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/271/#laws.2.9.0">2014 c 271 art 2 s 9</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.74.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" means any individual, proprietorship, partnership, association, cooperative, corporation, nonprofit organization, state or local government agency, and any other organization required to obtain one or more licenses.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.74.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.2.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/289/#laws.0.115.0">1983 c 289 s 115</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.75"><b>116J.75</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/200/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 200 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.76">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.76 GENERAL FUNCTIONS; POWERS AND DUTIES.</h1>
        <p>The bureau, by and through the director or the director's duly authorized employees, shall have the following functions, powers, and duties:</p>
        <p>(1) providing comprehensive information on licenses required for business undertakings, projects, and activities in the state and making the information available to applicants and other persons;</p>
        <p>(2) providing interested persons with an opinion as to the number, kind, and source of required licenses and potential difficulties in obtaining the licenses, based on a review of a potential applicant's business concept at an early stage in its planning;</p>
        <p>(3) developing with the assistance of other departments a master application procedure to expedite the identification and processing of these licenses;</p>
        <p>(4) facilitating or recommending consolidation of hearings required pursuant to licensing applications when feasible and advantageous;</p>
        <p>(5) encouraging and facilitating the participation of federal and local government agencies in licensing coordination;</p>
        <p>(6) making recommendations for eliminating, consolidating, simplifying, expediting, or otherwise improving licensing procedures affecting business undertakings;</p>
        <p>(7) serving as an advocate for small business license applicants with state, federal, and local agencies in the process of applying for licenses and complying with licensing standards and requirements; and</p>
        <p>(8) adopting rules, procedures, instructions, and forms as are required to carry out the functions, powers, and duties imposed upon the bureau by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.73">116J.73</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.86">116J.86</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.76.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.4.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.247.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 247</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.77">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.77 ASSISTANCE OF OTHER AGENCIES.</h1>
        <p>To effect the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.73">116J.73</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.86">116J.86</a>, and when requested by the commissioner, an agency shall to the extent practicable provide assistance, services, facilities, and data as will enable the bureau to carry out its functions, powers, and duties.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.77.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.5.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.78">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.78 COMPREHENSIVE LICENSE INFORMATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.78.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reports by agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>Not later than 90 days from June 2, 1981, each agency issuing licenses for business undertakings, projects, and activities shall report to the bureau, in a form prescribed by the bureau, on each and every type of license administered or issued by the agency. Application forms, applicable agency rules, fee schedules, and the estimated time period necessary for license application consideration based on experience and statutory or regulatory requirements shall accompany each report. The reports shall be updated every two years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.78.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Report supplementation.</span></h2>
          <p>Each agency issuing licenses for business undertakings, projects, and activities shall, subsequent to its report pursuant to subdivision 1, provide the bureau with a report of any new license or modification of any existing license, or licensing procedures, together with applicable forms, rules, and information required under subdivisions 1 and 2 regarding new or modified licenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.78.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Preparation of information file.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau shall prepare an information file on agency license requirements upon receipt of the agency reports and shall develop methods for its maintenance, revision, updating, and ready access.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.78.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">License information provided by bureau.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau shall provide comprehensive license information on the basis of information submitted in subdivisions 1 to 3. The bureau may prepare and distribute publications, guides, and other materials based upon the agency reports and the information file. These materials are designed to serve the convenience of license applicants and explain license requirements affecting business, including requirements having multiple license or multiple agency aspects.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.78.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.6.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.79">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.79 PREAPPLICATION CONFERENCES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.79.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requests for conference.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau, at the request of any person, proceeding in accordance with this section, may conduct a preapplication conference, pending the formal submission of application forms, in which affected agencies shall participate to the extent practicable in order to clarify the nature and scope of their interest, to provide guidance regarding license application and review procedures, and to coordinate agency actions and data collection or submission regarding license application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.79.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Multiple licenses; agencies to provide review and opinion.</span></h2>
          <p>If, in the course of a preapplication conference, it becomes clear in the opinion of the director that a proposed business undertaking: (a) may require multiple licenses from the same or different state departments; (b) will take place in phases over an extended period of time; (c) will involve substantial expense for preparation of detailed plans, specifications and license applications; or (d) is of a new or unique nature, then each affected agency shall, at the request of the director to the extent practicable, provide the applicant with a written review and opinion as to all licenses which the agency would require for the proposed undertaking, the standards and conditions which would have to be met in order to obtain the licenses, timetables involved, and any properly related circumstances or findings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.79.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Written opinions; time limits; extensions.</span></h2>
          <p>Each agency participating in the review and opinion process shall render the written opinion within a period not exceeding 60 days from the date fixed by the director. This period may be extended by the director at the request of an interested agency for the further consideration of information provided in accordance with this section. The director shall advise the person having requested the review and opinion of the extension, the reasons for it, and the revised period fixed by the director for rendering the written opinion. The person shall be entitled to confer with the bureau and with any agency having been granted an extension of time to ascertain what further information, if any, is required to facilitate the rendering of the review and opinion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.79.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Effect of review and opinion procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>A preapplication review and opinion shall not relieve the person from the responsibility of obtaining any required licenses and shall be contingent upon the submission of all detailed plans, specifications, and information required for license applications. An agency's written opinion as to required licenses shall remain in effect indefinitely for the proposed business undertaking, project, or activity as described in the applicant's submission. However, if new license requirements or related standards over which an agency has no control or discretion in establishing subsequently become effective, the new license requirements or standards shall not be considered to have been part of the preapplication review and opinion. The opinion of the agency may be modified or amended by the agency at any time and shall not prohibit the agency from requiring additional licenses as deemed necessary for the applicant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.79.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Rules of procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau shall promulgate rules for the procedures to be followed in the conduct of preapplication reviews and opinions.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.79.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.7.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.80">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.80 MASTER APPLICATION PROCEDURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.80.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Development and implementation.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau shall develop and implement a master application procedure to expedite the identification and processing of licenses for business undertakings, projects, and activities. A master application shall be made on a form prescribed by the bureau. This form shall request concise and specific information necessary to determine those licenses which are or may be required for the undertaking, project, or activity in order to insure speedy issuance of the licenses when all necessary requirements are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.80.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Bureau assistance in preparing.</span></h2>
          <p>Use of the master application procedure shall be at the option of any person proposing a business undertaking, project, or activity. Upon request, the bureau shall assist any person in preparing a master application, describe the procedures involved, and provide other information from the comprehensive license information file as may be helpful or necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.80.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Receipt of application; notification to agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon receipt of a master application the bureau shall immediately notify in writing each agency having a possible interest in the proposed business undertaking, project, or activity with respect to licenses which are or may be required.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.80.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Agency response.</span></h2>
          <p>Each agency so notified shall respond to the bureau within 20 days of receipt of the notice and shall advise the bureau whether one or more licenses under its jurisdiction are or may be required for the business undertaking, project, or activity described in the master application. The response shall specify the licenses which in the opinion of the agency are or may be required, if any, and shall indicate the fees to be charged.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.80.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Consequences of negative or nonresponses.</span></h2>
          <p>Any agency so notified which responds that it does not have an interest in the license requirements of the business undertaking, project, or activity described in the master application, or which does not respond within the time period specified in subdivision 4, shall not require a license for the undertaking, project, or activity described in the master application. Except that where unusual circumstances have prevented an agency from notifying the bureau, and the agency establishes that failure to require a license would result in substantial harm to the public health or welfare, the commissioner may order that the license be required.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.80.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Failure to provide accurate or pertinent information.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of subdivision 5 shall not apply if the commissioner of employment and economic development determines that the master application contained false, misleading, or deceptive information, or failed to include pertinent information, the lack of which could reasonably lead an agency to misjudge the applicability of licenses under its jurisdiction, or if new license requirements or related standards subsequently became effective for which an agency had no discretion in establishing the effective date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.80.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Notification to applicant.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau, following the 20 day notice and response period, shall promptly provide the person having submitted a master application with application forms and related information for all licenses specified by the interested agencies and shall advise the person:</p>
          <p>(1) that all forms are to be completed and submitted to the interested agencies; and</p>
          <p>(2) at the option of the applicant, that the bureau will receive all forms as a package with the fees to be charged, if any, and that the bureau will immediately separate and submit the forms and any allocable fees to the appropriate agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.80.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Withdrawal of application.</span></h2>
          <p>An applicant may withdraw a master application at any time without forfeiture of any license approval applied for or obtained under the master application procedures contained in this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.80.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.8.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/289/#laws.0.115.0">1983 c 289 s 115</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.81">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.81 LICENSE COORDINATION AND ASSISTANCE TO APPLICANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.81.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>Any applicant for licenses required for a business undertaking, project, or activity may confer with the bureau to obtain assistance in the prompt and efficient processing and review of applications.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.81.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties of bureau.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau shall, so far as possible, render assistance; and the director may designate an officer or employee of the bureau to act as an expediter for the purpose of:</p>
          <p>(1) facilitating contacts for the applicant with agencies responsible for processing and reviewing license applications;</p>
          <p>(2) arranging conferences to clarify the interest and requirements of any agency with respect to license applications;</p>
          <p>(3) considering with agencies the feasibility of consolidating hearings and data required of the applicant; and</p>
          <p>(4) assisting the applicant in the resolution of outstanding issues identified by agencies, including delays experienced in license review.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.81.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.9.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.82">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.82 CONSOLIDATED HEARINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.82.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Bureau may request.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau may request the Office of Administrative Hearings to consolidate hearings insofar as it is feasible and agreeable to all parties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.82.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rules of procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>A consolidated hearing shall be conducted in a manner consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.001">14.001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>, and the applicable rules of the Office of Administrative Hearings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.82.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prehearing conference.</span></h2>
          <p>The Office of Administrative Hearings, with the consent of the agencies having license jurisdiction, may provide for a prehearing conference to assist in the disposition of the type, time, place, and parties of the consolidated hearing, the simplification of the issues, the stipulations as to agreed facts and necessary documents, and other relevant matters.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.82.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.10.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.1.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/422/#laws.0.10.0">1990 c 422 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.83">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.83 LICENSE AUTHORITY RETAINED.</h1>
        <p>Each agency having jurisdiction to approve or deny a license shall have the continuing power vested in it to make such determinations. The provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.73">116J.73</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.86">116J.86</a> shall not lessen or reduce these powers and shall modify the procedures followed in carrying out these powers only to the extent provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.73">116J.73</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.86">116J.86</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.83.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.11.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.84">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.84 SERVICES PROVIDED AT NO CHARGE.</h1>
        <p>Services rendered by the bureau shall be made available without charge. Nothing contained in this section shall relieve an applicant of any part of the fees or charges established for the review and approval of license applications or relieve an applicant of any of the apportioned costs of a consolidated hearing conducted under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.79">116J.79</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.80">116J.80</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.84.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.12.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.85">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.85 FEDERAL AND LOCAL GOVERNMENT PARTICIPATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.85.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Encouragement.</span></h2>
          <p>Federal and local government license agencies shall be encouraged to participate in the business license information, coordination, and assistance services of the bureau and to make information available to applicants through the bureau with respect to any business undertaking, project, or activity which is referred to the bureau under the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.73">116J.73</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.86">116J.86</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.85.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Assistance to federal and local agency license applicants.</span></h2>
          <p>The bureau shall, so far as is practicable, advise applicants of federal and local agency license requirements and shall maintain an information file on licenses for which the state has delegated issuance authority to local government agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.85.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Coordination of license review procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>The director shall consult with local government officials with respect to cooperation in coordinating state and local license application and review procedures and shall recommend to the governor and the legislature any actions which would facilitate this coordination.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.85.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.13.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.86">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.86 COMPILATION AND MAINTENANCE OF STATISTICAL DATA.</h1>
        <p>The bureau shall obtain and keep on an annual basis appropriate statistical data regarding the number of licenses issued by agencies, the amount of time necessary for the licenses to be issued, the cost of obtaining the licenses, the types of projects for which specific licenses are issued, a geographic distribution of licenses issued, and other pertinent data which the director deems appropriate. The bureau shall analyze the data by type of license and by agency responsible and shall make its findings available to the public.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.86.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.3.14.0">1981 c 342 art 3 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.87"><b>116J.87</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.191.0">1987 c 404 s 191</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.871">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.871 FINANCIAL ASSISTANCE LIMITATIONS; PREVAILING WAGE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.871.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Economic development" means financial assistance provided to a person directly or to a local unit of government or nonprofit organization on behalf of a person who is engaged in the manufacture or sale of goods and services. Economic development does not include (1) financial assistance for rehabilitation of existing housing; (2) financial assistance for new housing construction in which total financial assistance at a single project site is less than $100,000; or (3) financial assistance for the new construction of fully detached single-family affordable homeownership units for which the financial assistance covers no more than ten fully detached single-family affordable homeownership units. For purposes of this paragraph, "affordable homeownership" means housing targeted at households with incomes, at initial occupancy, at or below 115 percent of the state or area median income, whichever is greater, as determined by the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development.</p>
          <p>(c) "Financial assistance" means (1) a grant awarded by a state agency or allocating agency for economic development related purposes if a single business receives $200,000 or more of the grant proceeds; (2) a loan or the guaranty or purchase of a loan made by a state agency or allocating agency for economic development related purposes if a single business receives $500,000 or more of the loan proceeds; or (3) a reduction, credit, or abatement of a tax assessed under chapter 297A where the tax reduction, credit, or abatement applies to a geographic area smaller than the entire state and was granted for economic development related purposes; or (4) allocations or awards of low-income housing credits by all allocating agencies as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462A.222">462A.222</a>, for which tax credits are used for multifamily housing projects consisting of more than ten units. Financial assistance does not include payments by the state of aids and credits under chapter 273 or 477A to a political subdivision.</p>
          <p>(d) "Project site" means the location where improvements are made that are financed in whole or in part by the financial assistance; or the location of employees that receive financial assistance in the form of employment and training services as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116L.19#stat.116L.19.4">116L.19, subdivision 4</a>, or customized training from a technical college.</p>
          <p>(e) "State agency" means any agency defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.01#stat.16B.01.2">16B.01, subdivision 2</a>, Enterprise Minnesota, Inc., and the Department of Iron Range Resources and Rehabilitation.</p>
          <p>(f) "Allocating agency" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462A.221#stat.462A.221.1a">462A.221, subdivision 1a</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.871.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prevailing wage required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A state agency or allocating agency may provide financial assistance to a person only if the person receiving or benefiting from the financial assistance certifies to the commissioner of labor and industry that laborers and mechanics at the project site during construction, installation, remodeling, and repairs for which the financial assistance was provided will be paid the prevailing wage rate as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.42#stat.177.42.6">177.42, subdivision 6</a>. The person receiving or benefiting from the financial assistance is also subject to the requirements and enforcement provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.27">177.27</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.30">177.30</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.32">177.32</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.41">177.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.435">177.435</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.45">177.45</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of complying with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.30">177.30</a>, paragraph (a), clauses (6) and (7), the state agency or allocating agency awarding the financial assistance is considered the contracting authority and the project is considered a public works project. The person receiving or benefiting from the financial assistance shall notify all employers on the project of the record keeping and reporting requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/177.30">177.30</a>, paragraph (a), clauses (6) and (7). Each employer shall submit the required information to the contracting authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.871.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prevailing wage; penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a misdemeanor for a person who has certified that prevailing wages will be paid to laborers and mechanics under subdivision 2 to subsequently fail to pay the prevailing wage. Each day a violation of this subdivision continues is a separate offense.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.871.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Notification.</span></h2>
          <p>A state agency or allocating agency shall notify any person applying for financial assistance from the state agency or allocating agency of the requirements under subdivision 2 and of the penalties under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.871.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Exception.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section denies any financial assistance granted to or qualified for by a person whose construction, installation, remodeling, or repairs commenced prior to August 1, 1990.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.871.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.7.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/322/#laws.0.19.0">1991 c 322 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.72.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 72</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.3.5.0">2005 c 10 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/290/#laws.0.2.0">2008 c 290 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.1.1.0">2023 c 53 art 1 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/85/#laws.0.15.0">2024 c 85 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.9.1.0">2024 c 127 art 9 s 1</a>-3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.872">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.872 ECONOMIC RESPONSE TEAM.</h1>
        <p>(a) The department shall operate a fast-action economic response team to contact and work with businesses that are identified as being:</p>
        <p>(1) at risk for relocating or expanding outside the state; or</p>
        <p>(2) prospects for expansion or relocation within the state.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) The fast-action response team must contact identified businesses within 24 hours.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.872.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/347/#laws.1.2.0">2010 c 347 art 1 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.873"><b>116J.873</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/452/#laws.0.40.0">1996 c 452 s 40</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8731">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8731 MINNESOTA INVESTMENT FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8731.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota investment fund is created to provide financial and technical assistance, through partnership with communities, for the creation of new employment or to maintain existing employment, and for business start-up, expansions, and retention. It shall accomplish these goals by the following means:</p>
          <p>(1) creation or retention of permanent private-sector jobs in order to create above-average economic growth consistent with environmental protection, which includes investments in technology and equipment that increase productivity and provide for a higher wage;</p>
          <p>(2) stimulation or leverage of private investment to ensure economic renewal and competitiveness;</p>
          <p>(3) increasing the local tax base, based on demonstrated measurable outcomes, to guarantee a diversified industry mix;</p>
          <p>(4) improving the quality of existing jobs, based on increases in wages or improvements in the job duties, training, or education associated with those jobs;</p>
          <p>(5) improvement of employment and economic opportunity for citizens in the region to create a reasonable standard of living, consistent with federal and state guidelines on low- to moderate-income persons; and</p>
          <p>(6) stimulation of productivity growth through improved manufacturing or new technologies, including cold weather testing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8731.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Administration. </span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as otherwise provided in this section, the commissioner shall administer the fund as part of the Small Cities Development Block Grant Program and funds shall be made available to local communities and recognized Indian tribal governments in accordance with the rules adopted for economic development grants in the small cities community development block grant program. All units of general purpose local government are eligible applicants for Minnesota investment funds. The commissioner may provide forgivable loans directly to a private enterprise and not require a local community or recognized Indian tribal government application other than a resolution supporting the assistance.</p>
          <p>(b) Eligible applicants for the state-funded portion of the fund also include development authorities as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.552#stat.116J.552.4">116J.552, subdivision 4</a>, provided that the governing body of the municipality approves, by resolution, the application of the development authority. A local government entity may receive more than one award in a fiscal year. The commissioner may also make funds available within the department for eligible expenditures under subdivision 3, clause (2).</p>
