          <p>(ii) enter into appropriate contracts and agreements with community development venture capital funds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8762.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Interests of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>Except upon the exercise of the commissioner's right of subrogation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8767">116J.8767</a>, the commissioner has no legal or equitable interest in any collateral, security, or other right of recovery in connection with any loan enrolled in the program, and the commissioner's consent is not necessary for any amendment to the lender's loan documents.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8762.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.148.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 148</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/153/#laws.0.3.0">2001 c 153 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8763">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8763 ELIGIBLE LOANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8763.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Loan types.</span></h2>
          <p>Eligible loans may include:</p>
          <p>(1) loans made for industrial, commercial, or agricultural purposes;</p>
          <p>(2) refinancing of loans made for the purposes in clause (1); and</p>
          <p>(3) lines of credit agreements established between the lender and borrower which are used for the purposes in clause (1).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8763.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Loan restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p>Eligible loans must meet the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the lender has not made the loan in order to enroll in the program prior debt which is not covered under the program and which is or was owed by the borrower to the lender;</p>
          <p>(2) the proceeds of the loan will not be used for that portion of a project or development devoted to housing;</p>
          <p>(3) the proceeds of the loan will not be used to finance passive real estate ownership; and</p>
          <p>(4) the proceeds of the loan will be used to finance a project or enterprise located within this state which will foster economic development in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8763.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Loan provisions.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible loan may provide for an interest rate, fees, and other terms and conditions as the lender and borrower may agree. If the loan amount to be borrowed is determined by a commitment agreement that establishes a line of credit, the amount of the loan is the maximum amount available to the borrower under the agreement.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8763.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.149.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 149</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8764">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8764 ENROLLMENT OF LOANS IN PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8764.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Filing requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To enroll a loan under this program, the lender must file a completed loan enrollment form with the commissioner. The lender must also certify the following to the commissioner as part of the filing:</p>
          <p>(1) the lender has no substantial reason to believe that the loan is being made to a borrower who does not meet the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.876#stat.116J.876.3">116J.876, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) that the lender has received from the borrower a written representation, warranty, pledge, and waiver stating that the borrower has no legal, beneficial, or equitable interest in the nonrefundable premium charges or any other funds credited to the reserve fund established to cover losses sustained by the lender on enrolled loans;</p>
          <p>(3) the loan being filed for enrollment is an eligible loan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8763">116J.8763</a>; and</p>
          <p>(4) premium changes required of the borrower and lender under this section have been deposited in the reserve fund.</p>
          <p>(b) The lender shall file the loan enrollment form within ten business days after the lender makes the loan. The date on which the lender makes a loan is the date on which the lender first disburses proceeds of the loan to the borrower or an earlier date on which the loan documents have been executed and the lender has obligated itself to disburse proceeds of the loan. The filing date of a loan enrollment form is the date on which the lender delivers the required documentation to the commissioner, delivers it to a professional courier service for delivery to the commissioner, or mails it to the commissioner by certified mail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8764.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner enrollment; acknowledgment.</span></h2>
          <p>When the commissioner receives the loan enrollment form, the commissioner shall enroll the loan, unless the information provided under subdivision 1 indicates that the loan is not an eligible loan, and shall deliver to the lender within five business days of receipt an acknowledgment of enrollment, signed by the commissioner or designee, including documentation of the amount being transferred by the commissioner into the reserve fund under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8764.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Enrollment of loans without commissioner's full premium payment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may continue to accept loans for enrollment into the program even if the amount of funds contained in the account is zero or an amount less than the full amount that is required to be transferred under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8765#stat.116J.8765.2">116J.8765, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a), (b), or (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8764.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Amount covered.</span></h2>
          <p>When filing a loan enrollment form, the lender may specify an amount to be covered under the program. The amount may be less than the total amount of the loan. Unless the context clearly requires otherwise, when used in connection with a loan or loans, the words "amount" and "proceeds" refer only to the amount covered under the agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8764.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Amount covered in refinancings.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In the case of a loan to refinance a loan previously made to the borrower by the lender that was not enrolled under the program, the lender may obtain coverage under the program for an amount not exceeding the amount of additional financing.</p>
          <p>(b) If an enrolled loan is refinanced and the total amount to be covered under the program does not exceed the covered amount of the loan as previously enrolled, the refinanced loan may continue as an enrolled loan without payment of additional premium charges or transfers by the commissioner to the reserve fund.</p>
          <p>(c) If an enrolled loan is refinanced in an amount exceeding the amount of the loan as previously enrolled, the lender may obtain coverage of the amount of the refinanced loan that exceeds the amount covered when the loan was previously enrolled by refiling the loan for enrollment under subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(d) Fluctuations in the outstanding balance of a line of credit, without increasing the enrolled amount under the program, are not a refinancing of the loan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8764.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Termination of enrollment.</span></h2>
          <p>If the outstanding balance of an enrolled loan which is not a line of credit is reduced to zero, the loan is no longer an enrolled loan. If an enrolled loan which is a line of credit has an outstanding balance of zero for a 12-month period, the line of credit is no longer an enrolled loan, unless, before the expiration of the 12-month period, the lender reaffirms in writing to the borrower that the line of credit will remain open and the borrower acknowledges the reaffirmation in writing.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8764.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.150.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 150</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.21.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8765">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8765 RESERVE FUND; PREMIUMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8765.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon execution of an agreement between the lender and the commissioner, the commissioner shall establish a reserve fund account with the lender in the name of the commissioner for the purpose of receiving all required premium charges to be paid by the lender and the borrower and transfers made by the commissioner under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.876">116J.876</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8769">116J.8769</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8765.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Premium payments and transfers to reserve fund.</span></h2>
          <p>The premium charges payable to the reserve fund by the lender and the borrower in connection with a loan filed for enrollment are determined by the lender. The premium paid by the borrower may not be less than 1.5 percent nor greater than 3.5 percent of the amount of the loan. The premium paid by the lender shall be equal to the amount of the premium paid by the borrower. The lender may recover from the borrower the cost of the lender's premium payment, in any manner in which the lender and borrower agree. When enrolling a loan, the commissioner shall transfer into the reserve fund from the account premium amounts determined as follows:</p>
          <p>(a) If the amount of any loan, plus the amount of loans previously enrolled by the lender, is less than $2,000,000, the premium amount transferred must be equal to 150 percent of the combined premiums paid into the reserve fund by the borrower and the lender for each enrolled loan.</p>
          <p>(b) If, prior to the enrollment of the loan, the amount of loans previously enrolled by the lender equals or exceeds $2,000,000, the premium amount transferred must be equal to the combined premiums paid into the reserve fund by the borrower and the lender for each enrolled loan.</p>
          <p>(c) If the amount of loans previously enrolled by the lender is less than $2,000,000, but the enrollment of a loan will cause the aggregate amount of all enrolled loans made by the lender to exceed $2,000,000, the premium amount transferred must be equal to a percentage of the combined amount paid by the lender and the borrower. The percentage must be determined by (1) multiplying by 150 that portion of the loan which when added to the amount of all previously enrolled loans totals $2,000,000, (2) multiplying the balance of the loan by 100, and (3) adding the products of the two amounts and dividing the sum by the total amount of the loan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8765.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Limitation of transfers.</span></h2>
          <p>A maximum premium amount of $150,000 may be transferred into the reserve funds of all lenders participating in the program by the commissioner over any three-year period in connection with any one borrower or any group of borrowers among which a common enterprise exists. This maximum premium amount may be exceeded upon the written request by a lender only if the commissioner approves in writing the transfer of an amount in excess of $150,000. For the purpose of this subdivision, the term "common enterprise" has the meaning given it in Code of Federal Regulations, title 12, section 32, as amended.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8765.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Control and investment of reserve fund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All money credited to the reserve fund is under the exclusive control of the commissioner. The commissioner may not withdraw money from the reserve fund except as specifically provided in this subdivision and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8766">116J.8766</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8768">116J.8768</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Money in the reserve fund must be deposited by the commissioner in an account with the lender unless the commissioner determines that the lender is not in substantial compliance with the requirements of the agreement. If money in the reserve fund is not deposited by the commissioner in an account with the lender, it must be invested or reinvested by the commissioner in (1) direct obligations of the United States or the state of Minnesota or in obligations the principal and interest of which are unconditionally guaranteed by the United States or the state of Minnesota, or (2) a deposit account at a depository institution whose deposits are insured by the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation or the Federal Savings and Loan Insurance Corporation.</p>
          <p>(c) Interest or income earned on the money credited to the reserve fund is part of the reserve fund. The commissioner may withdraw at any time from the reserve fund 50 percent of all interest or income that has been credited to the reserve fund, except that after the first withdrawal the commissioner may not withdraw more than 50 percent of all interest or income that has been credited to the reserve fund since the time of the last withdrawal. Any withdrawal made under this subdivision may be made prior to paying any claim. None of the amounts withdrawn need to be transferred back to the reserve fund. Any withdrawal under this subdivision must be credited in the capital access account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8765.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Pledge of the reserve fund.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall pledge to the lender that the money in the reserve fund will be available to pay claims under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8766">116J.8766</a>, that the lender will have a first security interest in the money in the reserve fund to pay the claims, and that the commissioner will not encumber or pledge the money to any other party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8765.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Quarterly reports; inspections.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the reserve fund is not maintained with the lender, the commissioner shall provide to the lender quarterly transaction reports indicating the balance in the reserve fund, payments and transfers into the reserve fund, withdrawals from the reserve fund, and interest or income earned on money credited to the reserve fund.</p>
          <p>(b) The records of the commissioner with respect to all payments and transfers into the reserve fund, withdrawals from the reserve fund, and interest or income earned on the money credited to the reserve fund, are available to the lender at the offices of the commissioner during normal business hours.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8765.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.151.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 151</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8766">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8766 CLAIMS BY LENDER TO RESERVE FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8766.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Claim process.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the lender charges off all or part of an enrolled loan, the lender may file a claim with the commissioner. The claim must be filed contemporaneously with the charge-off.</p>
          <p>(b) The lender's claim may include, in addition to the amount of principal charged off plus accrued interest, one-half of the documented out-of-pocket expenses incurred in pursuing its collection efforts, including preservation of collateral. The amount of principal and accrued interest included in the claim may not exceed the principal amount covered under the program upon enrollment, plus accrued interest attributable to the covered principal amount.</p>
          <p>(c) The lender shall determine when and how much to charge off on an enrolled loan in a manner consistent with its normal method for making these determinations on similar loans which are not enrolled loans.</p>
          <p>(d) If the lender files two or more claims contemporaneously and there are insufficient funds in its reserve fund at that time to cover the entire amount of the claims, the lender may designate the order of priority in which the commissioner shall pay the claims.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8766.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disbursement of reserve fund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Upon receipt by the commissioner of a claim filed by the lender, the commissioner shall, within ten business days, pay or authorize the lender to withdraw from the reserve fund the amount of the claim as submitted, unless the information provided by the lender was known by the lender to be false at the time the loan was filed for enrollment. No other violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.876">116J.876</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8769">116J.8769</a> or the agreement is grounds for denial of a claim. All money transferred or credited to the reserve fund from any source is appropriated to the commissioner to pay claims under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) If there is insufficient money in the reserve fund to cover the entire amount of the lender's claim, the commissioner shall pay to the lender or authorize the lender to withdraw an amount equal to the current balance in the reserve fund and the following shall apply:</p>
          <p>(1) If the enrolled loan for which the claim has been filed is not an early loan, the payment fully satisfies the claim, and the lender has no right to receive any further amount from the reserve fund with respect to that claim.</p>
          <p>(2) If the loan is an early loan, the partial payment does not satisfy the lender's claim, and at any time that the remaining balance of the claim is not greater than 75 percent of the balance in the reserve fund at the time of the loss, the commissioner, upon request of the lender, shall pay the remaining balance of the claim.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8766.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Recovery by lender subsequent to claim.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If, subsequent to payment of a claim by the commissioner, the lender recovers from a borrower any amount for which payment of the claim was made, the lender shall promptly pay to the commissioner for deposit in the reserve fund the amount recovered, less one-half of any documented out-of-pocket expenses incurred. The lender need pay to the commissioner for deposit in the reserve fund only amounts in excess of the amount of recovery needed to fully cover the lender's loss on an enrolled loan.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of this subdivision and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8767">116J.8767</a>, the lender's loss on an enrolled loan includes any losses on the loan including principal, accrued interest, and one-half of the documented out-of-pocket expenses attributable to principal amounts in excess of the amount covered under the program or the principal amount included in the claim.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8766.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Technical assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>When a borrower becomes 60 days delinquent in the payments of an enrolled loan or before a lender files a claim with the commissioner, the lender must notify the commissioner of the delinquency. The commissioner, after notification, shall inform the borrower of the technical assistance providers in the borrower's area that may assist in solving any business or management problems experienced by the borrower.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8766.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.152.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 152</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/423/#laws.0.4.0">1990 c 423 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.1.72.0">1991 c 345 art 1 s 72</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8767">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8767 SUBROGATION OF CLAIMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8767.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may exercise the right of subrogation under this section if the commissioner determines, in the commissioner's discretion, that the lender has not exercised reasonable care and diligence in its collection activities with respect to the loan or that there is a reasonable basis for believing that the lender will not exercise reasonable care and diligence in the future with respect to the collection activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8767.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Assignment of rights.</span></h2>
          <p>If the payment of a claim has fully covered the lender's loss on an enrolled loan, or if the payment of a claim when combined with any recovery from the borrower has fully covered the lender's loss, the commissioner, upon request, is subrogated to the rights of the lender with respect to any collateral, security, or other right of recovery in connection with the loan that has not been realized by the lender. The lender thereafter shall assign to the commissioner any right, title, or interest to any collateral, security, or other right of recovery in connection with the loan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8767.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Lender obligations.</span></h2>
          <p>If an assignment has been made, the commissioner is not required to undertake any obligations of the lender under its loan documents, except for any obligations directly related to the commissioner's assigned rights of recovery in connection with the loan. The lender shall fulfill any other obligations it may have under the loan documents in the same manner and to the same degree as required had the assignment not been made. The lender shall provide the commissioner with all reasonable assistance the commissioner requests in proceeding with respect to any collateral, security, or other right of recovery, except that the lender need not incur any out-of-pocket expenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8767.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Payment of lender's loss.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner decides to exercise the right of subrogation in connection with an enrolled loan and would be entitled to exercise the right except for the fact that the lender's loss has not been fully covered, the commissioner may pay from money in the reserve fund an amount sufficient to fully cover the lender's loss even though the payment may cover a principal amount not covered under the program or not included in the lender's claim. Upon making the payment, the commissioner is subrogated to the rights of the lender.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8767.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Recovered funds.</span></h2>
          <p>Any money received by the commissioner as a result of enforcement actions taken with respect to any collateral, security, or other rights of recovery must be promptly deposited by the commissioner in the reserve fund, less any out-of-pocket expenses incurred by the commissioner in taking such enforcement actions.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8767.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.153.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 153</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8768">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8768 EXCESS RESERVE FUNDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8768.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The lender shall file quarterly reports with the commissioner indicating the number and aggregate outstanding balance of all enrolled loans as of the end of each quarter. A quarterly report is not required for any quarter that ends with a balance in the reserve fund of zero, except that a calendar year-end report must be filed. In computing the aggregate outstanding balance of all enrolled loans, the balance of any loan may not be greater than the covered amount of the loan as enrolled.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.8768.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Withdrawal of excess reserve funds.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If reports filed under this section indicate that for the immediately preceding 24-month period the balance in the reserve fund continually exceeded the aggregate outstanding balance of all enrolled loans, the commissioner may withdraw from the reserve fund, on or before the last day of the month for which a report is due, an amount not greater than the amount by which the reserve fund balance exceeded the aggregate outstanding balance of all enrolled loans as of the most recent report, unless the lender has provided to the commissioner adequate documentation that at some time during that 24-month period the aggregate outstanding balance of all enrolled loans exceeded the balance then in the reserve fund. Any amounts withdrawn from the reserve fund must be transferred to the account.</p>
          <p>(b) If a report is not filed within 30 days of its original due date, the commissioner may withdraw from the reserve fund based on the commissioner's determination from an inspection of the lender's files an amount not greater than the amount by which the reserve fund balance exceeded the aggregate outstanding balance of all enrolled loans as of the date for which the report was required to be filed.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8768.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.154.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 154</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8769">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8769 TERMINATION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may terminate the obligation to a lender to enroll loans under the program if the commissioner determines that the lender is not in substantial compliance with the requirements of the program. The termination takes effect on the date specified in the notice of termination, except that the termination does not apply to any loan made on or before the date on which the notice of termination is received by the lender. If the commissioner is terminating the enrollment of loans for all participating lenders under the program, the commissioner shall provide notice of at least 90 days to the lender. Any terminations under this section are prospective only and do not apply to any loans previously refinanced. After termination, the amount covered under the program may not be increased beyond the covered amount as previously enrolled.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8769.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.155.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 155</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8770">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8770 EQUITY INVESTMENTS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may invest funds from the capital access account to make equity investments in community development venture capital funds for the purpose of providing capital for small and emerging businesses. The community development venture capital fund must have experience in equity investments with small businesses and the ability to raise private capital.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8770.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/153/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 153 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.8771">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.8771 WAIVER.</h1>
        <p>The capital access program is exempt from section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.05#stat.16C.05.2">16C.05, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.8771.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/153/#laws.0.5.0">2001 c 153 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.64.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 64</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.116J.88">
        <h1>116J.88 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.88.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.03#stat.116M.03.1">116M.03, subdivision 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.88.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.03">116M.03, subd 2</a>]</p>

      <h1 class="fgh">SMALL BUSINESS LOAN GUARANTEES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.881">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.881 SMALL BUSINESS LOAN GUARANTEE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.881.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Borrower" means a small business receiving an eligible loan under this section.</p>
          <p>(c) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
          <p>(d) "Eligible loan" means a loan to a small business to be used for business purposes exclusively in Minnesota, including: machinery or equipment purchases, maintenance, or repair; expenses related to moving into or within Minnesota; and working capital when the working capital is secured by fixed assets when possible.</p>
          <p>(e) "Loan guarantee" means a guarantee of 80 percent of the loan amount provided by a QED lender. The guaranteed portion of the loan must not exceed $200,000 and may be subordinate to other loans made by lenders in the overall financing package.</p>
          <p>(f) "Loan guarantee trust fund" means a dedicated fund established under this section for the purpose of compensation for defaulted loan guarantees and for program administration.</p>
          <p>(g) "Peacetime public health emergency" means any peacetime emergency declared by the governor in an executive order that is related to the infectious disease known as COVID-19.</p>
          <p>(h) "Qualified economic development lender" or "QED lender" means a bank or other commercial lender, a public entity, or a private nonprofit economic development organization with not less than three years of active lending experience that provides financing to small businesses.</p>
          <p>(i) "Small business" means a business employing no more than the equivalent of 250 full-time persons in Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(j) "Subordinated loan" means a loan secured by a lien that is lower in priority than one or more specified other liens.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.881.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Loan guarantee program.</span></h2>
          <p>A small business loan guarantee program to support the origination of small business loans that are expected to be made to Minnesota businesses by a QED lender, within the 12 months after a declaration of a peacetime public health emergency and not otherwise financially supported by a public entity, is created in the Department of Employment and Economic Development. The loan guarantee shall apply only to the portion of the loan that was made by the QED lender.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.881.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Required provisions.</span></h2>
          <p>Loan guarantees under this section for loans by QED lenders shall provide that:</p>
          <p>(1) principal and interest payments made by the borrower under the terms of the loan are to reduce the guaranteed and nonguaranteed portion of the loan on a proportionate basis. The nonguaranteed portion shall not receive preferential treatment over the guaranteed portion;</p>
          <p>(2) the QED lender shall not accelerate repayment of the loan or exercise other remedies if the borrower defaults, unless:</p>
          <p>(i) the borrower fails to make a required payment of principal or interest within 60 days of the due date;</p>
          <p>(ii) the commissioner consents in writing; or</p>
          <p>(iii) the loan guarantee agreement provides for accelerated repayment or other remedies.</p>
          <p class="bl">In the event of a default, the QED lender may not make a demand for payment pursuant to the guarantee unless the commissioner agrees in writing that the default has materially affected the rights or security of the parties;</p>
          <p>(3) the QED lender has timely prepared and delivered to the commissioner, annually by the date specified in the loan guarantee, an audited or reviewed financial statement for the loan, prepared by a certified public accountant according to generally accepted accounting principles, if available, and documentation that the borrower used the loan proceeds solely for purposes of its Minnesota operations;</p>
          <p>(4) the commissioner has access to the loan documents prior to approval of the state credit enhancement;</p>
          <p>(5) the QED lender maintains adequate records and documents concerning the loan so that the commissioner may determine the borrower's financial condition and compliance with program requirements; and</p>
          <p>(6) orderly liquidation of collateral securing the loan is provided for in the event of default, with an option on the part of the commissioner to acquire the QED lender's interest in the assets pursuant to the loan guarantee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.881.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Loan guarantee trust fund established.</span></h2>
          <p>A loan guarantee trust fund account in the special revenue fund is created in the state treasury to pay for defaulted loan guarantees. The commissioner shall administer this fund and provide annual reports concerning the performance of the fund to the chairs of the standing committees of the house of representatives and senate having jurisdiction over economic development issues.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.881.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>At no time shall total outstanding loan guarantees exceed five times the amount on deposit in the loan guarantee trust fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.881.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Guarantee fee.</span></h2>
          <p>Participating QED lenders shall pay a fee to the fund of 0.25 percent of the principal amount of each guaranteed loan upon approval of each loan guarantee. The guarantee fee, along with any interest earnings from the trust fund, shall be used only for the administration of the small business loan guarantee program and as additional loan loss reserves.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.881.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Loan guarantee application.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prepare a form for QED lenders to use in applying for loan guarantees under this section. The form shall include the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) the name and contact information for the QED lender, including the name and title of a contact person;</p>
          <p>(2) the names of the financial institutions, including the names and titles of contact persons, that are participating in the total financing being provided to the small business borrower, along with the dollar amount of the loan provided by the financial institution;</p>
          <p>(3) the percentage and dollar amount of the subordinated debt loan provided to the Minnesota small business by the QED lender;</p>
          <p>(4) the loan guarantee amount that is requested from the program; and</p>
          <p>(5) other information as requested by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.881.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Notice and application process.</span></h2>
          <p>Subject to the availability of funds under subdivision 4, the commissioner shall publish a notice regarding the opportunity for QED lenders to originate loans. The commissioner shall decide whether to provide a loan guarantee for each loan based on:</p>
          <p>(1) the completeness of the loan guarantee application;</p>
          <p>(2) the availability of funds in the loan guarantee trust fund; and</p>
          <p>(3) execution of agreements that satisfy requirements established in subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.881.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>By December 31, 2020, the commissioner shall report to the legislative committees with jurisdiction over economic development policy and finance on the loan guarantees provided under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.881.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/4/#laws.2.3.0">1Sp2011 c 4 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/71/#laws.2.3.0">2020 c 71 art 2 s 3</a></p>
      </div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.926"><b>116J.926</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.13">116M.13</a>]

      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.94"><b>116J.94</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/314/#laws.0.5.0">1987 c 314 s 5</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.941"><b>116J.941</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/316/#laws.0.4.0">1987 c 316 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.270.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 270</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.942"><b>116J.942</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/316/#laws.0.4.0">1987 c 316 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.270.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 270</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.951"><b>116J.951</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.1.13.0">1987 c 386 art 1 s 13</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">RURAL REHABILITATION REVOLVING ACCOUNT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.955">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.955 RURAL REHABILITATION REVOLVING ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.955.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The rural rehabilitation account is in the special revenue fund. The money transferred to the state as a result of liquidating the Rural Rehabilitation Corporation Trust, and money derived from transfer of the trust to the state, must be credited to the rural rehabilitation account. The principal amount of the rural rehabilitation account must be invested by the state investment board. The income attributable to investment of the principal is appropriated to the commissioner for the purposes of Laws 1987, chapter 386, article 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.955.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Expenditure of account.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may use the rural rehabilitation account for the purposes that are allowed under the Minnesota Rural Rehabilitation Corporation's charter and agreement, as may be amended or modified by, the United States Secretary of Agriculture as provided in Public Law 499, 81st Congress, enacted May 3, 1950 and as allowed under Laws 1987, chapter 386, article 1. Not more than three percent of the combined book value of the Minnesota rural rehabilitation account and the regional revolving funds may be used for administrative purposes in a year without approval of the United States Secretary of Agriculture. Any funds used for administrative purposes may only be drawn from money remaining in the Minnesota rural rehabilitation account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.955.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Transfer of authorized records to commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The authority, assets, books, and records held by the Minnesota Rural Rehabilitation Corporation and later by the state Executive Council under Public Law 499, 81st Congress, May 3, 1950, is transferred to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.955.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/254/#laws.0.2.0">1985 c 254 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.1.1.0">1987 c 386 art 1 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.49.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 49</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.22.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.961"><b>116J.961</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.1.13.0">1987 c 386 art 1 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.965"><b>116J.965</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.1.13.0">1987 c 386 art 1 s 13</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">TRADE PROMOTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.966">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.966 COMMISSIONER'S TRADE PROMOTION DUTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.966.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall promote, develop, and facilitate trade and foreign investment in Minnesota. In furtherance of these goals, and in addition to the powers granted by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.035">116J.035</a>, the commissioner may:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) locate, develop, and promote international markets for Minnesota products and services;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) arrange and lead trade missions to countries with promising international markets for Minnesota goods, technology, services, and agricultural products;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) promote Minnesota products and services at domestic and international trade shows;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) organize, promote, and present domestic and international trade shows featuring Minnesota products and services;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) host trade delegations and assist foreign traders in contacting appropriate Minnesota businesses and investments;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) develop contacts with Minnesota businesses and gather and provide information to assist them in locating and communicating with international trading or joint venture counterparts;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) provide information, education, and counseling services to Minnesota businesses regarding the economic, commercial, legal, and cultural contexts of international trade;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) provide Minnesota businesses with international trade leads and information about the availability and sources of services relating to international trade, such as export financing, licensing, freight forwarding, international advertising, translation, and custom brokering;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) locate, attract, and promote foreign direct investment and business development in Minnesota to enhance employment opportunities in Minnesota;</p>
          <p class="in">(10) provide foreign businesses and investors desiring to locate facilities in Minnesota information regarding sources of governmental, legal, real estate, financial, and business services;</p>
          <p class="in">(11) enter into contracts or other agreements with private persons and public entities, including agreements to establish and maintain offices and other types of representation in foreign countries, to carry out the purposes of promoting international trade and attracting investment from foreign countries to Minnesota and to carry out this section, without regard to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.06">16C.06</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(12) market trade-related materials to businesses and organizations, and the proceeds of which must be placed in a special revolving account and are appropriated to the commissioner to prepare and distribute trade-related materials.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The programs and activities of the commissioner of employment and economic development and the Minnesota Trade Division may not duplicate programs and activities of the commissioner of agriculture.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The commissioner shall notify the chairs of the senate Finance and house of representatives Ways and Means Committees of each agreement under this subdivision to establish and maintain an office or other type of representation in a foreign country.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The Minnesota Trade Office shall serve as the state's office of protocol providing assistance to official visits by foreign government representatives and shall serve as liaison to the foreign diplomatic corps in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.966.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agricultural promotion.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of agriculture and the commissioner of employment and economic development shall cooperate with each other to promote the beneficial agricultural interests of the state. The commissioner of agriculture has primary responsibility for promoting state agricultural interests to international markets. The commissioner of agriculture is also responsible for the promotion of national trade programs related to international marketing. The commissioner of agriculture has primary responsibility for promoting the agriculture interests of producers, promoting state agricultural markets, and promoting agricultural interests of the state in cooperative production and marketing efforts with other states and the United States Department of Agriculture. The commissioner of agriculture is also responsible for promoting the national and international marketing of state agricultural products.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.966.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.20.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/686/#laws.1.65.0">1988 c 686 art 1 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.20.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/632/#laws.4.45.0">1994 c 632 art 4 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.2.37.0">1998 c 386 art 2 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/260/#laws.0.17.0">2000 c 260 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.23.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.22.0">2004 c 206 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.27.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/284/#laws.2.12.0">2004 c 284 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/135/#laws.2.11.0">2007 c 135 art 2 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.9661"><b>116J.9661</b> MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.187.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 187</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.9665"><b>116J.9665</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.40.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 40</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.967"><b>116J.967</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.1.117.0">1991 c 345 art 1 s 117</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.9671"><b>116J.9671</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/200/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 200 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.9672"><b>116J.9672</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/380/#laws.2.21.0">2002 c 380 art 2 s 21</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.9673"><b>116J.9673</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/380/#laws.2.21.0">2002 c 380 art 2 s 21</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.968"><b>116J.968</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.109.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 109</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.970"><b>116J.970</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/629/#laws.0.23.0">1988 c 629 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/322/#laws.0.20.0">1991 c 322 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.971"><b>116J.971</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/629/#laws.0.23.0">1988 c 629 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/322/#laws.0.20.0">1991 c 322 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.974"><b>116J.974</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.975"><b>116J.975</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.1.40.0">1997 c 7 art 1 s 40</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.976">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.976 STATE APPROVAL OF GOVERNMENT PROCUREMENT AGREEMENTS.</h1>
        <p class="in">Any decision of the state to enter into government procurement agreements relating to United States trade agreements must be approved by the governor and the legislature.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.976.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/300/#laws.0.3.0">2008 c 300 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.977"><b>116J.977</b> MS 2010 [Expired, 2008 c 300 s 4]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.978">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.978 MINNESOTA TRADE OFFICES IN FOREIGN MARKETS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of employment and economic development shall establish three new Minnesota Trade Offices in key foreign markets selected for their potential to increase Minnesota exports and attract foreign direct investment.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner shall establish a performance rating system for the new offices established under this section and create specific annual goals for the offices to meet. The commissioner shall monitor activities of the office, including, but not limited to, the number of inquiries and projects received and completed, meetings arranged between Minnesota companies and potential investors, distributors, or customers, and agreements signed.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.978.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.3.10.0">2013 c 85 art 3 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.9781">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.9781 INVEST MINNESOTA.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall establish the Invest Minnesota marketing initiative. This initiative must focus on branding the state's economic development initiatives and promoting Minnesota business opportunities. The initiative may include measures to communicate the benefits of doing business in Minnesota to companies considering relocating, establishing a United States presence, or expanding.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.9781.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.3.12.0">2013 c 85 art 3 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.979">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.979 MINNESOTA STEP GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.979.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of employment and economic development shall create a State Trade and Export Promotion grants program, hereafter STEP grants, to provide financial and technical assistance to eligible Minnesota small businesses with an active interest in exporting products or services to foreign markets.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.979.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>Recipients may apply, on an application devised by the commissioner, for up to $7,500 in reimbursement for approved export-development activities, including, but not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) participation in trade missions;</p>
          <p>(2) export training;</p>
          <p>(3) exhibition at trade shows or industry-specific events;</p>
          <p>(4) translation of marketing materials;</p>
          <p>(5) development of foreign language websites, Gold Key, or other business matchmaking services;</p>
          <p>(6) company-specific international sales activities; and</p>
          <p>(7) testing and certification required to sell products in foreign markets.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.979.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.3.11.0">2013 c 85 art 3 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.980">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.980 COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.980.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The Department of Employment and Economic Development shall:</p>
          <p>(1) be responsible for administering all state community development and assistance programs, including the economic recovery account, the rural development programs, the Minnesota Public Facilities Authority loan and grant programs, and the enterprise zone program;</p>
          <p>(2) be responsible for state administration of federally funded community development and assistance programs, including the small cities development grant program, the Minnesota cleanup revolving loan fund program created under the Brownfields Revitalization and Environmental Restoration Act of 2001, Public Law 107-118, title II, under the authority of the United States Environmental Protection Agency, and land and water conservation programs;</p>
          <p>(3) provide technical assistance to rural communities for community development in cooperation with regional development commissions;</p>
          <p>(4) coordinate the development and review of state rural development policies; and</p>
          <p>(5) be responsible for coordinating community assistance and development programs in cooperation with regional development commissions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.980.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">General complement authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The department may combine all related state and federal complement positions into general fund positions, to carry out the responsibilities under subdivision 1. The number of general fund positions must not exceed the aggregate number of all state and federal positions that are to be combined. Records of the actual number of employee hours charged to each state and federal account must be maintained for each general fund position.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.980.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Coordination required for housing related grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must coordinate with the commissioner of the Minnesota Housing Finance Agency to ensure that housing related grant applications for the Small Cities Community Development Block Grant Program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.401">116J.401</a> are consistent with the agency's most recent housing affordability plan and do not duplicate existing state housing programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.980.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/200/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 200 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.980.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.4.1.0">1987 c 386 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.106.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/429/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 429 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/157/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 157 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.21.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 21</a>,22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/369/#laws.0.9.0">1996 c 369 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.23.0">2004 c 206 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.981"><b>116J.981</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/369/#laws.0.13.0">1996 c 369 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.982">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.982 COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT CORPORATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the terms in this subdivision have the meanings given them:</p>
          <p>(a) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
          <p>(b) "Economic development region" means an area so designated in the governor's executive order number 83-15, dated March 15, 1983.</p>
          <p>(c) "Federal poverty level" means the income level published annually by the United States Department of Health and Human Services under authority of the Omnibus Budget Reconciliation Act of 1981, Public Law 97-35, title VI, section 673(2).</p>
          <p>(d) "Low income" means an annual income below the federal poverty level.</p>
          <p>(e) A "low-income area" means an area in which (1) ten percent of the population have low incomes, or (2) there is one or more recognized subareas such as a census tract, city, township, or county in which 15 percent of the population have low incomes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Administration.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall administer this section except for subdivision 6, which shall be administered by the commissioner of housing finance. The commissioners of employment and economic development and housing finance may, separately or jointly, adopt rules necessary to implement this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Certification; corporations eligible.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall certify a community development corporation under this section if the corporation is a nonprofit corporation incorporated under chapter 317A and meets the other criteria in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) The corporation, in its articles of incorporation or bylaws, must designate a low-income area as the specific geographic community within which it will operate. Within cities of the first class, a designated community must be an identifiable neighborhood or a combination of neighborhoods but may not be the entire city. Outside cities of the first class, a designated community may be an identifiable neighborhood or neighborhoods, or home rule charter or statutory cities, townships, unincorporated areas, or combinations of those entities, but may not be an entire economic development region nor cross existing economic development region boundaries except as provided in this section.</p>
          <p>(c) The corporation's major purpose, in its articles of incorporation or bylaws, must be economic development, redevelopment, or housing in its designated community.</p>
          <p>(d) The corporation must be tax exempt under section 501, paragraph (c), clause (3), of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended.</p>
          <p>(e) The membership and board of directors of the corporation must be representative of the designated community. At least 20 percent of the directors shall have low incomes or shall reside in low-income areas described in subdivision 1, paragraph (e), clause (1), or the low-income subarea described in subdivision 1, paragraph (e), clause (2). At least 60 percent of the directors must be residents of, or be employed in, the designated community. Other directors shall be business, financial, or civic leaders or representatives-at-large of the designated community. At least 40 percent of the directors must reside in the designated community. Notwithstanding the requirements of this paragraph, a corporation which meets board structure requirements for a Community Housing Development Corporation under Code of Federal Regulations, title 24, part 92.2, is deemed to meet the board membership requirements of this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(f) The corporation shall not discriminate against any persons on the basis of a status protected under chapter 363A.</p>
          <p>(g) The corporation shall demonstrate that it has or can obtain the technical skills to analyze projects, that it is familiar with available public and private funding sources and economic development, redevelopment, and housing programs, and that it is capable of packaging economic development, redevelopment, and housing projects.</p>
          <p>(h) The corporation must have completed two or more economic development, redevelopment, or housing projects within its designated community during the last three years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Approval for certification.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall certify as a community development corporation any organization which meets the criteria in subdivision 3. The certification is for two years from the date of certification and is renewable. The commissioner shall certify as a community development corporation for a nonrenewable period of three years from the date of certification an organization which meets all the criteria in subdivision 3, except for paragraphs (d) and (h), but which plans to meet those requirements by the end of the three years.</p>
          <p>(b) As part of the certification process, the commissioner shall resolve disputes concerning boundaries of the designated community of a community development corporation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Grants; economic development contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may make a grant to a community development corporation and enter into contracts with certified community development corporations for:</p>
          <p>(1) specific economic development projects within the designated community, such as development of a proposal for a venture grant, or for establishment of a business venture, including assistance to an existing business venture, purchase of partial or full ownership of a business venture, real estate development, strategic development planning, infrastructure development, or development of resources or facilities necessary for the establishment of a business venture;</p>
          <p>(2) dissemination of information about, or taking applications for, programs operated by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(3) developing the internal organizational capacity to engage in economic development activities such as the partnership activities listed in clause (1).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Housing contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of the housing finance agency may enter into contracts with certified community development corporations for purposes of housing activities associated with economic development activity under subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.35.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 35</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Other programs.</span></h2>
          <p>A certified community development corporation is eligible to participate in a program available to nonprofit organizations which is operated by the commissioners of employment and economic development or housing finance if the certified development corporation meets the requirements of the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.<span class="headnote">Real estate license exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>A certified community development corporation is exempt from the licensure requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/82.87">82.87</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.35.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 35</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.982.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.35.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 35</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
