      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.9922">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.9922 CENTRAL MINNESOTA OPPORTUNITY GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9922.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
          <p>(c) "Community initiative" means a nonprofit organization which provides services to central Minnesota communities of color in one or more of the program areas listed in subdivision 4, paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(d) "Foundation" means the Central Minnesota Community Foundation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9922.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish a central Minnesota opportunity grant program, administered by the foundation, to identify and support community initiatives in the St. Cloud area that enhance long-term economic self-sufficiency by improving education, housing, and economic outcomes for central Minnesota communities of color.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9922.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grant to the Central Minnesota Community Foundation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall award all grant funds to the foundation, which shall administer the central Minnesota opportunity grant program. The foundation may use up to five percent of grant funds for administrative costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9922.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Grants to community initiatives.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The foundation must award funds through a competitive grant process to community initiatives that will provide services, either alone or in partnership with another nonprofit organization, in one or more of the following areas:</p>
          <p>(1) economic development, including but not limited to programs to foster entrepreneurship or small business development;</p>
          <p>(2) education, including but not limited to programs to encourage civic engagement or provide youth after-school or recreation programs; or</p>
          <p>(3) housing, including but not limited to, programs to prevent and respond to homelessness or to provide access to loans or grants for housing stability and affordability.</p>
          <p>(b) To receive grant funds, a community initiative must submit a written application to the foundation, using a form developed by the foundation. This grant application must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of the activities that will be funded by the grant;</p>
          <p>(2) an estimate of the cost of each grant activity;</p>
          <p>(3) the total cost of the project;</p>
          <p>(4) the sources and amounts of nonstate funds supplementing the grant;</p>
          <p>(5) how the project aims to achieve stated outcomes in areas including improved job training; workforce development; small business support; early childhood, kindergarten through grade 12, and higher education achievement; and access to housing, including loans; and</p>
          <p>(6) any additional information requested by the foundation.</p>
          <p>(c) In awarding grants under this subdivision, the foundation shall give weight to applications from organizations that demonstrate:</p>
          <p>(1) a history of successful provision of the services listed in paragraph (a); and</p>
          <p>(2) a history of successful fundraising from private sources for such services.</p>
          <p>(d) In evaluating grant applications, the foundation shall not consider the composition of a community initiative's governing board.</p>
          <p>(e) Grant funds may be used by a community initiative for the following purposes:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) operating costs, including but not limited to staff, office space, computers, software, and web development and maintenance services;</p>
          <p>(2) program costs;</p>
          <p>(3) travel within Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(4) consultants directly related to and necessary for delivering services listed in paragraph (a); and</p>
          <p>(5) capacity building.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9922.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reports to the legislature.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15, 2019, and each January 15 thereafter through 2022, the commissioner must submit a report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the house of representatives and the senate committees with jurisdiction over economic development that details the use of grant funds. This report must include data on the number of individuals served and, to the extent practical, measures of progress toward achieving the outcomes stated in subdivision 4, paragraph (b), clause (5).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.9922.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.6.8.0">2017 c 94 art 6 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.9923">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.9923 TELECOMMUTER FORWARD! CERTIFICATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9923.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>In this section, "political subdivision" means a city, township, or county.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9923.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Certification.</span></h2>
          <p>A political subdivision may apply to the commissioner of employment and economic development for certification as a Telecommuter Forward! Community. The commissioner of employment and economic development shall prescribe the form and manner for making an application. Before approving an application, the commissioner shall consider the application and the information in subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9923.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Resolution.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the application in subdivision 2, a political subdivision must adopt a resolution that does both of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) states the political subdivision's support and commitment to promote the availability of telecommuting options; and</p>
          <p>(2) provides for a single point of contact for coordinating telecommuting opportunities that has all of the following responsibilities:</p>
          <p>(i) coordination and partnership with broadband providers, realtors, economic development professionals, employers, employees, and other telecommuting stakeholders;</p>
          <p>(ii) collaboration with broadband providers and employers to identify, develop, and market telecommuter-capable broadband packages;</p>
          <p>(iii) communication and partnership with broadband providers and economic development professionals to develop common goals;</p>
          <p>(iv) promotion of telecommuter-friendly work spaces, such as business incubators with telecommuting spaces, if such a work space has been established in the political subdivision at the time the political subdivision adopts the resolution;</p>
          <p>(v) familiarity with broadband mapping tools and other state-level resources;</p>
          <p>(vi) maintaining regular communication with the state broadband office; and</p>
          <p>(vii) making regular reports to the governing body of the political subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.9923.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/13/#laws.0.1.0">2019 c 13 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.9924">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.9924 TARGETED COMMUNITY CAPITAL PROJECT GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9924.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Capital project" or "project" means the acquisition or betterment of buildings or other fixed assets and other improvements of a capital nature.</p>
          <p>(c) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
          <p>(d) "Economically disadvantaged persons or groups" means one or more persons or groups that:</p>
          <p>(1) qualify as a low-income person as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.14#stat.116M.14.4a">116M.14, subdivision 4a</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) live in a low-income area as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.14#stat.116M.14.4">116M.14, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) "Government entity" means a city, township, county, or any political subdivision, or an American Indian Tribal government entity located within a federally recognized American Indian reservation.</p>
          <p>(f) "Nonprofit organization" means a not-for-profit corporation under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code or a Tribal nonprofit under section 7871 of the Internal Revenue Code that serves underserved communities or economically disadvantaged persons or groups.</p>
          <p>(g) "Underserved community" means one or more persons or groups that qualify as:</p>
          <p>(1) a minority person as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.14#stat.116M.14.6">116M.14, subdivision 6</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) persons with disabilities as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.14#stat.116M.14.9">116M.14, subdivision 9</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9924.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall make competitive grants for capital projects to nonprofit organizations and government entities that provide, increase, or expand services to underserved communities or economically disadvantaged persons or groups.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall give priority to applicants under subdivision 3 that:</p>
          <p>(1) do not have a history of receiving capital grants from the state;</p>
          <p>(2) demonstrate local support for the project;</p>
          <p>(3) address needs for an underserved community, an economically disadvantaged area, or people or groups who are economically disadvantaged;</p>
          <p>(4) provide community benefits; or</p>
          <p>(5) have previously received phased grant funds as described under subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(c) In selecting projects for grants, the commissioner must equitably divide the total appropriation between the metropolitan areas and greater Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9924.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>A prospective grantee under this section must submit a written application to the commissioner in the form, at the time, and in the manner prescribed by the commissioner. The written application must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of the capital project to be funded by the grant;</p>
          <p>(2) the rationale for the project, including a description of the services provided and populations served by the applicant;</p>
          <p>(3) the total cost of the project and the cost of individual phases of the project, including but not limited to predesign, design, construction, engineering, furnishing, and equipping;</p>
          <p>(4) the requested grant amount;</p>
          <p>(5) the property owner of the facility to be improved;</p>
          <p>(6) the sources and amounts of state and nonstate funds previously received and committed to the project;</p>
          <p>(7) the public purpose achieved by the project;</p>
          <p>(8) an estimated timeline of the project; and</p>
          <p>(9) any additional information requested by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9924.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Grant amount; project phasing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall award grants in an amount not to exceed $1,500,000 per grant.</p>
          <p>(b) A grant awarded under this section must be no less than the amount required to complete one or more phases of the project, less any nonstate funds already committed for such activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9924.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Match.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may not award a grant for which the applicant does not provide nonstate funds for the project unless the applicant:</p>
          <p>(1) is located in an area with a very low net tax capacity;</p>
          <p>(2) the applicant is experiencing hardship; or</p>
          <p>(3) the applicant serves underserved communities or economically disadvantaged persons or groups.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of this section, "area with a very low net tax capacity" means a city with a net tax capacity per capita that is less than the median net tax capacity per capita among all cities statewide.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9924.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Applicability of other laws.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of chapter 16A that apply to general fund appropriations for capital projects also apply to grants under this section. Money granted under this section is available until the project is completed or abandoned subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.642">16A.642</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9924.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Appropriation; administration and monitoring.</span></h2>
          <p>Up to five percent of any appropriation for the program under this section is for administration and monitoring of the program. The commissioner must also use the funds under this subdivision to provide technical assistance, education, and support for program applicants, as needed, and may contract with a third-party to provide such services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9924.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Report to the legislature.</span></h2>
          <p>On or before January 31, 2022, and every January 31 thereafter, the commissioner must submit a report as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.195">3.195</a> that details the grants awarded under this section, including the total grants distributed, the recipients of the grants, the services supported by the grants, and any other information the commissioner deems pertinent. A copy of this report must also be sent to the chairs and ranking minority members of the committees of the house of representatives and the senate having jurisdiction over capital investment and economic development.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.9924.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.11.10.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 11 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.9926">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.9926 EMERGING DEVELOPER FUND PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9926.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
          <p>(c) "Disadvantaged community" means a community where the median household income is less than 80 percent of the area median income.</p>
          <p>(d) "Eligible project" means a project that is based in Minnesota and meets one or more of the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) it will stimulate community stabilization or revitalization;</p>
          <p>(2) it will be located within a census tract identified as a disadvantaged community or low-income community;</p>
          <p>(3) it will directly benefit residents of a low-income household;</p>
          <p>(4) it will increase the supply and improve the condition of affordable housing and homeownership;</p>
          <p>(5) it will support the growth needs of new and existing community-based enterprises that promote economic stability or improve the supply or quality of job opportunities; or</p>
          <p>(6) it will promote wealth creation, including by being a project in a neighborhood traditionally not served by real estate developers.</p>
          <p>(e) "Emerging developer" means a developer who:</p>
          <p>(1) has limited access to loans from traditional financial institutions; or</p>
          <p>(2) is a new or smaller developer who has engaged in educational training in real estate development; and</p>
          <p>(3) is either a:</p>
          <p>(i) minority as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.14#stat.116M.14.6">116M.14, subdivision 6</a>;</p>
          <p>(ii) woman;</p>
          <p>(iii) person with a disability, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116M.14#stat.116M.14.9">116M.14, subdivision 9</a>; or</p>
          <p>(iv) low-income person.</p>
          <p>(f) "Low-income person" means a person who:</p>
          <p>(1) has a household income at or below 200 percent of the federal poverty level; or</p>
          <p>(2) has a family income that does not exceed 60 percent of the area median income as determined by the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development.</p>
          <p>(g) "Partner organization" means a community development financial institution or a similarly qualified nonprofit corporation, as determined by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(h) "Program" means the emerging developer fund program created under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9926.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish an emerging developer fund program to make grants to partner organizations to make grants and loans to emerging developers for eligible projects to transform neighborhoods statewide and promote economic development and the creation and retention of jobs in Minnesota. The program must also reduce racial and socioeconomic disparities by growing the financial capacity of emerging developers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9926.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grants to partner organizations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall design a competitive process to award grants to partner organizations to make grants and loans to emerging developers under subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(b) A partner organization may use up to ten percent of grant funds for the administrative costs of the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9926.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Grants and loans to emerging developers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Through the program, partner organizations shall offer emerging developers predevelopment grants and predevelopment, construction, and bridge loans for eligible projects according to a plan submitted to and approved by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) Predevelopment grants must be for no more than $100,000. All loans must be for no more than $1,000,000.</p>
          <p>(c) Loans must be for a term set by the partner organization and approved by the commissioner of no less than six months and no more than eight years, depending on the use of loan proceeds.</p>
          <p>(d) Loans must be for zero interest or an interest rate of no more than the Wall Street Journal prime rate, as determined by the partner organization and approved by the commissioner based on the individual project risk and type of loan sought.</p>
          <p>(e) Loans must have flexible collateral requirements compared to traditional loans, but may require a personal guaranty from the emerging developer and may be largely unsecured when the appraised value of the real estate is low.</p>
          <p>(f) Loans must have no prepayment penalties and are expected to be repaid from permanent financing or a conventional loan, once that is secured.</p>
          <p>(g) Loans must have the ability to bridge many types of receivables, such as tax credits, grants, developer fees, and other forms of long-term financing.</p>
          <p>(h) At the partner organization's request and the commissioner's discretion, an emerging developer may be required to work with an experienced developer or professional services consultant who can offer expertise and advice throughout the development of the project.</p>
          <p>(i) All loan repayments must be paid into the emerging developer fund account created in this section to fund additional loans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9926.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Eligible expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The following are eligible expenses for a predevelopment grant or loan under the program:</p>
          <p>(1) earnest money or purchase deposit;</p>
          <p>(2) building inspection fees and environmental reviews;</p>
          <p>(3) appraisal and surveying;</p>
          <p>(4) design and tax credit application fees;</p>
          <p>(5) title and recording fees;</p>
          <p>(6) site preparation, demolition, and stabilization;</p>
          <p>(7) interim maintenance and project overhead;</p>
          <p>(8) property taxes and insurance;</p>
          <p>(9) construction bonds or letters of credit;</p>
          <p>(10) market and feasibility studies; and</p>
          <p>(11) professional fees.</p>
          <p>(b) The following are eligible expenses for a construction or bridge loan under the program:</p>
          <p>(1) land or building acquisition;</p>
          <p>(2) construction-related expenses;</p>
          <p>(3) developer and contractor fees;</p>
          <p>(4) site preparation, environmental cleanup, and demolition;</p>
          <p>(5) financing fees, including title and recording;</p>
          <p>(6) professional fees;</p>
          <p>(7) carrying costs;</p>
          <p>(8) construction period interest;</p>
          <p>(9) project reserves; and</p>
          <p>(10) leasehold improvements and equipment purchase.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9926.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Emerging developer fund account.</span></h2>
          <p>An emerging developer fund account is created in the special revenue fund in the state treasury. Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner for grants to partner organizations to make loans under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.9926.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reports to the legislature.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By January 15 of each year, beginning in 2025, each partner organization shall submit a report to the commissioner on the use of program funds and program outcomes.</p>
          <p>(b) By March 15 of each year, beginning in 2025, the commissioner shall submit a report to the chairs of the house of representatives and senate committees with jurisdiction over economic development on the use of program funds and program outcomes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.9926.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.15.16.0">2023 c 53 art 15 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">BUSINESS SUBSIDIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.993">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.993 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.993.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.993">116J.993</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.995">116J.995</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.993.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Benefit date.</span></h2>
          <p>"Benefit date" means the date that the recipient receives the business subsidy. If the business subsidy involves the purchase, lease, or donation of physical equipment, then the benefit date begins when the recipient puts the equipment into service. If the business subsidy is for improvements to property, then the benefit date refers to the earliest date of either:</p>
          <p>(1) when the improvements are finished for the entire project; or</p>
          <p>(2) when a business occupies the property. If a business occupies the property and the subsidy grantor expects that other businesses will also occupy the same property, the grantor may assign a separate benefit date for each business when it first occupies the property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.993.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Business subsidy.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Business subsidy" or "subsidy" means a state or local government agency grant, contribution of personal property, real property, infrastructure, the principal amount of a loan at rates below those commercially available to the recipient, any reduction or deferral of any tax or any fee, any guarantee of any payment under any loan, lease, or other obligation, or any preferential use of government facilities given to a business.</p>
          <p class="in">The following forms of financial assistance are not a business subsidy:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a business subsidy of less than $150,000;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) assistance that is generally available to all businesses or to a general class of similar businesses, such as a line of business, size, location, or similar general criteria;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) public improvements to buildings or lands owned by the state or local government that serve a public purpose and do not principally benefit a single business or defined group of businesses at the time the improvements are made;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) redevelopment property polluted by contaminants as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.552#stat.116J.552.3">116J.552, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) assistance provided for the sole purpose of renovating old or decaying building stock or bringing it up to code and assistance provided for designated historic preservation districts, provided that the assistance is equal to or less than 50 percent of the total cost;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) assistance to provide job readiness and training services if the sole purpose of the assistance is to provide those services;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) assistance for housing;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) assistance for pollution control or abatement, including assistance for a tax increment financing hazardous substance subdistrict as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.174#stat.469.174.23">469.174, subdivision 23</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) assistance for energy conservation;</p>
          <p class="in">(10) tax reductions resulting from conformity with federal tax law;</p>
          <p class="in">(11) workers' compensation and unemployment insurance;</p>
          <p class="in">(12) benefits derived from regulation;</p>
          <p class="in">(13) indirect benefits derived from assistance to educational institutions;</p>
          <p class="in">(14) funds from bonds allocated under chapter 474A, bonds issued to refund outstanding bonds, and bonds issued for the benefit of an organization described in section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended through December 31, 1999;</p>
          <p class="in">(15) assistance for a collaboration between a Minnesota higher education institution and a business;</p>
          <p class="in">(16) assistance for a tax increment financing soils condition district as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.174#stat.469.174.19">469.174, subdivision 19</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(17) redevelopment when the recipient's investment in the purchase of the site and in site preparation is 70 percent or more of the assessor's current year's estimated market value;</p>
          <p class="in">(18) general changes in tax increment financing law and other general tax law changes of a principally technical nature;</p>
          <p class="in">(19) federal assistance until the assistance has been repaid to, and reinvested by, the state or local government agency;</p>
          <p class="in">(20) funds from dock and wharf bonds issued by a seaway port authority;</p>
          <p class="in">(21) business loans and loan guarantees of $150,000 or less;</p>
          <p class="in">(22) federal loan funds provided through the United States Department of Commerce, Economic Development Administration; and</p>
          <p class="in">(23) property tax abatements granted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.1813">469.1813</a> to property that is subject to valuation under Minnesota Rules, chapter 8100.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.993.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Grantor.</span></h2>
          <p>"Grantor" means any state or local government agency with the authority to grant a business subsidy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.993.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Local government agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local government agency" includes a statutory or home rule charter city, housing and redevelopment authority, town, county, port authority, economic development authority, community development agency, nonprofit entity created by a local government agency, or any other entity created by or authorized by a local government with authority to provide business subsidies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.993.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Recipient.</span></h2>
          <p>"Recipient" means any for-profit or nonprofit business entity that receives a business subsidy. Only nonprofit entities with at least 100 full-time equivalent positions and with a ratio of highest to lowest paid employee, that exceeds ten to one, determined on the basis of full-time equivalent positions, are included in this definition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.993.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Residence.</span></h2>
          <p>"Residence" means the place where an individual has established a permanent home from which the individual has no present intention of moving.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.993.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">State government agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"State government agency" means any state agency that has the authority to award business subsidies.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.993.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/243/#laws.12.1.0">1999 c 243 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/482/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 482 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.7.1.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 7 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/259/#laws.4.1.0">2006 c 259 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.5.2.0">2008 c 366 art 5 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.994">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.994 REGULATING LOCAL AND STATE BUSINESS SUBSIDIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Public purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>A business subsidy must meet a public purpose which may include, but may not be limited to, increasing the tax base. Job retention may only be used as a public purpose in cases where job loss is specific and demonstrable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Developing a set of criteria.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A business subsidy may not be granted until the grantor has adopted criteria after a public hearing for awarding business subsidies that comply with this section. The criteria may not be adopted on a case-by-case basis. The criteria must set specific minimum requirements that recipients must meet in order to be eligible to receive business subsidies. The criteria must include a specific wage floor for the wages to be paid for the jobs created. The wage floor may be stated as a specific dollar amount or may be stated as a formula that will generate a specific dollar amount. A grantor may deviate from its criteria by documenting in writing the reason for the deviation and attaching a copy of the document to its next annual report to the department. The commissioner of employment and economic development may assist local government agencies in developing criteria. A copy of the criteria must be submitted to the Department of Employment and Economic Development along with the first annual report following May 15, 2000, or with the first annual report after it has adopted criteria, whichever is earlier. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.993#stat.116J.993.3">116J.993, subdivision 3</a>, clauses (1) and (21), for the purpose of this subdivision, "business subsidies" as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.993">116J.993</a> includes the following forms of financial assistance:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a business subsidy of $25,000 or more; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) business loans and guarantees of $75,000 or more.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Subsidy agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A recipient must enter into a subsidy agreement with the grantor of the subsidy that includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of the subsidy, including the amount and type of subsidy, and type of district if the subsidy is tax increment financing;</p>
          <p>(2) a statement of the public purposes for the subsidy;</p>
          <p>(3) measurable, specific, and tangible goals for the subsidy;</p>
          <p>(4) a description of the financial obligation of the recipient if the goals are not met;</p>
          <p>(5) a statement of why the subsidy is needed;</p>
          <p>(6) a commitment to continue operations in the jurisdiction where the subsidy is used for at least five years after the benefit date;</p>
          <p>(7) the name and address of the parent corporation of the recipient, if any; and</p>
          <p>(8) a list of all financial assistance by all grantors for the project.</p>
          <p>(b) Business subsidies in the form of grants must be structured as forgivable loans and are subject to the financial review under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.981">16B.981</a>. For other types of business subsidies, the agreement must state the fair market value of the subsidy to the recipient, including the value of conveying property at less than a fair market price, or other in-kind benefits to the recipient.</p>
          <p>(c) If a business subsidy benefits more than one recipient, the grantor must assign a proportion of the business subsidy to each recipient that signs a subsidy agreement. The proportion assessed to each recipient must reflect a reasonable estimate of the recipient's share of the total benefits of the project.</p>
          <p>(d) The state or local government agency and the recipient must both sign the subsidy agreement and, if the grantor is a local government agency, the agreement must be approved by the local elected governing body, except for the St. Paul Port Authority and a seaway port authority.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding the provision in paragraph (a), clause (6), a recipient may be authorized to move from the jurisdiction where the subsidy is used within the five-year period after the benefit date if, after a public hearing, the grantor approves the recipient's request to move. For the purpose of this paragraph, if the grantor is a state government agency other than the Department of Iron Range Resources and Rehabilitation, "jurisdiction" means a city or township.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Wage and job goals.</span></h2>
          <p>The subsidy agreement, in addition to any other goals, must include: (1) goals for the number of jobs created, which may include separate goals for the number of part-time or full-time jobs, or, in cases where job loss is specific and demonstrable, goals for the number of jobs retained; (2) wage goals for any jobs created or retained; and (3) wage goals for any jobs to be enhanced through increased wages. After a public hearing, if the creation or retention of jobs is determined not to be a goal, the wage and job goals may be set at zero. The goals for the number of jobs to be created or retained must result in job creation or retention by the recipient within the granting jurisdiction overall.</p>
          <p>In addition to other specific goal time frames, the wage and job goals must contain specific goals to be attained within two years of the benefit date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Public notice and hearing.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Before granting a business subsidy that exceeds $500,000 for a state government grantor and $150,000 for a local government grantor, the grantor must provide public notice and a hearing on the subsidy. A public hearing and notice under this subdivision is not required if a hearing and notice on the subsidy is otherwise required by law.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Public notice of a proposed business subsidy under this subdivision by a state government grantor, other than the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation, must be published in the State Register. Public notice of a proposed business subsidy under this subdivision by a local government grantor or the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation must be published in a local newspaper of general circulation. The public notice must identify the location at which information about the business subsidy, including a summary of the terms of the subsidy, is available. Published notice should be sufficiently conspicuous in size and placement to distinguish the notice from the surrounding text. The grantor must make the information available in printed paper copies and, if possible, on the Internet. The government agency must provide at least a ten-day notice for the public hearing.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The public notice must include the date, time, and place of the hearing.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The public hearing by a state government grantor other than the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation must be held in St. Paul.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) If more than one nonstate grantor provides a business subsidy to the same recipient, the nonstate grantors may designate one nonstate grantor to hold a single public hearing regarding the business subsidies provided by all nonstate grantors. For the purposes of this paragraph, "nonstate grantor" includes the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The public notice of any public meeting about a business subsidy agreement, including those required by this subdivision and by subdivision 4, must include notice that a person with residence in or the owner of taxable property in the granting jurisdiction may file a written complaint with the grantor if the grantor fails to comply with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.993">116J.993</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.995">116J.995</a>, and that no action may be filed against the grantor for the failure to comply unless a written complaint is filed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Failure to meet goals.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The subsidy agreement must specify the recipient's obligation if the recipient does not fulfill the agreement. At a minimum, the agreement must require a recipient failing to meet subsidy agreement goals to pay back the assistance plus interest to the grantor or, at the grantor's option, to the account created under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.551">116J.551</a> provided that repayment may be prorated to reflect partial fulfillment of goals. The interest rate must be set at no less than the implicit price deflator for government consumption expenditures and gross investment for state and local governments prepared by the Bureau of Economic Analysis of the United States Department of Commerce for the 12-month period ending March 31 of the previous year. The grantor, after a public hearing, may extend for up to one year the period for meeting the wage and job goals under subdivision 4 provided in a subsidy agreement. A grantor may extend the period for meeting other goals under subdivision 3, paragraph (a), clause (3), by documenting in writing the reason for the extension and attaching a copy of the document to its next annual report to the department.</p>
          <p>(b) A recipient that fails to meet the terms of a subsidy agreement may not receive a business subsidy from any grantor for a period of five years from the date of failure or until a recipient satisfies its repayment obligation under this subdivision, whichever occurs first.</p>
          <p>(c) Before a grantor signs a business subsidy agreement, the grantor must check with the compilation and summary report required by this section to determine if the recipient is eligible to receive a business subsidy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reports by recipients to grantors.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A business subsidy grantor must monitor the progress by the recipient in achieving agreement goals.</p>
          <p>(b) A recipient must provide information regarding goals and results for two years after the benefit date or until the goals are met, whichever is later. If the goals are not met, the recipient must continue to provide information on the subsidy until the subsidy is repaid. The information must be filed on forms developed by the commissioner in cooperation with representatives of local government. Copies of the completed forms must be sent to the local government agency that provided the subsidy or to the commissioner if the grantor is a state agency. If the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation is the grantor, the copies must be sent to the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation. The report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the type, public purpose, and amount of subsidies and type of district, if the subsidy is tax increment financing;</p>
          <p>(2) the hourly wage of each job created with separate bands of wages;</p>
          <p>(3) the sum of the hourly wages and cost of health insurance provided by the employer with separate bands of wages;</p>
          <p>(4) the date the job and wage goals will be reached;</p>
          <p>(5) a statement of goals identified in the subsidy agreement and an update on achievement of those goals;</p>
          <p>(6) the location of the recipient prior to receiving the business subsidy;</p>
          <p>(7) the number of employees who ceased to be employed by the recipient when the recipient relocated to become eligible for the business subsidy;</p>
          <p>(8) why the recipient did not complete the project outlined in the subsidy agreement at their previous location, if the recipient was previously located at another site in Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(9) the name and address of the parent corporation of the recipient, if any;</p>
          <p>(10) a list of all financial assistance by all grantors for the project; and</p>
          <p>(11) other information the commissioner may request.</p>
          <p class="bl">A report must be filed no later than March 1 of each year for the previous year. The local agency and the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation must forward copies of the reports received by recipients to the commissioner by April 1.</p>
          <p>(c) Financial assistance that is excluded from the definition of "business subsidy" by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.993#stat.116J.993.3">116J.993, subdivision 3</a>, clauses (4), (5), (8), and (16), is subject to the reporting requirements of this subdivision, except that the report of the recipient must include instead:</p>
          <p>(1) the type, public purpose, and amount of the financial assistance, and type of district if the assistance is tax increment financing;</p>
          <p>(2) progress towards meeting goals stated in the assistance agreement and the public purpose of the assistance;</p>
          <p>(3) if the agreement includes job creation, the hourly wage of each job created with separate bands of wages;</p>
          <p>(4) if the agreement includes job creation, the sum of the hourly wages and cost of health insurance provided by the employer with separate bands of wages;</p>
          <p>(5) the location of the recipient prior to receiving the assistance; and</p>
          <p>(6) other information the grantor requests.</p>
          <p>(d) If the recipient does not submit its report, the local government agency must mail the recipient a warning within one week of the required filing date. If, after 14 days of the postmarked date of the warning, the recipient fails to provide a report, the recipient must pay to the grantor a penalty of $100 for each subsequent day until the report is filed. The maximum penalty shall not exceed $1,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Reports by grantors.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Local government agencies of a local government with a population of more than 2,500 and state government agencies, regardless of whether or not they have awarded any business subsidies, must file a report by April 1 of each year with the commissioner. Local government agencies of a local government with a population of 2,500 or less are exempt from filing this report if they have not awarded a business subsidy in the past five years. The report must include a list of recipients that did not complete the recipient report required under subdivision 7 and a list of recipients that have not met their job and wage goals within two years and the steps being taken to bring them into compliance or to recoup the subsidy.</p>
          <p class="in">If the commissioner has not received the report by April 1 from an entity required to report, the commissioner shall issue a warning to the government agency. If the commissioner has still not received the report by June 1 of that same year from an entity required to report, then that government agency may not award any business subsidies until the report has been filed.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The report required under paragraph (a) is also required for financial assistance of $25,000 and greater that is excluded from the definition of "business subsidy" by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.993#stat.116J.993.3">116J.993, subdivision 3</a>, clause (1), and of $75,000 and greater that is excluded from the definition of "business subsidy" by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.993#stat.116J.993.3">116J.993, subdivision 3</a>, clause (21). The report for the financial assistance under this paragraph must be completed within one year of the granting of the financial assistance. The report required for financial assistance under this paragraph must include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the name of the recipient, its organizational structure, its address and contact information, and its industry sector;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a description of the amount and use of the financial assistance and the total project budget, including a list of all financial assistance by all grantors for the project and the private sources of financial assistance;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the public purpose of the financial assistance, the job goals associated with both the financial assistance and the total project in which the financial assistance is included, the hourly wage of each job created, and the cost of health insurance provided by the employer;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the date the project will be completed;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the name and address of the parent corporation of the recipient, if any; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) any other information the commissioner may request.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Within one year of completing a report under paragraph (b), the local government agency must report to the commissioner on progress in achieving the purposes and goals under paragraph (b), clause (3).</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner of employment and economic development must provide information on reporting requirements to state and local government agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Compilation and summary report.</span></h2>
          <p>The Department of Employment and Economic Development must publish a compilation and summary of the results of the reports for the previous two calendar years by December 1 of 2004 and every other year thereafter. The reports of the government agencies to the department and the compilation and summary report of the department must be made available to the public. The commissioner must make copies of all business subsidy reports submitted by local and state granting agencies available on the department's website by October 1 of the year in which they were submitted.</p>
          <p>The commissioner must coordinate the production of reports so that useful comparisons across time periods and across grantors can be made. The commissioner may add other information to the report as the commissioner deems necessary to evaluate business subsidies. Among the information in the summary and compilation report, the commissioner must include:</p>
          <p>(1) total amount of subsidies awarded in each development region of the state;</p>
          <p>(2) distribution of business subsidy amounts by size of the business subsidy;</p>
          <p>(3) distribution of business subsidy amounts by time category;</p>
          <p>(4) distribution of subsidies by type and by public purpose;</p>
          <p>(5) percent of all business subsidies that reached their goals;</p>
          <p>(6) percent of business subsidies that did not reach their goals by two years from the benefit date;</p>
          <p>(7) total dollar amount of business subsidies that did not meet their goals after two years from the benefit date;</p>
          <p>(8) percent of subsidies that did not meet their goals and that did not receive repayment;</p>
          <p>(9) list of recipients that have failed to meet the terms of a subsidy agreement in the past five years and have not satisfied their repayment obligations;</p>
          <p>(10) number of part-time and full-time jobs within separate bands of wages for the entire state and for each development region of the state;</p>
          <p>(11) benefits paid within separate bands of wages for the entire state and for each development region of the state; and</p>
          <p>(12) number of employees in the entire state and in each development region of the state who ceased to be employed because their employers relocated to become eligible for a business subsidy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Compilation.</span></h2>
          <p>The Department of Employment and Economic Development must publish a compilation of granting agencies' criteria policies adopted in the previous two calendar years by December 1 of 2004 and every other year thereafter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.994.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person with residence in or an owner of taxable property located in the jurisdiction of the grantor may bring an action for equitable relief arising out of the failure of the grantor to comply with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.993">116J.993</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.995">116J.995</a>. The court may award a prevailing party in an action under this subdivision costs and reasonable attorney fees.</p>
          <p>(b) Prior to bringing an action, the party must file a written complaint with the grantor stating the alleged violation and proposing a remedy. The grantor has up to 30 days to reply to the complaint in writing and may take action to comply with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.993">116J.993</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.995">116J.995</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The written complaint under this subdivision for failure to comply with subdivisions 1 to 5, must be filed with the grantor within 180 days after approval of the subsidy agreement under subdivision 3, paragraph (d). An action under this subdivision must be commenced within 30 days following receipt of the grantor's reply, or within 180 days after approval of the subsidy agreement under subdivision 3, paragraph (d), whichever is later.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.994.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/243/#laws.12.2.0">1999 c 243 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/482/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 482 s 2</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.28.0">2001 c 7 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.24.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 24</a>-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.24.0">2004 c 206 s 24</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.23.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 23</a>,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.7.2.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 7 s 2</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.5.3.0">2008 c 366 art 5 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.7.11.0">2017 c 94 art 7 s 11</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.7.13.0">2023 c 62 art 7 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.995">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.995 ECONOMIC GRANTS.</h1>
        <p>An appropriation rider in an appropriation to the Department of Employment and Economic Development that specifies that the appropriation be granted to a particular business or class of businesses must contain a statement of the expected benefits associated with the grant. At a minimum, the statement must include goals for the number of jobs created or enhanced, wages paid, and the tax revenue increases due to the grant. The wage and job goals must contain specific goals to be attained within two years of the benefit date. The statement must specify the recipient's obligation if the recipient does not attain the goals. At a minimum, the statement must require a recipient failing to meet the job and wage goals to pay back the assistance plus interest to the Department of Employment and Economic Development provided that repayment may be prorated to reflect partial fulfillment of goals. The interest rate must be set at no less than the implicit price deflator as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.994#stat.116J.994.6">116J.994, subdivision 6</a>. The legislature, after a public hearing, may extend for up to one year the period for meeting the goals provided in the statement.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.995.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/243/#laws.12.3.0">1999 c 243 art 12 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/482/#laws.0.12.0">2000 c 482 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.29.0">2001 c 7 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.13.27.0">2003 c 128 art 13 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116J.996">
        <h1 class="shn">116J.996 MILITARY RESERVIST ECONOMIC INJURY AND VETERAN-OWNED SMALL BUSINESS LOANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.996.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Active service" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/190.05">190.05</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of employment and economic development.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) "Eligible business" means a small business, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/645.445">645.445</a>, that was operating in Minnesota on the date a military reservist received orders for active service.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) "Essential employee" means a military reservist who is an owner or employee of an eligible business and whose managerial or technical expertise is critical to the day-to-day operation of the eligible business.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) "Military reservist" means a member of the reserve component of the armed forces.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) "Reserve component of the armed forces" has the meaning given it in United States Code, title 10, section 101(c).</p>
          <p class="in">(h) "Substantial economic injury" means an economic harm to an eligible business that results in the inability of the eligible business to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) meet its obligations as they mature;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) pay its ordinary and necessary operating expenses; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) manufacture, produce, market, or provide a product or service ordinarily manufactured, produced, marketed, or provided by the eligible business.</p>
          <p>(i) "Veteran-owned small business" means a small business, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/645.445">645.445</a>, that is majority-owned and operated by a recently separated veteran.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.996.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Loan program.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may make onetime, interest-free loans of up to $20,000 per borrower to:</p>
          <p>(1) eligible businesses that have sustained or are likely to sustain substantial economic injury as a result of the call to active service for 180 days or more of an essential employee; or</p>
          <p>(2) recently separated veterans who are veterans as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/197.447">197.447</a>, and have served in active military service, at any time on or after September 11, 2001, to start a veteran-owned small business.</p>
          <p class="bl">Loans for economic injury must be made for the purpose of preventing, remedying, or ameliorating the substantial economic injury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.996.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Revolving loan account.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall use money appropriated for the purpose to establish a revolving loan account. All repayments of loans made under this section must be deposited into this account. Interest earned on money in the account accrues to the account. Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner for purposes of the loan program created in this section, including costs incurred by the commissioner to establish and administer the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116J.996.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Using the expedited rulemaking procedures of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.389">14.389</a>, the commissioner shall develop and publish expedited rules for loan applications, use of funds, needed collateral, terms of loans, and other details of military reservist economic injury and veteran-owned small business loans.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116J.996.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/300/#laws.0.5.0">2008 c 300 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/363/#laws.10.10.0">2008 c 363 art 10 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/347/#laws.1.6.0">2010 c 347 art 1 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116J.997"><b>116J.997</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.3.23.0">2014 c 312 art 3 s 23</a>]</div>
    </div>
